Client Name 
Hamad Medical Corporation 
Project Name 
RLGH, MHS, See & Treat, AAH 
Department Name 
Housekeeping 
Job Location 
Patient isolation Areas/High risk areas 
Task Description 
Donning & Doffing of PPE’s 
Related RA/ JSA Ref. 
HSEQ-RA-HMC-10 
 
1. Training & induction Requirements 
Use correct PPE for the job; Describe donning & doffing of PPE’s and importance of infection control in an 
isolation area. 
2. Required Personal Protective Equipment (PPE) 
 
Disposable Gloves 
 
 
Shoe Covers Disposable - 
Optional Depends On The 
Risk Area. 
 
Disposable Mask 
 
Disposable Hair Net - 
Optional Depends On The 
Risk Area. 
 
N95 Face Mask – Optional 
Depends On The Risk Area 
 
Face Shield – Optional 
Depends On The Risk 
Area 
 
Medical Disposable Gown - 
Optional Depends On The 
Risk Area.

## Page 2

METHOD STATEMENT 
MS-CLE-HMC-14 
Donning & Doffing of PPE’s 
DATE 
5/15/2024

Template version: 2.0 dated 01-Feb-2018 
 
Page 2 of 4 
 
3. Required Materials 
 
Foam Hand Wash 
 
 
Sanitizer 
 
 
4. Pre-Job Check 
4.1 Wash hands as per the approved method prior to commencing task. 
4.2 Check hands for any cuts and abrasions, if yes cover with waterproof dressing (Refer to HMC policies 
CL 7241 Hand Hygiene Policy). 
4.3 Wear appropriate PPE as per guidelines (Refer to HMC policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices) 
4.4 Ensure the correct size of the chosen PPE’s. 
4.5 Ensure you have been trained in RA, MS, SDS, COSHH and chemical handling. 
 
5. Steps 
 
5.1 Donning 
 
5.1.1 
Identify and gather the proper PPE to don. 
5.1.2 
Put on isolation gown. Tie all the ties on the gown. Assistance may 
be needed by co-worker. 
5.1.3 
Put on disposable hairnet, wearing it this way pulls the meshes closer together. 
Locate across forehead and pull the hairnet over the crown. Ensuring all hair in 
the nape is covered. Locate under the ears to keep the hairnet securely in 
place. 
 
5.1.4 
Put on disposable shoe covers. 
 
5.1.5 
Put on N95 face mask/respirator/ disposable face mask. If the 
respirator has a nosepiece, it should be fitted to the nose with 
both hands, not bent or tented. Do not pinch the nosepiece with 
one hand. Respirator/facemask should be extended under chin. 
Both your mouth and nose should be protected. 
Do not wear respirator/facemask under your chin 
or store in scrubs pocket between patients.

## Page 3

METHOD STATEMENT 
MS-CLE-HMC-14 
Donning & Doffing of PPE’s 
DATE 
5/15/2024 
REVISION 
V2.0 
 
Template version: 2.0 dated 01-Feb-2018 
 
Page 3 of 4 
 
5.1.6 
Put on gloves. Gloves should cover the cuff (wrist) of gown. 
 
 
 
 
5.2 Doffing 
 
5.1.1 
Remove gloves. Ensure gloves removal does not cause 
additional contamination of hands. Gloves can be removed using more 
than one technique (e.g., glove-in-glove or bird beak). 
 
 
5.2.2 
Remove gown. Untie all ties (or unsnap all buttons). 
Some gown ties can be broken rather than untied. Do 
so in gentle manner, avoiding a forceful movement. 
Reach up to the shoulders and carefully pull gown down 
and away from the body. Rolling the gown down is an 
acceptable approach. 
 
 
 
 
5.2.3 
Perform hand hygiene using sanitizer. 
 
 
 
 
5.2.4 
Remove the hairnet. 
 
 
 
 
5.2.5 
Remove shoe covers. 
 
 
 
5.2.6 
Remove face shield or goggles. Carefully remove face 
shield or goggles by grabbing the strap and pulling 
upwards and away from head. Do not touch the front 
of face shield or goggles. 
 
 
5.2.7 
Carefully remove N95 face mask/respirator/ disposable face mask. 
5.2.8 
Perform hand hygiene using hand sanitizer or hand wash. 
 
 
6. Health and safety – Points to remember 
6.1 Use appropriate PPEs as per (HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
6.2 Avoid physical contact with chemical (specially with eyes).

6.3 To complete the job safely, follow the associated risk assessment. 
6.4 Follow the HMC guidelines with regards to COVID 19 Precautionary measures. 
6.5 Always follow your superior's guidelines when performing a task. 
6.6 Comply with all HMC regulations for safety, good hygiene, and infection control. 
6.7 Never leave any PPEs in the area unattended. 
6.8 As much as possible, keep the pathways clear of obstructions. 
 
 
7. Quality Verification & Post Job Check 
 
7.1 Remove your PPE (HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
7.2 Wash your hands thoroughly after the task is completed (HMC Policies CL 7241 Hand Hygiene.) 
7.3 Dispose trashes and PPE pouches to the waste bin. 
7.4 Proceed to the work location as per the work allocation after donning of PPE’s. 
7.5 Make sure doffed PPEs disposed at the designated area and correct bin.

4. Pre-Job Check 
4.1 Infection Control & HSEQ 
4.1.1 
Wash hands as per the approved method prior to commencing task. Check hands for any cuts 
and abrasions, if yes cover with waterproof dressing (Refer to HMC policies CL 7241 Hand 
Hygiene Policy). 
4.1.2 
Wear appropriate PPE as per guidelines (Refer to HMC policies CL 7242 Infection Control 
Practices) 
4.1.3 
Ensure you have been trained in RA, MS, SDS, COSHH and chemical handling. 
4.1.4 
Identify the work area Risk access and place the warning sign. If the area is like a corridor or an 
open space such as patient waiting area etc. the warning sign needs to be placed on both sides. 
4.1.5 
Ventilate the area where appropriate.

4.2 Tools & Equipment 
4.2.1 
Check & prepare all the equipment that may be required for the task. 
4.2.2 
Choose appropriate colour coded tools & equipment and ensure they are assembled safely. 
4.2.3 
Condition of tools – check if these are in clean, good working condition & safe to use. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
5. Job Steps 
5.1 Trolley set up procedure: 
 
5.1.1 
Colour coded MFC should be folded in a 
systematic way and arranged in 
appropriate colour coded boxes on top of 
the trolley, Colour coded chemical bottles 
can be placed along with the MFC if 
required (only in the High Areas Trolleys). 
 
 
 
5.2 Trolley Cabin Arrangements 
 
 
5.2.1 BOTTOM DRAWER 
 
• 
Diluted chemical bottles with 
dilution/expiry date sticker 
• 
Hand soap & Shower gel refills 
• 
Individually folded Garbage bags 
for general waste, bio-hazard 
spill kit etc. 
 
 
 
 
5.2.2 MIDDLE DRAWER 
 
• 
Paper Consumables (Inter fold 
tissues and toilet rolls)

5.2.3 TOP DRAWER 
 
• 
Extra MFC’s 
• 
Basic PPE’s etc. 
 
 
 
5.2.4 
 
Dirty hampers – Blue Bag for dirty 
MFC’s and mops used in all patient 
and non-patient areas (except 
toilets) and RED bag for dirty MFC’s, 
and mops used only inside toilets. 
 
 
5.2.5 
 
Use the tool holders available in the 
trolley to attach the Mop & Floor 
Squeegee Handles, Litter Picker, 
Caution Board, Soft Brush, Dustpan 
with brush etc. 
 
 
5.3 Top-Down System 
 5.3.1 In a clean bucket, prepare 2 litres of diluted cleaning solution. 
** Medium & Low Risk Areas - 16 ml of Ecolab (Oasis Pro 14), 2 litres of water. (This solution is 
diluted and dispensed from the Ecolab dispenser located in the housekeeping room). 
5.3.2 
Place 20-25 microfibre flat mops inside the plastic box with lid. 
5.3.3 
Place the box on a top of the sink in the housekeeping room and pour 
the diluted chemical solution into the plastic box over the chemical tray. 
5.3.4 
Close the box with the lid and seal it properly. 
5.3.5 
Turn the box upside down for pre-soaking (charging or impregnating) 
the mops for at least 20-30 minutes. 
5.3.6 
Place the box on the housekeeping cart. 
 
5.4 Points to Remember: - 
5.4.1 
Appropriate label stickers to be placed on all 3 drawers with details of materials stored 
inside.

5.4.2 
Chemical spray bottles will have color-coded trigger and nozzles based on the areas of usage 
Red for toilets, Blue for medium risk areas and Green for low-risk areas. 
5.4.3 
For the top-down system, 20-25 mop cloths to be placed inside the box, diluted chemical 
solution to be poured into the plastic container over the chemical tray - 2 Litters of water 
and 16 ml chemical (Oasis Pro 14), close the box with the lid and seal it properly, turn the 
box upside down for pre-soaking (charging or impregnating) the mops, pre socking 
procedure after chemical application into the top-down box is very important. Soak the mop 
cloths for at least 20-30 minutes. 
5.4.4 
Tool holders and the additional holders must be available in the trolley. 
5.4.5 
Trolleys are designed with wheel locking system. 
5.4.6 
There are 2 different trolley with a safety closing feature. The 01 trolley have compartments 
will be lined with laundry bag for dirty mops and cloths , and other trolley have 
compartments with domestic garbage bag for domestic waste. 
5.4.7 
Door locking feature is also available on the cart along with a secretive storage space for the 
janitor can hide the door key. Ensure the trolley is always kept locked. 
5.4.8 
Housekeeping trolley can be stocked with all the required amenities to complete a shifts 
cleaning task. 
5.4.9 
The housekeeping trolley must be always well organized and well stocked before starting 
each shift. 
5.4.10 A well-stocked housekeeping trolley will avoid unnecessary movements to the 
housekeeping room. 
5.4.11 The number of supplies loaded on to the housekeeping trolley will be according to the 
assigned area or location. 
5.4.12 Housekeeping trolleys are normally prepared from the area housekeeping room. 
5.4.13 Never over stock or under stock the trolley, Overstocking will increase the risk of accidents 
or damage to the supplies. Whereas under stocking can be slow down the efficiency of 
cleaning tasks due to the unnecessary movements to the housekeeping room to collect the 
required materials. 
 
5.5 Housekeeping Trolley Cleaning Methods: 
5.5.1 
Janitor should check housekeeping trolley before using to ensure its safety, and make sure 
there is no damage in its parts. 
5.5.2 
Clean all parts of Housekeeping trolley, Cleaning the soiled cloths and mops collection bag 
and its lid, tray, tool baskets, wringer, and bucket/ top-down system. 
5.5.3 
Using MFC with chemical/Disinfectant solution, wipe all surfaces of the housekeeping 
trolley including wheels. 
5.5.4 
Allow all surfaces to air dry. 
5.5.5 
Return housekeeping trolley to Housekeeping room for next use. 
5.5.6 
Report any broken or damaged parts to the supervisor. 
 
 
6. Health and Safety – Points to Remember 
6.1 Always place the precautionary/warning signage

6.2 Stay in a comfortable working position, do not over-stretch to reach the task area. 
6.3 Handle all chemicals safely according to COSHH / SDS. 
6.4 Use appropriate PPEs (refer to HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
6.5 Avoid physical contact with chemical (specially with eyes) 
6.6 To complete the job safely, follow the associated risk assessment. 
6.7 Follow the HMC guidelines with regards to COVID 19 Precautionary measures. 
6.8 Perform a complete workplace 360 degrees check for any unusual conditions or new hazards and 
report them to your team leader right away. 
6.9 Only use appropriate tools and equipment for the task. 
6.10 Comply with all HMC regulations for safety, hygiene, and infection control. 
6.11 Never leave any tool or material in the area unattended. 
6.12 As much as possible, keep the pathways clear of obstructions. 
 
7. Quality Verification & Post Job Check 
7.1 Warning signs are checked, cleaned, disinfected, and removed as necessary. 
7.2 MFC should be laundered or disposed of as appropriate. 
7.3 Double Check if there are no tools, materials left in the area. 
7.4 Remove your PPE (HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
7.5 Wash your hands thoroughly after the task is completed (HMC Policies CL 7241 Hand Hygiene.) 
7.6 Did you notice any additional hazards during the work task that wasn’t mentioned in the Method 
Statement? (if yes, inform your team leader) 
7.7 Are there any faults with your tools and PPE that you must report? 
7.8 Report all Damages, Incidents & accidents / Health events to your team leader.

5.5 Housekeeping Trolley Cleaning Methods: 
5.5.1 
Janitor should check housekeeping trolley before using to ensure its safety, and make sure 
there is no damage in its parts. 
5.5.2 
Clean all parts of Housekeeping trolley, Cleaning the soiled cloths and mops collection bag 
and its lid, tray, tool baskets, wringer, and bucket/ top-down system. 
5.5.3 
Using MFC with chemical/Disinfectant solution, wipe all surfaces of the housekeeping trolley 
including wheels. 
5.5.4 
Allow all surfaces to air dry. 
5.5.5 
Return housekeeping trolley to Housekeeping room for next use. 
5.5.6 
Report any broken or damaged parts to the supervisor. 
 
 
6. Health and Safety – Points to Remember 
 
6.1 Always place the precautionary/warning signage 
6.2 Stay in a comfortable working position, do not over-stretch to reach the task area. 
6.3 Handle all chemicals safely according to COSHH / SDS. 
6.4 Use appropriate PPEs (refer to HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
6.5 Avoid physical contact with chemical (specially with eyes) 
6.6 To complete the job safely, follow the associated risk assessment. 
6.7 Follow the HMC guidelines with regards to COVID 19 Precautionary measures. 
6.8 Perform a complete workplace 360 degrees check for any unusual conditions or new hazards and 
report them to your team leader right away. 
6.9 Only use appropriate tools and equipment for the task. 
6.10 Comply with all HMC regulations for safety, hygiene, and infection control. 
6.11 Never leave any tool or material in the area unattended. 
6.12 As much as possible, keep the pathways clear of obstructions. 
 
 
7. Quality Verification & Post Job Check 
 
7.1 Warning signs are checked, cleaned, disinfected, and removed as necessary. 
7.2 MFC should be laundered or disposed of as appropriate. 
7.3 Double Check if there are no tools, materials left in the area. 
7.4 Remove your PPE (HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
7.5 Wash your hands thoroughly after the task is completed (HMC Policies CL 7241 Hand Hygiene.) 
7.6 Did you notice any additional hazards during the work task that wasn’t mentioned in the Method 
Statement? (if yes, inform your team leader) 
7.7 Are there any faults with your tools and PPE that you must report? 
7.8 Report all Damages, Incidents & accidents / Health events to your team leader.

4. Pre-Job Check 
 
4.1 Infection Control & HSEQ 
4.1.1 
Wash hands as per the approved method prior to commencing task. Check hands for any cuts and 
abrasions, if yes cover with waterproof dressing (Refer to HMC policies CL 7241 Hand Hygiene 
Policy). 
4.1.2 
Wear appropriate PPE as per guidelines (Refer to HMC policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices) 
4.1.3 
Ensure you have been trained in RA, MS, SDS, COSHH and chemical handling. 
4.1.4 
For ladder use follow GL-HSEQ-11-T12 Ladders Inspection and Use Checklist and GL-HSEQ-11-T13 
Ladder Inspection Form 
4.1.5 
Identify the work area Risk access and place the warning sign. If the area is like a corridor or an open 
space such as patient waiting area etc. the warning sign needs to be placed on both sides. 
4.1.6 
Ensure that the work area is free from any hindrance or obstacles that could affect the Cleaning / 
Housekeeping operation. 
4.1.7 
Switch off electric devices or hanging lights while performing the task. 
 
4.2 Tools, Materials & Equipment 
4.2.1 
Check & prepare all the equipment that may be required for the task. 
4.2.2 
Choose appropriate colour coded tools & equipment and ensure they are assembled safely. 
4.2.3 
Condition of tools – check if these are in clean, good working condition & safe to use. 
4.2.4 
Check & prepare all the equipment and materials that may be required on service trolley to save 
wasted journeys. 
 
4.3 Chemical Dilution & Usage 
4.3.1 
Always check that the appropriate cleaning chemicals are being used for the task (Oasis Pro 14, Pro 
41, Actichlor solution etc.) 
4.3.2 
Ensure that the chemicals spray bottles are properly labelled with details such as dilution /expiry 
date & time. 
4.3.3 
Chemicals spray bottles nozzle must be colour coded according to the risk area. 
4.3.4 
Diluted chemical solution is dispensed from the Automatic Chemical Dispensing machine located in 
the housekeeping rooms. The chemical dispensing machine is pre-calibrated to dispense the 
chemical solution as per HMC Infection Control guidelines. 
4.3.5 
The dilution ratio for Oasis Pro 14 and Pro 41 is 8ml chemical for 1 ltr water and 1:1000 ppm for 
Actichlor (1 Actichlor tablet added to 1 ltr water). 
4.3.6 
Always check the preparation date of the diluted cleaning chemicals. The usage of the chemical 
solution must be within 24 hours of the dilution process. 
4.3.7 
Ensure that you have COSHH Awareness and chemical handling trainings. 
4.3.8 
Follow the Proper dilution of the cleaning agents and should be referred to manufacturer’s 
recommendation and HMC-IPC guidelines.

5. Job Steps 
 
5.1 Start with dusting by using a dry folded MFC in a manageable area. Vertical surfaces working from top to 
bottom. MFC turned all clean side were used and replace if necessary. 
5.2 Use snake microfiber hand dusters where the spaces hard to reach. 
5.3 Take another clean & folded MFC, apply chemical into MFC by using a 
chemical spray bottle. 
5.4 Wipe the dusted surface with damp MFC, task must be carried out in a 
systematic way using straight overlapping passes to clean all the surfaces 
effectively and efficiently. 
5.5 Turn or change the MFC cloth if appropriate, re-folded after a manageable 
use. 
5.6 Used MFC should be washed before recharging. 
5.7 One MFC to one location shall be followed. 
5.8 If any heavy stains found on the surface, then use a non- abrasive pad to remove and repeat damp wiping 
process again over the surface. 
5.9 The surface shall let dry by itself without any intervention. 
5.10 Refer to MS-CLE-HMC-018, MS-CLE-HMC-019 for dusting and damp wiping in terminal cleaning high-risk 
areas. 
 
6. Health and safety – Points to remember 
6.1 Always place the precautionary/warning signage. 
6.2 Stay in a comfortable working position, do not over-stretch to reach the task area, use long handled tools 
instead. 
6.3 Handle all chemicals safely according to COSHH / SDS. 
6.4 Use appropriate PPEs as per (HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
6.5 Avoid physical contact with chemical (specially with eyes). 
6.6 To complete the job safely, follow the associated risk assessment. 
6.7 Follow the HMC guidelines with regards to COVID 19 Precautionary measures. 
6.8 Perform a complete workplace 360 degrees check for any unusual conditions or new hazards and report 
them to your team leader right away. 
6.9 Use the appropriate tools and equipment for the task. 
6.10 Staff to follow TBT for slip, trip & fall. 
6.11 Always follow your superior's guidelines when performing a task. 
6.12 Comply with all HMC regulations for safety, hygiene, and infection control. 
6.13 Never leave any tool or material in the area unattended. 
6.14 As much as possible, keep the pathways clear of obstructions. 
6.15 Ensure that you have COSHH Awareness and chemical handling trainings.

7.11 Report all damages, incidents & accidents / health events to your team leader.

4. Pre-Job Check 
4.1.1 
Prior to commencing the damp mopping process, ensure that a thorough dust mopping of the area has 
been completed (except isolation rooms). For dust mopping procedure, please see MS - CLE-HMC-007-
Dust moping. 
4.1.2 
To remove any stubborn stains/marks before mopping, use an abrasive pad and in case of residual glue 
from floor stickering or chewing gum stuck on the floor, use a floor scrapper. 
 
4.2 Infection Control & HSEQ 
4.2.1 
Wash hands as per the approved method prior to commencing task. Check hands for any cuts and 
abrasions, if yes cover with waterproof dressing (Refer to HMC policies CL 7241 Hand Hygiene Policy). 
4.2.2 
Wear appropriate PPE as per guidelines (Refer to HMC policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices) 
4.2.3 
Ensure you have been trained in RA, MS, SDS, COSHH and chemical handling. 
4.2.4 
Identify the work area Risk access and place the warning sign. If the area is like a corridor or an open 
space such as patient waiting area etc. the warning sign needs to be placed on both sides. 
4.2.5 
Ventilate the area where appropriate. 
 
4.3 Tools & Equipment 
4.3.1 
Check & prepare all the equipment that may be required for the task. 
4.3.2 
Choose appropriate colour coded tools & equipment and ensure they are assembled safely. 
4.3.3 
Condition of tools – check if these are in clean, good working condition & safe to use. 
4.3.4 
Floors are checked for any adhesive soiling, use a non-abrasive pad as required only. 
 
4.4 Top-Down System 
4.4.1 
In a clean bucket, prepare 2 litres of diluted cleaning solution. 
4.4.2 
**High Risk Areas - 16 ml of Ecolab Oasis Pro 14 chemical and 2 litres of water. 
4.4.3 
** Medium & Low Risk Areas - 16 ml of Ecolab High Performance Neutral Floor Cleaner 2 litres of water. 
(This solution is diluted and dispensed from the Ecolab dispenser located in the housekeeping room). 
4.4.4 
Place 20-25 microfibre flat mops inside the plastic box with lid. 
4.4.5 
Place the box on a clean pallet in the housekeeping room and pour the diluted chemical solution into the 
plastic box over the chemical tray.

## Page 28

METHOD STATEMENT 
MS-CLE-HMC-06 
Damp Mopping 
DATE 
5/14/2024 
REVISION 
V3.0 
 
Template version: 2.0 dated 01-Feb-2018 
 
Page 4 of 6 
 
4.4.6 
Close the box with the lid and seal it properly. 
4.4.7 
Turn the box upside down for pre-soaking (charging or impregnating) the mops for at least 20-30 
minutes. 
4.4.8 
Place the box on the housekeeping cart. 
 
4.5 Double Mop Bucket w/Wringer 
4.5.1 
For ISOLATION Rooms, a double mop bucket with wringer will be used with disposable mops. 
4.5.2 
Ensure that each Isolation room is assigned with a dedicated double mop bucket with wringer and the 
exact room number is written on this bucket set and it is stored in the housekeeping room. 
4.5.3 
In the blue coloured bucket, prepare a cleaning solution with 8 ml Oasis Pro 14 chemical to 1 litre of 
water ratio. 
 
5. Job Steps 
5.1 
Using the Aluminium handle with mop holder, attach the pre-soaked microfiber flat mop to the mop 
holder. 
5.2 
ONLY for CONTACT ISOLATION ROOMS, ensure only disposable mops are used 
with dedicated top-down system. 
5.3 
Commence the process by mopping the floor along the edges of areas 
lengthwise. Mop edges adjacent to skirting boards (mop in parallel to skirting 
boards – cutting in) 
5.4 
Wipe skirting boards to remove splash marks, if any. 
5.5 
In a systematic way, mop the floor in a figure 8 shape, using continuous 
strokes overlapping passes to make sure that all the areas are effectively and 
efficiently cleaned. 
5.6 
Always ensure one mop is to one room ratio. 
5.7 
After completion of task, remove the used/soiled dirty mop with the help of a 
picker and place inside the hampers in the housekeeping cart. 
5.8 
Place the dirty flat mops of all areas (except toilets) in the blue hamper bag 
and dirty mops from Toilets to be placed inside the red coloured hamper. 
5.9 
All disposable mops heads to be disposed inside contact isolation rooms. 
5.10 
Area mopped shall be left to dry and there shall be no obvious mop marks. 
 
5.11 
For marble floors, use Kentucky mops to apply Ecolab no-low maintenance floor cleaner and protector 
to get the maximum chemical contact on the marble pores. 
 
6. Health and safety – Points to remember 
6.1 
Always place the precautionary/warning signage 
6.1.1 
Stay in a comfortable working position, do not over-stretch to reach the task area, use long handled 
tools instead. 
6.1.2 
Handle all chemicals safely according to COSHH / SDS. 
6.1.3 
Use appropriate PPEs (refer to HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices).

6.1.7

4.2.3 Condition of tools – check if these are in clean, good working condition & safe to use. 
4.2.4 Place the dust mop handles with their respective mops on the housekeeping trolley. 
4.2.5 Floors are checked for any adhesive soiling, use a non-abrasive pad as required only. 
 
 
5. Job Steps 
 
5.1 Check the area that needs to be cleaned for chewing gum and big waste items, if any collect it in a 
waste collection bag (use scrapper to remove chewing gum). 
5.2 The chosen floor area to start dust mopping should be adjacent to the skirting 
board and swept by cutting in parallel to skirting boards. 
5.3 Commence sweeping in a systemic manner, starting from the far end corner of 
the room, and coming back (S type action), the sweeping shall be in one stroke 
and the mop shall never be lifted during sweeping. 
5.4 To check mop, turn it up slowly without lifting it, if mop is saturated with soil 
or dust, then it shall be cleaned using vacuum cleaner (to operate suction 
cleaner, ensure that the machine is off, the socket is off, connect the machine 
to the socket, put the socket ON and then the machine ON 
5.5 Upon completion clean the mop with vacuum cleaner (if applicable) 
5.6 Once the use of vacuum cleaner is completed, switch it off, switch power 
socket off, and unplug (never unplug by pulling cable) 
5.7 Once sweeping is completed, soil shall be collected using Joby Kit and dispose 
the debris in the waste collection cart. 
 
 
6. Health and safety – Points to remember 
6.1 Always place the precautionary/warning signage 
6.2 Wear the right non-slip safety footwear for the job. 
6.3 Stay in a comfortable working position, do not over-stretch to reach the task area rather than using 
long handled tools. 
6.4 Use appropriate PPEs (refer to HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
6.5 To complete the job safely, follow the associated risk assessment. 
6.6 Follow the HMC guidelines with regards to COVID 19 Precautionary measures. 
6.7 Perform a complete workplace 360 degrees check for any unusual conditions or new hazards and 
report them to your team leader right away. 
6.8 Only use appropriate tools and equipment for the task. 
6.9 Comply with all HMC regulations for safety, hygiene, and infection control. 
6.10 Never leave any tool or material in the area unattended. 
6.11 As much as possible, keep the pathways clear of obstructions.

7. Quality Verification & Post Job Check 
 
7.1 After completion of the task, the appearance of area shall be clean and satisfactory. 
7.2 In case any furniture/equipment etc. are moved from the cleaning area, it shall be restored back to its 
original state (reinstate the area). 
7.3 Ensure that the tools, signs, and barricading has been removed from the task location and safely take 
them back to the HK room/store and keep the area clear for user. 
7.4 Double Check if there are no tools, materials left in the area. 
7.5 Notify your team leader that the work is complete, and area is free of materials. 
7.6 Remove your PPE (HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
7.7 Wash your hands thoroughly after the task is completed (HMC Policies CL 7241 Hand Hygiene.) 
7.8 Did you notice any additional hazards during the work task that wasn’t mentioned in the Method 
Statement? (if yes, inform your team leader) 
7.9 Are there any faults with your tools and PPE that you must report? 
7.10 Report all Damages, Incidents & accidents / Health events to your team leader. 
7.11 Waste must be properly disposed as per SA-1054-Hazardous materials and waste 
management program.

4. Pre-Job Check 
4.1 Infection Control & HSEQ 
4.1.1 
Wash hands as per the approved method prior to commencing task. 
4.1.2 
Check hands for any cuts and abrasions, if yes cover with waterproof dressing (Refer to HMC 
policies CL 7241 Hand Hygiene Policy). 
4.1.3 
Wear appropriate PPE as per guidelines (Refer to HMC policies CL 7242 Infection Control 
Practices) 
4.1.4 
Ensure you have been trained in RA, MS, SDS, COSHH and chemical handling. 
4.1.5 
To Identify the work area and place the warning signs as needed. 
4.1.6 
Ensure that the work area is free from any hindrance or obstacles that could affect the Cleaning / 
Housekeeping operation. 
 
4.2 Tools & Equipment 
4.2.1 
Check & prepare all the equipment that may be required for the task. 
4.2.2 
Choose appropriate colour coded tools & equipment and ensure they are assembled safely. 
4.2.3 
Condition of tools – check if these are in clean, good working condition & safe to use. 
4.2.4 
Ensure the chemicals spray bottles are properly labelled with details such as dilution /expiry date & 
time. 
4.2.5 
Always check the preparation date of the diluted cleaning chemicals (Oasis Pro 14, Pro 41 Glass 
Cleaner). The usage of the chemical solution must be within 24 hours of the dilution process. 
4.2.6 
Any damage to surfaces or a reportable incident should be reported to the team leader. 
4.2.7 
Always monitor the tyre pressure of outdoor window cleaning system and refill when necessary. 
 
5. Job Steps 
 
5.1 Low Level Glass Cleaning 
5.1.1 
Place “Cleaning in Progress” signage; “floor signs should be visible from all entry & exit points”. 
5.1.2 
Fill the chemical bucket with diluted Ecolab Oasis Pro Glass Cleaner. 
5.1.3 
Remove the dust from the glass surfaces with MFC/ dry applicator in a 
vertical motion. 
5.1.4 
Rinse the applicator inside the diluted glass cleaner chemical.

## Page 39

METHOD STATEMENT 
MS-CLE-HMC-05 
Glass Cleaning 
DATE 
5/14/2024 
REVISION 
V2.0 
 
Template version: 2.0 dated 01-Feb-2018 
 
Page 5 of 8 
 
5.1.5 
Apply the cleaning agent (Ecolab Oasis Pro Glass Cleaner) on the glass 
surface by using an applicator. 
5.1.6 
Apply the applicator using a figure “S” pattern or in a systematic way (zig 
zag/vertical/ horizontal/parallel moves) with overlaps. 
5.1.7 
Using a glass squeegee, remove the chemical from the glass surface 
properly and make sure that there is no more chemical content left over. 
5.1.8 
Perform the task in a systematic way (zig zag/vertical/ horizontal/parallel 
moves) depends on the design of glass surface. 
5.1.9 
Pay attention to wipe the squeegee with MFC in every stroke. 
5.1.10 Wipe out the excess water from the frames and glass surface with a dry 
MFC. 
5.1.11 Remove any residual water from the glass using an MFC. 
5.1.12 Empty dirty/soiled water in designated disposal area. 
5.1.13 Perform damp moping task if the floor is wet during glass cleaning task. 
Refer MS-CLE-HMC-006 for damp mopping steps. 
5.1.14 Remove the warning signs once the floor is completely dried. 
5.2 For High Level Glass Cleaning 
5.2.1 
Use podium ladder and select the ladder depending on the height. 
5.2.2 
Adjust the height of the selected podium ladder. 
5.2.3 
Follow FMM work at height procedure while using a ladder. 
5.2.4 
Refer the below Podium Ladders Inspections and Use. 
5.2.5 
Use extension pole if the glass surface still not reachable with the help of a podium ladder. 
5.2.6 
Continue the glass cleaning steps as mentioned above. 
 
5.3 Indoor Window Cleaning System 
5.3.1 
Assemble the window cleaning machine with required accessories. (Pad holder, extendable pole 
with chemical trigger, cleaning pad, belt along with solution 
tank/motor/battery) 
5.3.2 
Fill Oasis pro 41 diluted glass cleaner in the solution tank. (Available in the 
Ecolab chemical dispenser machine) 
5.3.3 
Wear the accessories belt on your waist. 
5.3.4 
Always check if the battery is fully charged before starting the task. 
5.3.5 
Check the working condition of the motor. 
5.3.6 
Connect the battery cable to the motor. 
5.3.7 
Attach the cleaning pad on the holder to start the task. 
5.3.8 
Adjust the pole length according to the task and height, make sure the 
lock engaged carefully.

5.3.9 
Start the cleaning by pressing on the trigger to apply chemical on the 
glass, apply chemical in a manageable area. 
5.3.10 Wipe the glass over the applied chemical to remove the dust and dirt. 
5.3.11 Should follow systematic overlapping passes while cleaning the glasses 
for better result or to do not miss any glass surface. 
5.3.12 Attach another clean pad on pad holder to clean the excess chemical or 
dust left over on the glass surface. 
5.3.13 Use podium ladder wherever required depends on the height of the glass surface. 
 
5.4 Outdoor Window Cleaning System 
5.4.1 
Wheel the machine to scheduled task location. 
5.4.2 
Locate facility water source and attach the water hose pipe. 
5.4.3 
The other end of the hose pipe to be connected with the filter inlet. 
5.4.4 
Make sure the valve is firmly attached to the unit. 
5.4.5 
Attach the red water fed pole hose to the top of the filter unit. 
5.4.6 
Turn the valve to the ‘off’ position. 
5.4.7 
Attach the square brush on the top of the pole and close the clamp. 
5.4.8 
Attach the red hose to the T bar located at the end of the brush hose. 
5.4.9 
With all the attachments, extend the inline pole by releasing the black latch. 
5.4.10 When the blue tape appears, retract the pole rather than extending it. 
5.4.11 Switch on the facility's water supply before turning on the motor. 
5.4.12 Adjust the height of water fed pole according to the area to be cleaned. 
5.4.13 Control the water flow with valve based on the dirt and dust accumulation 
 on the glass surface and frame. 
5.4.14 Clean the glass surface and frames with the attached square brush 
in an up and down motion. 
5.4.15 Always select the glass surfaces to be cleaned from top to bottom as 
the bottom glasses will be always dirty due to the water flow from the fed pole. 
5.4.16 Chemical application not required for this task as the machine equipped with water purifying 
system that gives a better finishing on the glass surface and frames. 
 
5.5 Sky Light Cleaning 
 
5.5.1 
Refer to 5.4 to use Outdoor windows cleaning system to clean skylight. 
5.5.2 
Use podium ladder wherever required depends on the height of the 
skylight from roof top. 
5.5.3 
After cleaning the skylight glass, carryout cleaning of skylight frames 
 and claddings as mentioned below. 
5.5.4 
Remove the excess water from the skylight frames and claddings with an MFC. 
5.5.5 
Damp wipe the skylight frame and cladding with an MFC. 
5.5.6 
Move the outdoor window cleaning system and podium ladder to the next skylight area scheduled. 
5.5.7 
Continue cleaning the remaining skylights by following the above steps.

5.6 Podium Ladders Inspections and Use. 
 
5.6.1 
Check all 4 castors are secure and the fixing bolts are tightened correctly. 
5.6.2 
Check all 2-wheel brakes operate and lock the wheel and castor into position. 
5.6.3 
Check the tubes and aluminium components of the podium step. There should not be any cracks or 
excessive dents or distortion. 
5.6.4 
Check there is free movement of all hinge points of the podium step. Check the plywood platform. 
There should not be excessive contamination, cracking, holes or damage, including any missing or 
damaged rivet fixings. 
5.6.5 
Check the following podium step locking mechanisms are operating and locking correctly. Ladder 
claws and locks, gate claw and lock, rear folding frame elbow locking joints and the platform 
locking tabs. 
5.6.6 
Make sure the total load (1 person plus tools & materials) should not be more than 150kg. 
5.6.7 
Make sure the 4 castors are pointing outwards and the wheel brakes are locked. 
5.6.8 
Check the access ladder is located correctly. 
5.6.9 
Make sure the podium step is on flat, level, dry and firm ground. 
5.6.10 Make sure the podium step is correctly positioned to avoid overreaching. 
5.6.11 Make sure the platform is at the correct height. 
5.6.12 Make sure the safety gate is shut and locked once you are on the platform. 
Key Notes: 
5.6.13 Never stand on the guardrails, boxes, stepladders, or other objects on the platform, to gain 
additional working height. 
5.6.14 Never suspend the podium step from another structure or level podium step. 
5.6.15 The maximum number of occupants must not exceed one. 
5.6.16 Never use the podium step if any part is damaged or shows signs of excessive wear. 
5.6.17 Never move the podium step when a person is standing on it. 
5.6.18 Never over-reach or lean too far over the side of the podium step. 
5.6.19 Never use the podium step as a method of gaining access to another structure or level. 
5.6.20 In addition, Use Pre use Ladder checklist. 
 
6. Health and safety – Points to Remember 
6.1.1 
Always place the precautionary/warning signage. 
6.1.2 
Stay in a comfortable working position, do not over-stretch to reach the task area, use long 
handled tools instead. 
6.1.3 
Handle all chemicals safely according to COSHH / SDS. 
6.1.4 
Use appropriate PPEs as per (HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
6.1.5 
Avoid physical contact with chemical (specially with eyes). 
6.1.6 
To complete the job safely, follow the associated risk assessment. 
6.1.7 
Follow the HMC guidelines with regards to COVID 19 Precautionary measures. 
6.1.8 
Perform a complete workplace 360 degrees check for any unusual conditions or new hazards and 
report them to your team leader right away. 
6.1.9 
Use the appropriate tools for the task. 
6.1.10 Comply with all HMC regulations on safety, hygiene, and infection control.

4. Pre-Job Check 
4.1 Infection Control & HSEQ 
4.1.1 
Wash hands as per the approved method prior to commencing task. Check hands for any cuts 
and abrasions, if yes cover with waterproof dressing (Refer to HMC policies CL 7241 Hand 
Hygiene Policy). 
4.1.2 
Wear appropriate PPE as per guidelines (Refer to HMC policies CL 7242 Infection Control 
Practices) 
4.1.3 
Ensure you have been trained in RA, MS, SDS, COSHH and chemical handling. 
4.1.4 
To Identify the work area and place the warning signs as needed. 
4.1.5 
Ensure that the work area is free from any hindrance or obstacles that could affect the 
Cleaning / Housekeeping operation. 
 
4.2 Tools & Equipment 
4.2.1 
Check & prepare all the equipment that may be required for the task. 
4.2.2 
Choose appropriate colour coded tools & equipment and ensure they are assembled safely. 
4.2.3 
Condition of tools – check if these are in clean, good working condition & safe to use. 
4.2.4

4.2.5

5. Job Steps 
5.1 Place the Cleaning in Progress signage, “Floor signs should be visible from all entry & exit points”. 
5.2 Cover the WC bowl and flush it before starting and then push water back in the 
WC using the WC brush. 
5.3 Apply the Ecolab Maxx Into WC2 chemical in the WC and then close the lid.

## Page 46

5.4 Start the cleaning process from Clean to Dirty using a red coloured disposable 
cloth and Oasis Pro14 All-Purpose cleaner (8 ml/ltr). 
5.5 Special attention shall be given to sanitary appliances and high-touch areas such 
as toilet handles, rim, overflow, handrails, etc. 
5.6 Rinse the external surface of the WC including seat cover and hidden areas with 
water to remove any urine splashes or visible dirt. 
5.7 Clean external surface of the WC including seat cover and hidden areas of WC by 
using a red sponge and Oasis Pro 14 chemical. 
5.8 Rinse with water after cleaning external surfaces and seat cover. 
5.9 Lift the WC seat cover, use the toilet brush to clean inside the WC and flush while 
WC bowl is closed. Use flush water to clean brush and return it to its container. 
5.10 Using the RED coloured MFC, spray the Oasis Pro 14 Solution from the spray 
bottle with Red Nozzle onto the Red coloured MFC and damp wipe the WC 
including the seat cover. For ISOLATION ROOMS, please use RED coloured disposable cloth. 
5.11 Repeat the process with another dried RED MFC (disposable red cloth – for high-risk area) until the 
surfaces are completely dried. 
5.12 Do not use RED coloured MFC on other surfaces except WC/Urinal bowl. 
5.13 White coloured MFC can be used for other surfaces such as toilet bowl brush and waste bin placed 
beside WC. 
5.14 After cleaning WC, rinse the floor with water to eliminate urine splashes and drops. 
5.15 Push water to the drain with a floor squeeze. 
5.16 Apply Oasis Pro 14 chemical on the floor to clean and disinfect the cubicle floor. 
5.17 Damp mop the floor (Refer MS-CLE-HMC-06 - Damp Moping) 
 
6. Health and safety – Points to remember 
6.1 Always place the precautionary/warning signage. 
6.2 Stay in a comfortable working position, do not over-stretch to reach the task area rather than using 
long handled tools. 
6.3 Handle all chemicals safely according to COSHH / SDS. 
6.4 Use appropriate PPEs as per (HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
6.5 Avoid physical contact with chemical (specially with eyes). 
6.6 To complete the job safely, follow the associated risk assessment. 
6.7 Follow the HMC guidelines with regards to COVID 19 Precautionary measures. 
6.8 Perform a complete workplace 360 degrees check for any unusual conditions or new hazards and 
report them to your team leader right away. 
6.9 Use the appropriate tools and equipment for the task. 
6.10 Comply with all HMC regulations for safety, hygiene, and infection control. 
6.11 Never leave any tool or material in the area unattended. 
6.12 As much as possible, keep the pathways clear of obstructions.

7.5 Remove your PPE (HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
7.6 Wash your hands thoroughly after the task is completed (HMC Policies CL 7241 Hand Hygiene). 
7.7 Did you notice any additional hazards during the work task that wasn’t mentioned in the Method 
Statement? (if yes, inform your team leader) 
7.8 Are there any faults with your tools and PPE that you must report? 
7.9 Report all Damages, Incidents & accidents / Health events to your team leader. 
7.10 Waste must be properly disposed as per SA-1054-Hazardous materials and waste management 
program. 
7.11 MFC and disposable cloth should be laundered or disposed of as appropriate.

4.1.1 
Wash hands as per the approved method prior to commencing task. Check hands for any cuts 
and abrasions, if yes cover with waterproof dressing (Refer to HMC policies CL 7241 Hand 
Hygiene Policy). 
4.1.2 
Wear appropriate PPE as per guidelines (Refer to HMC policies CL 7242 Infection Control 
Practices) 
4.1.3 
Ensure you have been trained in RA, MS, SDS, COSHH and chemical handling. 
4.1.4 
To Identify the work area and place the warning signs as needed. 
4.1.5 
Ensure that the work area is free from any hindrance or obstacles that could affect the 
Cleaning / Housekeeping operation. 
 
4.2 Tools & Equipment 
4.2.1 
Check & prepare all the equipment that may be required for the task. 
4.2.2 
Choose appropriate colour coded tools & equipment and ensure they are assembled safely. 
4.2.3 
Condition of tools – check if these are in clean, good working condition & safe to use. 
4.2.4 
Ensure the chemicals spray bottles are properly labelled with details such as dilution /expiry 
date & time. 
4.2.5

the chemical solution must be within 24 hours of the dilution process. 
4.2.6 
Staff to follow the Proper dilution of the cleaning agents and should be referred to 
manufacturer’s recommendation and HMC-IPC guidelines. 
 
5. Job Steps 
 
5.1 Place the warning yellow sign in a visible area. 
a. in front of the wash basin that is going to be cleaned 
b. or outside the washroom if it is a small area. 
 
5.2 Remove the plughole, waste, hair and rinse the wash basin surface with 
water and place waste in the nearest waste bin. 
5.3 Remember – do not get the finger trapped while removing the plughole 
cover. 
5.4 Apply Oasis Pro 14 All Purpose cleaning solution into a sponge and scrub 
the wash basin and related surfaces after the chemical reaction time. 
5.5 Use the squeegee to remove the chemical and the water left on the other 
surfaces. 
5.6 Dry the surface using the MFC. 
5.7 Pay attention to corners and hidden areas underneath and around the wash 
basin. 
5.8 Rinse wash basin surface with water after scrubbing. 
5.9 Remove the water from wash basin surfaces by using white MFC/ white disposable cloth and squeeze. 
5.10 Repeat the process with a dry white MFC/ white disposable cloth till the surfaces are completely 
dried. 
5.11 Wipe the plug hole and tap fittings with white MFC (White Disposable cloths for Isolation Rooms). 
Buff the steel fittings with a dry white MFC. 
5.12 Fix the plughole into the appropriate space.

6. Health and safety – Points to remember 
6.1 Always place the precautionary/warning signage 
6.2 Stay in a comfortable working position, do not over-stretch to reach the task area rather than using 
long handled tools. 
6.3 Handle all chemicals safely according to COSHH / SDS 
6.4 Use appropriate PPEs as per (HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
6.5 Avoid physical contact with chemical (specially with eyes) 
6.6 To complete the job safely, follow the associated risk assessment. 
6.7 Follow the HMC guidelines with regards to COVID 19 Precautionary measures. 
6.8 Perform a complete workplace 360 degrees check for any unusual conditions or new hazards and 
report them to your team leader right away. 
6.9 Only use the appropriate tools and equipment for the task. 
6.10 Comply with all HMC regulations for safety, hygiene, and infection control. 
6.11 Never leave any tool or material in the area unattended. 
6.12 As much as possible, keep the pathways clear of obstructions. 
 
7. Quality Verification & Post Job Check 
7.1 Visually check the wash basin free from stains, dirt, hair or any chemical residues. 
7.2 Ensure that the tools, signs, and barricading has been removed from the task location and safely take 
them back to the HK room/store and keep the area clear for user. 
7.3 Double Check if there are no tools, materials left in the area. 
7.4 Notify your team leader that the work is complete, and area is free of materials. 
7.5 Remove your PPE (HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
7.6 Wash your hands thoroughly after the task is completed (HMC Policies CL 7241 Hand Hygiene.) 
7.7 Did you notice any additional hazards during the work task that wasn’t mentioned in the Method 
Statement? (if yes, inform your team leader) 
7.8 Are there any faults with your tools and PPE that you must report? 
7.9 Report all Damages, Incidents & accidents / Health events to your team leader. 
7.10 Waste must be properly disposed as per SA-1054-Hazardous materials and waste management 
program. 
7.11 MFC/disposable cloth should be laundered or disposed of as appropriate.

4. Pre-Job Check 
4.1 Infection Control & HSEQ 
4.1.1 
Wash hands as per the approved method prior to commencing task. Check hands for any cuts 
and abrasions, if yes cover with waterproof dressing (Refer to HMC policies CL 7241 Hand 
Hygiene Policy). 
4.1.2 
Wear appropriate PPE as per guidelines (Refer to HMC policies CL 7242 Infection Control 
Practices) 
4.1.3 
To Identify the work area and place the barricades and warning signs as needed. 
4.1.4 
Warning sign to be placed on both the entrance/exit doors. 
4.1.5 
Ensure that the work area is free from any hindrance or obstacles that could affect the Cleaning 
/ Housekeeping operation. 
 
4.2 Tools & Equipment 
4.2.1 
Check & prepare all the equipment that may be required for the task. 
4.2.2 
Choose appropriate colour coded tools & equipment and ensure they are assembled safely. 
4.2.3 
Condition of tools – check if these are in clean, good working condition & safe to use. 
4.2.4 
Place the dust mop handles with their respective mops on the housekeeping trolley. 
4.2.5 
Ensure the trolley is well stocked with cleaning agents (chemicals), microfibre dusters and pre-
Charged microfibre mops as per colour code, toilet rolls, interfold tissue packs, garbage bags 
(black and yellow) etc. before starting work in line with the relevant COSHH card. 
4.2.6 
Always check the preparation date of the diluted cleaning chemicals (Oasis Pro 14, Pro 41 Glass 
Cleaner etc.) The usage of the chemical solution must be within 24 hours of the dilution process. 
4.2.7 
Fill a cleaning solution of 8 ml Oasis Pro 14 chemical to 1 litre of water ratio in a small bucket 
with BLUE handle and place it on top of the trolley/cart. 
(This solution is diluted and dispensed from the Ecolab dispenser located in the housekeeping 
room).

## Page 57

METHOD STATEMENT 
MS-CLE-HMC-26 
Patient Room Cleaning 
DATE 
5/15/2024 
REVISION 
V2.0 
 
Template version: 2.0 dated 01-Feb-2018 
 
Page 5 of 12 
 
4.2.8 
Floors are checked for any adhesive soiling, use a non-abrasive pad as required only. 
 
4.3 Floor Scrubbing Machine & Wet/Dry Vacuum Machine Pre-Check 
4.3.1 
Check that the unit switches on and off correctly. 
4.3.2 
Carry out a safety check of each part of the electrical equipment as appropriate. 
4.3.3 
Cables should be checked for exposed wires, cuts or scrapes - by running a clean colour coded 
cloth from the machine to the plug, if there are any faults then the machine should not be used 
but should be marked as “out of order” in line with the reporting procedure. 
4.3.4 
The cable should be neatly placed and should at no time be present as a trip hazard. 
4.3.5 
Filters should be clean and correctly fitted, if the filter is clogged it should be cleaned or 
changed. 
4.3.6 
The casing of the machine should be complete and in good working condition; the wheels free 
from debris and freely moving. 
4.3.7 
Ensure correct accessories are selected, pad drives and pads or brushes depending on the floor 
type. 
4.3.8 
Avoid standing on the cable at any point, this is primarily for the operator’s safety, it is possible 
that something sharp on the sole of a shoe could cut though the wire. 
4.3.9 
Cut out switches should be checked. 
 
4.4 Top-Down System 
4.4.1 
Preparation of Anti-Bacterial Solution (Oasis Pro 14) 
4.4.1.1 In a clean bucket, fill 2 litres of diluted anti-bacterial cleaning solution 
(This solution is diluted and dispensed from the Ecolab dispenser located in the 
housekeeping room). 
4.4.1.2 Place 20-25 blue and Red coloured microfibre flat mops inside the separate plastic box with 
lid and BLUE handle. 
4.4.1.3 Pour the diluted chemical solution into the plastic box over the chemical tray. 
4.4.1.4 Close the box with the lid and seal it properly. 
4.4.1.5 Turn the box upside down for pre-soaking (charging or impregnating) the mops for at least 
20-30 minutes. 
4.4.1.6 Place the box on the housekeeping cart. 
 
4.4.2 
Pre-charging of MFC Cloths in Anti-Bacterial Solution (Oasis Pro 14) 
4.4.2.1 Place folded blue MFC inside the bucket with blue coloured handle. 
4.4.2.2 Pour one litre of diluted anti-bacterial solution which was dispensed from the chemical 
dispenser. 
4.4.2.3 Pour evenly over the MFC and allow soaking time of 20 minutes to pre-charge 
(impregnating) the cloths. 
 
 
 
5. Job Steps

5.1 Patient Room Cleaning 
5.1.1 
Cleaning the In-Patient Room 
This cleaning procedure is carried out while patient admitted for treatment. 
The following cleaning steps are to be followed: 
5.1.1.1 
Seek approval from nursing in charge to clean the patient room. 
5.1.1.2 
Identify the work area risk access and place the safety signages (caution boards) outside 
the patient room. 
5.1.1.3 
Wheel the housekeeping trolley and waste collection trolley just outside the patient 
room, do not take the trolley(s) inside. 
5.1.1.4 
Knock the door and ask permission to enter inside the patient room. 
5.1.1.5 
Greet the patient or visitors and state your purpose for being there, ask for any 
preferences or special requests during regular cleaning. 
5.1.1.6 
Inform the patient that you will return at a more convenient time if the approached time 
is not convenient for them. 
5.1.1.7 
The patient room doors should remain closed while the cleaning is performed. 
5.1.1.8 
Commence the cleaning process by first removing any large 
debris on the floor using a dustpan and brush. 
5.1.1.9 
If any blood or body fluid spillage is observed on the floor, 
immediately clean it using the absorbent pads from the spill 
kit. Refer and follow CL 7242 IC in Housekeeping. 
5.1.1.10 Remove all waste from the room as per policy (Refer to CL 7249 IC in Management of 
Infectious Waste). Always start with domestic waste first followed by removal of 
infectious waste. 
(**infectious waste to be labelled with details) 
5.1.1.11 Place the waste bags in the waste collection trolley. Use the 
BLUE compartment for GENERAL waste and RED 
compartment for INFECTIOUS waste. 
5.1.1.12 After removal of waste from the patient room, commence 
the cleaning procedure by using a pre-charged blue MFC 
Charged in Oasis Pro 14 solution from the bucket with blue 
coloured handle. 
5.1.1.13 Clean all high touch areas/elements from top to bottom and 
clockwise or anti clockwise. 
5.1.1.14 Using a blue MFC, spray Oasis Pro 14 Solution from the spray 
bottle with blue nozzle onto the blue MFC, clean all surfaces 
(including frequently touch points), furniture and fixtures with 
Oasis Pro 14 chemical solution starting at the top using 
systematic passes, moving downwards. This includes all the 
frames, side tables, chairs, sofa, window curtain, sofa, garbage 
bins etc.

## Page 59

METHOD STATEMENT 
MS-CLE-HMC-26 
Patient Room Cleaning 
DATE 
5/15/2024 
REVISION 
V2.0 
 
Template version: 2.0 dated 01-Feb-2018 
 
Page 7 of 12 
 
5.1.1.15 Wipe the mattress frame using a blue MFC charged in Oasis Pro 
14 solution. Start from the top and move downwards towards 
the frame and base. 
 
 
5.1.1.16 Seek approval from the patient before disinfecting the bed rails 
and frames. 
 
 
5.1.1.17 Wipe down and clean the top and bottom of each removable 
component. 
 
 
 
5.1.1.18 Always wipe the area of beneath the removable platforms before 
the components are put back in place. 
 
5.1.1.19 Change the MFC regularly as and when required and dispose 
them in the laundry hamper. 
5.1.1.20 Using a blue MFC Charged in Oasis Pro 14 solution, wipe all 
doors, door frame, door handles, glass in doors, kick plates, 
cabinets, all furniture/fixtures and any other high touch elements 
including waste bins (general and infectious waste). 
5.1.1.21 After completion of all furniture and fixtures cleaning, move onto floor cleaning. 
5.1.1.22 Mop the floor using pre-charged blue coloured microfibre mops from the top-down 
system bucket with BLUE Handle. 
5.1.1.23 In a systematic way, mop the floor in a figure 8 shape, using continuous strokes 
overlapping passes to make sure that all the areas are effectively and efficiently cleaned. 
Pay particular attention to the area under the patient bed and work your way back 
towards the door ensuring that all the floor surfaces are covered. 
5.1.1.24 Remove the dirty mop and place it in the red coloured hamper of the service trolley. 
5.1.1.25 Check and replenish consumables. 
 
5.1.2 
Terminal Cleaning of Patient Room 
Terminal cleaning of the patient room is usually carried out at the end of the day or between 
patient discharges, subject to instruction from nursing in charge. 
The following cleaning steps are to be followed: 
5.1.2.1 
Identify the work area risk access and place the safety signages 
(caution boards) outside the patient room. 
5.1.2.2 
Wheel the housekeeping trolley and waste collection trolley just 
outside the patient room, do not take the trolley(s) inside. 
5.1.2.3 
Ensure all dirty/soiled linen have been removed by the nursing 
team prior to commencing cleaning. 
5.1.2.4 
The patient room doors should remain opened while the cleaning is performed.

5.1.2.5 
Remove any large debris on the floor using a dustpan and brush. 
5.1.2.6 
If any blood or body fluid spillage is observed on the floor, immediately clean it using the 
absorbent pads from the spill kit. Follow the HMC SPIL procedure. 
5.1.2.7 
In case of any small amount of blood or body fluid found on the floor, wall or any other 
fixtures, spot clean using a blue MFC Charged in chlorine disinfectant solution (Actichlor 
500 PPM). 
5.1.2.8 
Remove all waste from the room as per policy (refer to Cl 7249 IC in Management of 
Infectious Waste). Always start with domestic waste first followed by removal of infectious 
waste. 
5.1.2.9 
Place the waste bags in the waste collection trolley. Use the BLUE 
compartment for GENERAL waste and RED compartment for 
INFECTIOUS waste. 
5.1.2.10 Commence the cleaning process by starting from the ceiling. 
5.1.2.11 Using zebry pad with Oasis Pro 14 solution. 
5.1.2.12 Wipe the ceiling/ceiling tiles in systematic strokes with overlapping passes as to not miss 
any area. 
5.1.2.13 After completion of wiping the ceiling, remove the dirty zebry pad 
and place it in the blue coloured hamper of the service trolley. 
5.1.2.14 Commence the process of cleaning the walls using the zebry pad 
spray with Oasis Pro 14 solution. 
5.1.2.15 Wipe the walls moving the zebry pad from top to bottom. 
5.1.2.16 Once the wall cleaning is completed, remove the dirty zebry pad, and place it in the blue 
coloured hamper of the service trolley. 
5.1.2.17 Wipe the bed frame using a blue MFC charged in Oasis Pro 14 
solution. Start from the top and move downwards towards the 
frame and base. 
5.1.2.18 To effectively clean the bed frame, the attachments need to be 
disassembled and moved. 
5.1.2.19 Thoroughly inspect and disinfect the mattress including the sides 
and bottom. 
5.1.2.20 Wipe down and clean the top and bottom of each removable 
component. 
5.1.2.21 Always wipe the area of beneath the removable platforms before the components are put 
back in place. 
5.1.2.22 After cleaning the top surface of the bed frame, move towards the base and thoroughly 
wipe the underneath side of the bed frame. Continue wiping down till the legs and castor 
wheels. 
5.1.2.23 Change the MFC regularly as and when required and dispose them in the laundry hamper. 
 
5.1.2.24 Using a blue MFC Charged in Oasis Pro 14 solution, wipe all doors, 
door frame, door handles, glass in doors, kick plates, cabinets, all 
furniture/fixtures, and any other high touch elements including 
waste bins (general and infectious waste).

5.1.2.25 After completion of all furniture and fixtures cleaning, move onto floor cleaning. 
5.1.2.26 Mop the floor using pre-charged blue coloured microfibre mops from the top-down system 
bucket +with BLUE Handle. 
5.1.2.27 In a systematic way, mop the floor in a figure 8 shape, using continuous strokes overlapping 
passes to make sure that all the areas are effectively and efficiently cleaned. Pay particular 
attention to the area under the patient bed and work your way back towards the door 
ensuring that all the floor surfaces are covered. 
5.1.2.28 Remove the dirty mop and place it in the red coloured hamper of the service trolley. 
5.1.2.29 Prepare for Floor Scrubbing procedure. 
5.1.2.30 Move all movable furniture to one half of the patient room. 
5.1.2.31 Bring the Floor Scrubbing machine (single disc) and Wet & Dry vacuum inside the patient 
room. 
5.1.2.32 Place the red coloured pad to the pad holder in the bottom of the scrubbing machine. 
5.1.2.33 Fill 2 litres of anti-bacterial solution to the tank of the scrubbing machine. 
5.1.2.34 Connect the electrical power cable to the electrical socket. 
5.1.2.35 Commence the scrubbing process. Refer to the method statement for Floor Scrubbing (MS-
CLE-HMC-004) for further information. 
5.1.2.36 Start from the farthest corner of the room and run the machine in an overlapping pattern. 
5.1.2.37 Ensure that all areas of the floor on half side of the room is covered. 
5.1.2.38 Remove the chemical solution from the floor using a wet and dry vacuum. 
5.1.2.39 While removing the water from the floor, also mop the floor and allow the area to dry. 
5.1.2.40 After the floor is completely dry, move all furniture from the other half of the room to the 
scrubbed side of the room. 
5.1.2.41 Commence the scrubbing process and follow steps 5.1.2.39 to 5.1.2.41. 
5.1.2.42 After completion of the scrubbing and drying process, disinfect the floor by mopping the 
floor using pre-charged blue coloured mops in Pro 14 solution. 
5.1.2.43 In a systematic way, mop the floor in a figure 8 shape, using continuous strokes overlapping 
passes to make sure that all the areas are effectively and efficiently cleaned. Pay particular 
attention to the area under the patient bed and work your way back towards the door 
ensuring that all the floor surfaces are covered. 
5.1.2.44 Remove the dirty mop and place it in the red coloured hamper of the service trolley. 
5.1.2.45 Reinstate all furniture to their original position. 
5.1.2.46 Check and replenish consumables. 
 
5.2 Toilet Cleaning 
5.2.1 
Soak a White Coloured MFC in Oasis Pro 14 solution from the Blue bucket, wrap the MFC over 
an applicator wipe the ceiling tiles using systematic strokes followed by cleaning the walls from 
top to bottom with overlapping passes as not to miss any area.

5.2.2 
Remove all waste from the toilet as per policy (refer to Cl 7249 IC in Management of Infectious 
Waste) 
5.2.3 
Using the white coloured MFC, spray the Oasis Pro 14 Solution from the spray bottle with Red 
Nozzle onto the White MFC, clean the washbasin, faucet, mirror, soap dispenser, tissue 
dispenser, shower hose & head, shower rod, shower curtain etc. Start at the top using systematic 
passes and move downwards. 
5.2.4 
Cover the WC bowl and flush it before starting and then push water back in 
the WC using the WC brush. 
5.2.5 
Apply the Ecolab Maxx into WC2 chemical in the WC and then close the lid. 
(If available) 
5.2.6 
Start the cleaning process from Clean to Dirty using a red coloured 
disposable cloth and Oasis Pro14 All-Purpose cleaner (8 ml/ltr). 
5.2.7 
Special attention shall be given to sanitary appliances and high-touch areas 
such as toilet handles, rim, overflow, handrails, etc. 
5.2.8 
Clean external surface of the WC including seat cover and hidden areas of 
WC by using a red sponge and Oasis Pro 14 chemical. 
5.2.9 
Rinse with water after cleaning external surfaces and seat cover. 
5.2.10 Lift the WC seat cover, use the toilet brush to clean inside the WC and flush 
while WC bowl is closed. Use flush water to clean brush and return it to its 
container. 
5.2.11 Soak the white MFC in Pro 14 disinfectant solution wrap the MFC over an 
applicator wipe the ceiling tiles using systematic strokes followed by 
cleaning the walls from top to bottom with overlapping passes as not to miss any area. 
5.2.12 Using the white MFC, spray the Pro 14 disinfectant solution from the spray bottle with Red Nozzle 
onto the white MFC, clean the washbasin, faucet, mirror, soap dispenser, tissue dispenser, 
shower hose & head, shower rod, shower curtain etc. Start at the top using systematic passes 
and move downwards. 
5.2.13 Using a White MFC spray Pro 14 disinfectant solution from the spray bottle with red nozzle onto 
the MFC to clean all surfaces. 
5.2.14 Check and replenish consumables. 
5.2.15 Place fresh garbage bags inside the garbage bins. 
5.2.16 Using a Red microfiber Rapido mop, damp-mop the floor along the edges of areas lengthwise, 
using a figure-of eight stroke on the floor itself. 
5.2.17 Area mopped shall be left to dry and there shall be no obvious mop marks. 
5.2.18 Before leaving the toilet, clean and disinfect all equipment and tools with Pro 14 disinfectant 
solution. 
 
6. Health and safety – Points to remember 
6.1 
Always place the precautionary/warning signage near doors on both sides of the patient room. 
6.2 
Wear the right non-slip safety footwear for the job. 
6.3 
Stay in a comfortable working position, do not over-stretch to reach the task area rather than using 
long handled tools. 
6.4 
Use appropriate PPEs (Refer to HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
6.5 
To complete the job safely, follow the associated risk assessment.

6.6 
Follow the HMC guidelines with regards to COVID 19 Precautionary measures. 
6.7 
Perform a complete workplace 360 degrees check for any unusual conditions or new hazards and 
report them to your team leader right away. 
6.8 
Only use appropriate tools and equipment for the task. 
6.9 
While handling waste, always treat every bag of waste as if it could have a sharp object in it. 
6.10 Never compress the waste materials down into the waste bin with your hands. 
6.11 Always carry the waste bags from the top and away from your body. 
6.12 Always clean from top to bottom surfaces and clean to dirty areas. 
6.13 When cleaning a surface, clean from back to front or farthest away to closest. 
6.14 Comply with all HMC regulations for safety, hygiene, and infection control. 
6.15 Never leave any tool or material in the area unattended. 
6.16 As much as possible, keep the pathways clear of obstructions. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
7. Quality Verification & Post Job Check 
 
7.1 
After completion of the task, the appearance of area shall be clean and satisfactory. 
7.2 
Ensure all elements as per the Terminal Cleaning Checklist/patient room checklist are covered. 
7.3 
Supervisor to acknowledge submit a signed Terminal Cleaning Checklist to nursing department. 
7.4 
In case any furniture/equipment etc. are moved from the cleaning area, it shall be restored back to 
its original state (reinstate the area). 
7.5 
Ensure that the tools, signs, and barricading has been removed from the task location and safely take 
them back to the HK room/store and keep the area clear for user. 
7.6 
Double Check if there are no tools, materials left in the area. 
7.7 
Notify your team leader that the work is complete, and area is free of materials. 
7.8 
Remove your PPE (HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
7.9 
Wash your hands thoroughly after the task is completed (HMC Policies CL 7241 Hand Hygiene.) 
7.10 Did you notice any additional hazards during the work task that wasn’t mentioned in the Method 
Statement? (if yes, inform your team leader) 
7.11 Are there any faults with your tools and PPE that you must report? 
7.12 Report all Damages, Incidents & accidents / Health events to your team leader. 
7.13 Waste must be properly disposed as per SA-1054-Hazardous materials and waste management 
program.

4. Pre-Job Check 
4.1 Infection Control & HSEQ 
4.1.1 
Wash hands as per the approved method prior to commencing task. Check hands for any 
cuts and abrasions, if yes cover with waterproof dressing (Refer to HMC policies CL 7241 
Hand Hygiene Policy). 
4.1.2 
Wear appropriate PPE as per guidelines (Refer to HMC policies CL 7242 Infection Control 
Practices) 
4.1.3 
To Identify the work area and place the barricades and warning signs as needed. 
4.1.4 
Warning sign to be placed on both the entrance/exit doors. 
4.1.5 
Ensure that the work area is free from any hindrance or obstacles that could affect the 
Cleaning / Housekeeping operation. 
 
4.2 Tools & Equipment 
4.2.1 
Check & prepare all the equipment that may be required for the task. 
4.2.2 
Choose appropriate colour coded tools & equipment and ensure they are assembled safely. 
4.2.3 
Condition of tools – check if these are in clean, good working condition & safe to use. 
4.2.4 
Place the dust mop handles with their respective mops on the housekeeping trolley. 
4.2.5 
Ensure the trolley is well stocked with cleaning agents (chemicals), disposable cloths, mops, 
toilet rolls, interfold tissue packs, garbage bags (black and yellow) etc. before starting work 
in line with the relevant COSHH card. 
4.2.6 
Always check the preparation date of the diluted cleaning chemicals (Oasis Pro 14, Pro 41 
Glass Cleaner etc.) The usage of the chemical solution must be within 24 hours of the dilution 
process. 
4.2.7 
Fill a cleaning solution of 8 ml Oasis Pro 14 chemical to 1 litre of water ratio in a small bucket 
with BLUE handle and place it on top of the trolley/cart. 
(This solution is diluted and dispensed from the Ecolab dispenser located in the 
housekeeping room). 
4.2.8 
Floors are checked for any adhesive soiling, use a non-abrasive pad as required only.

## Page 69

METHOD STATEMENT 
MS-CLE-HMC-43 
ICU Cleaning

4/14/2024

Page 5 of 12 
 
4.3 Floor Scrubbing Machine & Wet/Dry Vacuum Machine Pre-Check 
4.3.1 
Check that the unit switches on and off correctly. 
4.3.2 
Carry out a safety check of each part of the electrical equipment as appropriate. 
4.3.3 
Cables should be checked for exposed wires, cuts or scrapes - by running a clean colour coded 
cloth from the machine to the plug, if there are any faults then the machine should not be 
used but should be marked as “out of order” in line with the reporting procedure. 
4.3.4 
The cable should be neatly placed and should at no time be present as a trip hazard. 
4.3.5 
Filters should be clean and correctly fitted, if the filter is clogged it should be cleaned or 
changed. 
4.3.6 
The casing of the machine should be complete and in good working condition; the wheels 
free from debris and freely moving. 
4.3.7 
Ensure correct accessories are selected, pad drives and pads or brushes depending on the 
floor type. 
4.3.8 
Avoid standing on the cable at any point, this is primarily for the operator’s safety, it is 
possible that something sharp on the sole of a shoe could cut though the wire. 
4.3.9 
Cut out switches should be checked. 
 
4.4 Top-Down System 
4.4.1 
Preparation of Anti-Bacterial Solution (Oasis Pro 14) 
4.4.1.1 In a clean bucket, fill 2 litres of diluted anti-bacterial cleaning solution. 
(This solution is diluted and dispensed from the Ecolab dispenser located in the 
housekeeping room). 
4.4.1.2 Place 20-25 yellow coloured microfibre flat mops inside the plastic box with lid and BLUE 
handle. 
4.4.1.3 Place the box on a clean pallet in the housekeeping room and pour the diluted chemical 
solution into the plastic box over the chemical tray. 
4.4.1.4 Close the box with the lid and seal it properly. 
4.4.1.5 Turn the box upside down for pre-soaking (charging or impregnating) the mops for at 
least 20-30 minutes. 
4.4.1.6 Place the box on the housekeeping cart. 
 
4.4.2 
Pre-charging of MFC Cloths in Anti-Bacterial Solution (Oasis Pro 14) 
4.4.2.1 Place folded Yellow MFC inside the bucket with blue coloured handle. 
4.4.2.2 Pour one litre of diluted anti-bacterial solution which was dispensed from the chemical 
dispenser. 
4.4.2.3 Pour evenly over the MFC and allow soaking time of 20 minutes to pre-charge 
(impregnating) the cloths.

5. Job Steps 
5.1 ICU Cleaning 
5.1.1 
Cleaning the ICU Room 
This cleaning procedure is carried out while patient admitted for treatment. 
The following cleaning steps are to be followed: 
5.1.1.1 Seek approval from nursing in charge to clean the ICU. 
5.1.1.2 Identify the work area risk access and place the safety signages (caution boards) outside 
the patient room. 
5.1.1.3 Wheel the housekeeping trolley and waste collection trolley just outside the icu, do not 
take the trolley(s) inside. 
5.1.1.4 Knock the door and ask permission to enter inside the ICU. 
5.1.1.5 Ensure all dirty/soiled linen have been removed by the nursing team prior to 
commencing cleaning. 
5.1.1.6 Commence the cleaning process by first removing any large debris on the floor using a 
dustpan and brush. 
5.1.1.7 If any blood or body fluid spillage is observed on the floor, immediately clean it using the 
absorbent pads from the spill kit. Refer and follow CL 7242 IC in Housekeeping. 
5.1.1.8 Remove all waste from the room as per policy (Refer to CL 7249 IC in Management of 
Infectious Waste). Always start with domestic waste first followed by removal of 
infectious waste. 
(**infectious waste to be labelled with details) 
5.1.1.9 Place the waste bags in the waste collection trolley. Use the BLUE 
compartment for GENERAL waste and RED compartment for 
INFECTIOUS waste. 
5.1.1.10 After removal of waste from the ICU, commence the cleaning 
procedure by using a pre-charged Yellow MFC Charged in Oasis Pro 
14 solution from the bucket. 
5.1.1.11 Clean all high touch areas/elements from top to bottom and 
clockwise or anti clockwise. 
5.1.1.12 Using a blue MFC, spray Oasis Pro 14 Solution from the spray bottle 
with blue nozzle onto the Yellow MFC, clean all surfaces (including 
frequently touch points), furniture and fixtures with Oasis Pro 14 
chemical solution starting at the top using systematic passes, 
moving downwards. This includes all the frames, side tables, chairs, 
sofa, window curtain, sofa, garbage bins etc. 
5.1.1.13 Wipe the mattress frame using a Yellow MFC charged in Oasis Pro 14 
solution. Start from the top and move downwards towards the 
frame and base. 
5.1.1.14 Wipe down and clean the top and bottom of each removable 
component. 
5.1.1.15 Always wipe the area of beneath the removable platforms before the 
components are put back in place.

## Page 71

5.1.1.16 Change the MFC regularly as and when required and dispose them in the laundry 
hamper. 
 
5.1.1.17 Using a Yellow Charged in Oasis Pro 14 solution, wipe all doors, 
door frame, door handles, glass in doors, kick plates, cabinets, all 
furniture/fixtures and any other high touch elements including 
waste bins (general and infectious waste). 
5.1.1.18 After completion of all furniture and fixtures cleaning, move onto 
floor cleaning. 
5.1.1.19 Mop the floor using pre-charged Yellow coloured microfibre mops 
from the top-down system bucket with BLUE Handle. 
5.1.1.20 In a systematic way, mop the floor in a figure 8 shape, using 
continuous strokes overlapping passes to make sure that all the 
areas are effectively and efficiently cleaned. Pay particular 
attention to the area under the patient bed and work your way 
back towards the door ensuring that all the floor surfaces are 
covered. 
 
5.1.1.21 Remove the dirty mop and place it in the red coloured hamper of 
the service trolley. 
 
 
5.1.1.22 Check and replenish consumables. 
 
 
5.1.2 
Cleaning the ICU Toilet 
5.1.2.1 Remove all waste from the toilet as per policy (refer to Cl 7249 IC in Management of 
Infectious Waste) 
5.1.2.2 Using the white coloured MFC, spray the Oasis Pro 14 Solution from the spray bottle 
with Red Nozzle onto the White MFC, clean the washbasin, faucet, mirror, soap 
dispenser, tissue dispenser, shower hose & head, shower rod, shower curtain etc. 
Start at the top using systematic passes and move downwards. 
5.1.2.3 Cover the WC bowl and flush it before starting and then push water back in the WC using 
the WC brush. 
5.1.2.4 Apply the Ecolab into WC chemical in the WC and then close the lid. (If available) 
5.1.2.5 Start the cleaning process from Clean to Dirty using a red coloured MFC and Oasis Pro14 
All-Purpose cleaner (8 ml/ltr). 
5.1.2.6 Special attention shall be given to sanitary appliances and high-touch areas such as toilet 
handles, rim, overflow, handrails, etc. 
5.1.2.7 Clean external surface of the WC including seat cover and hidden areas of WC by using a 
red sponge and Oasis Pro 14 chemical. 
5.1.2.8 Rinse with water after cleaning external surfaces and seat cover. 
5.1.2.9 Lift the WC seat cover, use the toilet brush to clean inside the WC and flush while WC 
bowl is closed. Use flush water to clean brush and return it to its container.

5.1.2.10 Using the white MFC, spray the Pro 14 disinfectant solution from the spray bottle with 
Red Nozzle onto the white MFC, clean the washbasin, faucet, mirror, soap dispenser, 
tissue dispenser, shower hose & head, shower rod, shower curtain etc. Start at the top 
using systematic passes and move downwards. 
5.1.2.11 Using a White MFC spray Pro 14 disinfectant solution from the spray bottle with red

5.1.2.12 Using a Red microfiber Rapido mop, damp-mop the floor along the edges of areas

5.1.2.13 Area mopped shall be left to dry and there shall be no obvious mop marks. 
5.1.2.14 Check and replenish consumables. 
5.1.2.15 Place fresh garbage bags inside the garbage bins. 
5.1.2.16 Before leaving the toilet, clean and disinfect all equipment and tools with Pro 14 
disinfectant solution. 
 
5.1.3 
Terminal Cleaning of ICU 
Terminal cleaning of the ICU is usually carried out at the end of the day or between patient 
discharges, subject to instruction from nursing in charge. 
The following cleaning steps are to be followed: 
5.1.3.1 Identify the work area risk access and place the safety signages (caution boards) outside 
the ICU. 
5.1.3.2 Wheel the housekeeping trolley and waste collection trolley just outside the ICU, do not 
take the trolley(s) inside. 
5.1.3.3 Ensure all dirty/soiled linen have been removed by the nursing team prior to commencing 
cleaning. 
5.1.3.4 The ICU doors should remain closed while the cleaning is performed. 
5.1.3.5 Remove any large debris on the floor using a dustpan and brush. 
5.1.3.6 If any blood or body fluid spillage is observed on the floor, immediately clean it using the 
absorbent pads from the spill kit. Follow the HMC SPIL procedure. 
5.1.3.7 In case of any small amount of blood or body fluid found on the floor, wall or any other 
fixtures, spot clean using a blue MFC Charged in chlorine disinfectant solution (Actichlor 
500 PPM). 
5.1.3.8 Remove all waste from the room as per policy (refer to Cl 7249 IC in Management of 
Infectious Waste). Always start with domestic waste first followed by removal of 
infectious waste. 
5.1.3.9 Place the waste bags in the waste collection trolley. Use the BLUE compartment for 
GENERAL waste and RED compartment for INFECTIOUS waste. 
5.1.3.10 Commence the cleaning process by starting from the ceiling. 
5.1.3.11 Charge the zebry pad in the Oasis Pro 14 solution using a spray bottle. 
5.1.3.12 Wipe the ceiling/ceiling tiles using the charged zebry pad in systematic strokes with 
overlapping passes to ensure no area is missed. 
5.1.3.13 After wiping the ceiling, remove the dirty zebry pad and place it in the blue-coloured 
hamper of the service trolley. 
5.1.3.14 Clean the walls using the charged zebry pad in Oasis Pro 14 solution. 
5.1.3.15 Wipe the walls from top to bottom using the zebry pad.
