5.1.3.16 After cleaning the walls, remove the dirty zebry pad and place it in the blue-coloured 
hamper of the service trolley. 
5.1.3.17 Wipe the bed frame using a Yellow MFC Charged in Oasis Pro 14 solution. Start from the 
top and move downwards towards the frame and base. 
5.1.3.18 To effectively clean the bed frame, the attachments need to be disassembled and moved. 
5.1.3.19 Thoroughly inspect and disinfect the mattress including the sides and bottom. 
5.1.3.20 Wipe down and clean the top and bottom of each removable component. 
5.1.3.21 Always wipe the area of beneath the removable platforms before the components are 
put back in place. 
5.1.3.22 After cleaning the top surface of the bed frame, move towards the base and thoroughly 
wipe the underneath side of the bed frame. Continue wiping down till the legs and 
castor wheels. 
5.1.3.23 Change the MFC regularly as and when required and dispose them in the laundry 
hamper. 
5.1.3.24 Using a Yellow MFC Charged in Oasis Pro 14 solution, wipe all doors, door frame, door 
handles, glass in doors, kick plates, cabinets, all furniture/fixtures and any other high 
touch elements including waste bins (general and infectious waste). 
5.1.3.25 After completion of all furniture and fixtures cleaning, move onto floor cleaning. 
5.1.3.26 Mop the floor using pre-charged Yellow coloured microfibre mops from the top-down 
system bucket. 
5.1.3.27 In a systematic way, mop the floor in a figure 8 shape, using continuous strokes 
overlapping passes to make sure that all the areas are effectively and efficiently 
cleaned. Pay particular attention to the area under the patient bed and work your way 
back towards the door ensuring that all the floor surfaces are covered. 
5.1.3.28 Remove the dirty mop and place it in the red coloured hamper of the service trolley. 
5.1.3.29 Prepare for Floor Scrubbing procedure. 
5.1.3.30 Move all movable furniture to one half of the ICU. 
5.1.3.31 Bring the Floor Scrubbing machine (single disc) and Wet & Dry vacuum inside the ICU. 
5.1.3.32 Place the red coloured pad to the pad holder in the bottom of the scrubbing machine. 
5.1.3.33 Fill 2 litres of anti-bacterial solution to the tank of the scrubbing machine. 
5.1.3.34 Connect the electrical power cable to the electrical socket. 
5.1.3.35 Commence the scrubbing process. Refer to the method statement for Floor Scrubbing 
(MS-CLE-HMC-004) for further information. 
5.1.3.36 Start from the farthest corner of the room and run the machine in an overlapping 
pattern. 
5.1.3.37 Ensure that all areas of the floor on half side of the room is covered. 
5.1.3.38 Remove the chemical solution from the floor using a wet and dry vacuum. 
5.1.3.39 While removing the water from the floor, also mop the floor and allow the area to dry. 
5.1.3.40 After the floor is completely dry, move all furniture from the other half of the room to 
the scrubbed side of the room. 
5.1.3.41 Commence the scrubbing process and follow steps 5.1.2.44 to 5.1.2.47 
5.1.3.42 After completion of the scrubbing and drying process, disinfect the floor by mopping the 
floor using pre-charged Yellow coloured mops in Pro 14 solution.

5.1.4.7 Special attention shall be given to sanitary appliances and high-touch areas such as toilet 
handles, rim, overflow, handrails, etc. 
5.1.4.8 Clean external surface of the WC including seat cover and hidden areas of WC by using a 
red sponge and Oasis Pro 14 chemical. 
5.1.4.9 Rinse with water after cleaning external surfaces and seat cover. 
5.1.4.10 Lift the WC seat cover, use the toilet brush to clean inside the WC and flush while WC bowl 
is closed. Use flush water to clean brush and return it to its container. 
5.1.4.11 Using the white MFC, spray the Pro 14 disinfectant solution from the spray bottle with Red 
Nozzle onto the white MFC, clean the washbasin, faucet, mirror, soap dispenser, tissue 
dispenser, shower hose & head, shower rod, shower curtain etc. Start at the top using 
systematic passes and move downwards. 
5.1.4.12 Using a White MFC spray Pro 14 disinfectant solution from the spray bottle with red nozzle 
onto the MFC to clean all surfaces. 
5.1.4.13 Check and replenish consumables. 
5.1.4.14 Place fresh garbage bags inside the garbage bins. 
5.1.4.15 Using a Red microfiber Rapido mop, damp-mop the floor along the edges of areas 
lengthwise, using a figure-of eight stroke on the floor itself. 
5.1.4.16 Area mopped shall be left to dry and there shall be no obvious mop marks. 
5.1.4.17 Before leaving the toilet, clean and disinfect all equipment and tools with Pro 14 
disinfectant solution.

6. Health and safety – Points to remember 
6.1 
Always place the precautionary/warning signage near doors on both sides of the ICU. 
6.2 
Wear the right non-slip safety footwear for the job. 
6.3 
Stay in a comfortable working position, do not over-stretch to reach the task area rather than using 
long handled tools. 
6.4 
Use appropriate PPEs (Refer to HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
6.5 
To complete the job safely, follow the associated risk assessment. 
6.6 
Follow the HMC guidelines with regards to COVID 19 Precautionary measures. 
6.7 
Perform a complete workplace 360 degrees check for any unusual conditions or new hazards and 
report them to your team leader right away. 
6.8 
Only use appropriate tools and equipment for the task. 
6.9 
While handling waste, always treat every bag of waste as if it could have a sharp object in it. 
6.10 Never compress the waste materials down into the waste bin with your hands. 
6.11 Always carry the waste bags from the top and away from your body. 
6.12 Always clean from top to bottom surfaces and clean to dirty areas. 
6.13 When cleaning a surface, clean from back to front or farthest away to closest. 
6.14 Comply with all HMC regulations for safety, hygiene, and infection control. 
6.15 Never leave any tool or material in the area unattended. 
6.16 As much as possible, keep the pathways clear of obstructions. 
 
7. Quality Verification & Post Job Check 
7.1 
After completion of the task, the appearance of area shall be clean and satisfactory. 
7.2 
Ensure all elements as per the Terminal Cleaning Checklist/patient room checklist are covered. 
7.3 
Supervisor to acknowledge submit a signed Terminal Cleaning Checklist to nursing department. 
7.4 
In case any furniture/equipment etc. are moved from the cleaning area, it shall be restored back 
to its original state (reinstate the area). 
7.5 
Ensure that the tools, signs, and barricading has been removed from the task location and safely 
take them back to the HK room/store and keep the area clear for user. 
7.6 
Double Check if there are no tools, materials left in the area. 
7.7 
Notify your team leader that the work is complete, and area is free of materials. 
7.8 
Remove your PPE (HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
7.9 
Wash your hands thoroughly after the task is completed (HMC Policies CL 7241 Hand Hygiene.) 
7.10 Did you notice any additional hazards during the work task that wasn’t mentioned in the Method 
Statement? (if yes, inform your team leader) 
7.11 Are there any faults with your tools and PPE that you must report? 
7.12 Report all Damages, Incidents & accidents / Health events to your team leader. 
7.13 Waste must be properly disposed as per SA-1054-Hazardous materials and waste management 
program.

4. Pre-Job Check 
4.1 Infection Control & HSEQ 
4.1.1 Wash hands as per the approved method prior to commencing task. Check hands for any cuts and abrasions, if 
yes cover with waterproof dressing (Refer to HMC policies CL 7241 Hand Hygiene Policy). 
4.1.2 Wear appropriate PPE as per guidelines (Refer to HMC policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices) 
4.1.3 PPE to be donned (worn) upon entry into the Ante room. 
4.1.4 Ensure you have been trained in RA, MS, SDS, COSHH and chemical handling. 
4.1.5 Ventilate the area where appropriate. 
 
4.2 Tools & Equipment 
4.2.1 Check & prepare all the equipment that may be required on service trolley to save wasted journeys. 
4.2.2 Ensure the trolley is well stocked with cleaning agents (chemicals), disposable cloths and mops, toilet rolls, 
interfold tissue packs, garbage bags (black and yellow) etc. before starting work in line with the relevant 
COSHH card. 
4.2.3 Choose appropriate colour coded tools & equipment and ensure they are assembled safely. 
4.2.4 Condition of tools – check if these are in clean, good working condition & safe to use. 
4.2.5 Always check the preparation date of the diluted cleaning chemicals (Oasis Pro 14, Pro 41 Glass Cleaner) The 
usage of the chemical solution must be within 24 hours of the dilution process. 
4.2.6 Prepare a cleaning solution of 8 ml Oasis Pro 14 chemical to 1 litre of water ratio in a small bucket with yellow 
handle and place it on top of the trolley/cart.

## Page 80

METHOD STATEMENT 
MS-CLE-HMC-18 
Isolation Room Terminal Cleaning 
(Droplet, Airborne & Protective Environment) 
DATE 
5/15/2024 
REVISION

Template version: 2.0 dated 01-Feb-2018 
 
Page 4 of 7 
 
4.2.7 Ensure the trolley is well stocked with cleaning agents (chemicals), microfibre dusters and pre-soaked microfibre 
mops as per colour code, toilet rolls, interfold tissue packs, garbage bags (black and yellow) etc. before starting 
work in line with the relevant COSHH card. 
4.2.8 Place all chemical spray bottles (Oasis Pro 14, Pro 41 Glass Cleaner), sponge, yellow and red MFC cloths and 
mops, applicator, etc. on a caddy tray. 
4.2.9 Wear appropriate PPE as per guidelines (Refer to CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
 
5. Job Steps 
5.1 Room Cleaning: 
5.1.1 Identify the work area risk access and place the safety sign (caution board) outside the 
isolation room. 
5.1.2 Wheel the trolley inside the ante room just outside the isolation room, do not take the 
trolley inside the isolation room. 
5.1.3 If Ante room is not available, park the housekeeping trolley in front of isolation room 
entrance door on the corridor. 
5.1.4 Carry the caddy tray along with two mop handles with yellow and red clips and place 
it in the Ante room. 
5.1.5 Ensure all dirty/soiled linen have been removed by the nursing team prior to 
commencing cleaning. 
5.1.6 Remove curtains from the room and place them in linen hamper with green bag lined 
with a water-soluble bag. 
5.1.7 Remove all waste from the room as per policy (Refer to CL 7249 IC in Management 
of Infectious Waste) 
5.1.8 Commence the cleaning process and use Oasis Pro 14 chemical solution & Using the 
Zebry pad and wipe the ceiling tiles using systematic strokes followed by cleaning 
the walls from top to bottom with overlapping passes as not to miss any area. 
 
 
5.1.9 Using the pre-soaked YELLOW coloured Charged MFC cloth in Oasis Pro 14 chemical 
solution, clean the bed in the room starting at the top using systematic passes, 
moving down to frame, not forgetting the base and wheels. 
5.1.10 Using a pre - soaked YELLOW coloured Charged MFC in Oasis Pro 14 Solution , clean 
all surfaces (including frequently touch points), furniture and fixtures starting at the 
top using systematic passes, moving downwards. This includes mattress cover, 
pillows, and garbage bins. Use 8 sides on each cloth before disposing. 
 
 
5.1.11 If there is a wash basin in the room, clean the sink area, faucet, splash board, hand 
soap dispenser, interfold tissue dispenser with the charged yellow MFC cloth using 
the Oasis Pro 14 solution. 
5.1.12 Check and replenish consumables. 
5.1.13 After allowing a contact time of 10 minutes, place fresh garbage bags inside the 
garbage bins.

## Page 81

METHOD STATEMENT 
MS-CLE-HMC-18

(Droplet, Airborne & Protective Environment)

Page 5 of 7 
 
5.1.14 Mop the floor using pre soaked yellow coloured Microfibre mops from the top-down 
system bucket. In a systematic way, mop the floor in a figure 8 shape, using continuous 
strokes overlapping passes to make sure that all the areas are effectively and 
efficiently cleaned. Pay particular attention to the area under the patient bed and work 
your way back towards the door ensuring that all the floor surfaces are covered. 
5.1.15 Area mopped shall be left to dry and there shall be no obvious mop marks. 
5.1.16 Refer to MS-CLE-HMC-006 – Damp Moping for damp moping steps. 
5.1.17 Before leaving the room, clean and disinfect all equipment and tools with chlorine-based solution (Actichlor 
1000 PPM) 
 
5.2 Toilet Cleaning: 
5.2.1 Use Oasis Pro 14 solution Chemicals & using with zebry pad and wipe the ceiling tiles 
using systematic strokes followed by cleaning the walls from top to bottom with 
overlapping passes as not to miss any area. 
5.2.2 Remove all waste from the toilet as per policy (Refer to CL 7249 IC in Management of 
Infectious Waste) 
5.2.3 Using the pre soaked white coloured MFC cloth in Oasis Pro 14 Solution, clean the washbasin, faucet, mirror, 
soap dispenser, tissue dispenser, shower hose & head, shower rod, shower curtain etc. Start at the top using 
systematic passes and move downwards. 
5.2.4 Cover the WC bowl and flush it before starting and then push water back in the WC using 
the WC brush. 
5.2.5 Apply the Ecolab max into WC2 chemical in the WC and then close the lid (If available). 
5.2.6 Start the cleaning process from Clean to Dirty using a red coloured MFC cloth and Oasis 
Pro14 All-Purpose cleaner (8 ml/ltr). 
5.2.7 Special attention shall be given to sanitary appliances and high-touch areas such as 
toilet handles, rim, overflow, handrails, etc. 
5.2.8 Clean external surface of the WC including seat cover and hidden areas of WC by using 
a red MFC cloth and Oasis Pro 14 chemical. 
5.2.9 Rinse with water after cleaning external surfaces and seat cover. 
5.2.10 Lift the WC seat cover, use the toilet brush to clean inside the WC and flush while WC 
bowl is closed. Use flush water to clean brush and return it to its container. 
5.2.11 Using the pre charged RED coloured MFC cloth, clean the toilet brush and garbage 
bins. 
5.2.12 Place fresh garbage bags inside the garbage bins. 
5.2.13 Using a Red microfiber Rapido mop, damp-mop the floor along the edges of areas lengthwise, using a figure-of 
eight stroke on the floor itself. 
5.2.14 Area mopped shall be left to dry and there shall be no obvious mop marks. 
5.2.15 Refer to MS-CLE-HMC-006 – Damp Moping for damp moping steps. 
5.2.16 Before leaving the toilet, clean and disinfect all equipment and tools with chlorine-based solution. (Actichlor 
1000 PPM) 
5.2.17 Check and replenish consumables.

5.3 Regular Room Cleaning for Candida Auris Patient: 
5.3.1 Last room to clean 
5.3.2 Cleaning materials to be separated 
5.3.3 
Cleaning with Pro 14 Plus (8ml of chemical in 1 liter of water) 
5.3.4 Disinfect with Actichlor (1 Actichlor tablet in 1 liter of water) 
5.3.5 High touch surfaces shall be cleaned with Sporicidal Wipes 
5.4 Terminal Room Cleaning for Candida Auris Patient: 
 5.4.1 Last room to clean. 
5.4.2 
 Cleaning materials to be separated. 
5.4.3 
 Cleaning with Pro 14 Plus (8ml of chemical in 1 liter of water) 
5.4.4 
 Disinfect with Actichlor (10 Actichlor tablet in 1 liter of water) 
5.4.5 
 High touch surfaces will be cleaned by Sporicidal Wipe. 
5.4.6 
 Consumables items and curtains to be disposed. 
 5.4.7 Fumigation process to be done. 
 5.4.8 Terminal cleaning to be performed 3 times – 2 times before fumigation and 1 time after fumigation. 
 
6 
Health and Safety – Points to Remember 
6.4 Always place the precautionary/warning signage 
6.5 Stay in a comfortable working position, do not over-stretch to reach the task area, use long handled tools instead. 
6.6 Handle all chemicals safely according to COSHH / SDS. 
6.7 Use appropriate PPEs (refer to HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
6.8 Avoid physical contact with chemical (specially with eyes) 
6.9 To complete the job safely, follow the associated risk assessment. 
6.10 Follow the HMC guidelines with regards to COVID 19 Precautionary measures. 
6.11 Perform a complete workplace 360 degrees check for any unusual conditions or new hazards and report them to 
your team leader right away. 
6.12 Only use appropriate tools and equipment for the task. 
6.13 Comply with all HMC regulations for safety, hygiene, and infection control. 
6.14 Never leave any tool or material in the area unattended. 
6.15 As much as possible, keep the pathways clear of obstructions. 
6.16 Wait until the floor is entirely dry before removing the barricades, and then double-check that the floor is dry and 
clean. 
 
 
 
 
7 
Quality Verification & Post Job Check 
7.4 Visually check the isolation room and the toilet cleaned satisfactory. 
7.5 Ensure the floor completely dried and ready for next use. 
7.6 Warning signs, tools, equipment are checked, cleaned, disinfected, and removed as necessary. 
7.7 Remove PPE (Refer to CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). PPE to be removed and discarded before exiting the 
room. 
7.8 Wash your hands thoroughly (following hand hygiene policy). 
7.9 Did you notice any hazards during the work task that wasn’t mentioned in the Method Statement? (If yes, inform 
your supervisor) 
7.10 Double Check there are no tools, materials left in the area.

5.2.3 
Using the white coloured disposable cloth, pre – soaking in Oasis Pro 14, clean the washbasin, 
faucet, mirror, soap dispenser, tissue dispenser, shower hose & head, shower rod, shower 
curtain etc. Start at the top using systematic passes and move downwards. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
5.2.4 
Cover the WC bowl and flush it before starting and then push water 
back in the WC using the WC brush. 
 
5.2.5 
Apply the Ecolab Maxx into WC2 chemical in the WC and then close 
the lid (If available). 
 
 
 
 
5.2.6 
Start the cleaning process from Clean to Dirty using a red coloured 
disposable cloth and 14 plus antibacterial All-Purpose cleaner (8 
ml/ltr). 
 
 
5.2.7 
Special attention shall be given to sanitary appliances and high-touch 
areas such as toilet handles, handrails, etc. 
 
 
 
5.2.8 
Clean external surface of the WC including seat cover and hidden 
areas of WC by using a red sponge and 14 plus antibacterial chemical.

## Page 90

5.2.9 
Rinse with water after cleaning external surfaces and seat cover. 
 
 
5.2.10 Dispose the sponge after usage. 
 
5.2.11 Lift the WC seat cover, use the toilet brush to clean inside the WC and 
flush while WC bowl is closed. Use flush water to clean brush and 
return it to its container. 
 
5.2.12 Using the pre-soaked RED coloured disposable cloth, 14 plus antibacterial Solution clean the 
toilet brush and garbage bins. 
5.2.13 All surfaces, fixtures and floors shall be disinfected with 0.1% (1000ppm) chlorine-based 
disinfectant 
5.2.14 Check and replenish consumables. 
 
5.2.15 Place fresh garbage bags inside the garbage bins. 
 
5.2.16 Using a disposable mop, damp-mop the floor along the edges of areas lengthwise, using a 
figure-of eight stroke on the floor itself. 
5.2.17 Area mopped shall be left to dry and there shall be no obvious mop marks. 
5.2.18 Refer to MS-CLE-HMC-006 – Damp Moping for damp moping steps. 
5.2.19 Before leaving the toilet, clean and disinfect all equipment and tools with chlorine-based 
solution. (Actichlor 1000 PPM) 
 
 
6. Health and Safety – Points to Remember 
6.1 Always place the precautionary/warning signage 
6.2 Stay in a comfortable working position, do not over-stretch to reach the task area, use long handled 
tools instead. 
6.3 Handle all chemicals safely according to COSHH / SDS. 
6.4 Use appropriate PPEs (refer to HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
6.5 Avoid physical contact with chemical (specially with eyes) 
6.6 To complete the job safely, follow the associated risk assessment. 
6.7 Follow the HMC guidelines with regards to COVID 19 Precautionary measures. 
6.8 Perform a complete workplace 360 degrees check for any unusual conditions or new hazards and 
report them to your team leader right away. 
6.9 Only use appropriate tools and equipment for the task. 
6.10 Comply with all HMC regulations for safety, hygiene, and infection control. 
6.11 Never leave any tool or material in the area unattended. 
6.12 As much as possible, keep the pathways clear of obstructions. 
6.13 Wait until the floor is entirely dry before removing the barricades, and then double-check that the 
floor is dry and clean.

7. Quality Verification & Post Job Check 
 
7.1 Visually check the isolation room and the toilet cleaned satisfactory. 
7.2 Ensure the floor completely dried and ready for next use. 
7.3 Warning signs, tools, equipment are checked, cleaned, disinfected, and removed as necessary. 
7.4 Remove PPE (Refer to CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). PPE to be removed and discarded before 
exiting the room. 
7.5 Wash your hands thoroughly (following hand hygiene policy). 
7.6 Did you notice any hazards during the work task that wasn’t mentioned in the Method Statement? 
(If yes, inform your supervisor) 
7.7 Double Check there are no tools, materials left in the area. 
7.8 Wash your hands thoroughly (following hand hygiene policy). 
7.9 One last check to ensure room is ready and all equipment has been returned to correct position. 
7.10 Notify your supervisor that the work is complete, and area is free of materials.

4. Pre-Job Check 
4.1 Infection Control & HSEQ 
4.1.1 
Wash hands as per the approved method prior to commencing task. Check hands for any cuts and 
abrasions, if yes cover with waterproof dressing (Refer to HMC policies CL 7241 Hand Hygiene 
Policy). 
4.1.2 
Wear appropriate PPE as per guidelines (Refer to HMC policies CL 7242 Infection Control 
Practices) 
4.1.3 
To Identify the work area and place the barricades and warning signs as needed. 
4.1.4 
Warning sign to be placed on both the entrance/exit doors. 
4.1.5 
Inspect the area thoroughly if found any maintenance or damages, informed to the concern 
person. 
4.1.6 
Ensure that the work area is free from any hindrance or obstacles that could affect the Cleaning / 
Housekeeping operation. 
 
4.2 Tools & Equipment 
4.2.1 
Check & prepare all the equipment that may be required for the task. 
4.2.2 
Choose appropriate colour coded tools & equipment and ensure they are assembled safely. 
4.2.3 
Condition of tools – check if these are in clean, good working condition & safe to use. 
4.2.4 
Place the dust mop handles with their respective mops on the housekeeping trolley. 
4.2.5 
Ensure the trolley is well stocked with cleaning agents (chemicals), disposable cloths, mops, 
interfold tissue packs, garbage bags (black and yellow) etc. before starting work in line with the 
relevant COSHH card. 
4.2.6 
Always check the preparation date of the diluted cleaning chemicals (Oasis Pro 14, Pro 41 Glass 
Cleaner, Actichlor Solution etc.) The usage of the chemical solution must be within 24 hours of the 
dilution process. 
4.2.7 
Prepare a 1000 ppm of chlorine-based disinfectant solution (1 tablet of Actichlor to 1 litre of water) 
in a small bucket with RED handle and place it on top of the trolley/cart. 
4.2.8 
Prepare a cleaning solution of 8 ml Oasis Pro 14 chemical to 1 litre of water ratio in a small bucket 
with BLUE handle and place it on top of the trolley/cart. (needed clarification of charging or 
spraying) 
4.2.9 
Floors are checked for any adhesive soiling, use a non-abrasive pad as required only. 
 
4.3 Floor Scrubbing Machine & Wet/Dry Vacuum Machine Pre-Check 
4.3.3 
Check that the unit switches on and off correctly. 
4.3.4 
Carry out a safety check of each part of the electrical equipment as appropriate. 
4.3.5 
Cables should be checked for exposed wires, cuts or scrapes - by running a clean colour coded cloth 
from the machine to the plug, if there are any faults then the machine should not be used but 
should be marked as “out of order” in line with the reporting procedure. 
4.3.6 
The cable should be neatly placed and should at no time be present as a trip hazard. 
4.3.7 
Filters should be clean and correctly fitted, if the filter is clogged it should be cleaned or changed. 
4.3.8 
The casing of the machine should be complete and in good working condition; the wheels free 
from debris and freely moving.

4.3.9 
Ensure correct accessories are selected, pad drives and pads or brushes depending on the floor 
type. 
4.3.10 
Avoid standing on the cable at any point, this is primarily for the operator’s safety, it is possible 
that something sharp on the sole of a shoe could cut though the wire. 
4.3.11 
Cut out switches should be checked. 
 
4.4 Top-Down System 
4.4.1 
Preparation of Anti-Bacterial Solution (Oasis Pro 14) 
4.4.1.1 In a clean bucket, prepare 2 litres of diluted anti-bacterial cleaning solution. 
**High Risk Areas – 16 ml of Ecolab Oasis Pro 14 chemical and 2 litres of water. 
(This solution is diluted and dispensed from the Ecolab dispenser located in the housekeeping 
room). 
4.4.1.2 Place 20-25 yellow coloured microfibre flat mops inside the plastic box with lid and BLUE 
handle. 
4.4.1.3 Place the box on a clean pallet in the housekeeping room and pour the diluted chemical solution 
into the plastic box over the chemical tray. 
4.4.1.4 Close the box with the lid and seal it properly. 
4.4.1.5 Turn the box upside down for pre-soaking (charging or impregnating) the mops for at least 20-
30 minutes. 
4.4.1.6 Place the box on the housekeeping cart. 
 
4.4.2 
Preparation of Chlorine Disinfectant Solution (Actichlor) 
4.4.2.1 In a clean bucket, add 2 Actichlor chlorine tablets to 2 litres of clean water to prepare a chlorine 
disinfectant solution (1000 ppm). 
4.4.2.2 Place 20-25 yellow coloured microfibre flat mops inside the plastic box with lid and RED handle. 
4.4.2.3 Place the box on a clean pallet in the housekeeping room and pour the diluted chlorine 
disinfectant solution into the plastic box over the chemical tray. 
4.4.2.4 Close the box with the lid and seal it properly. 
4.4.2.5 Turn the box upside down for pre-soaking (charging or impregnating) the mops for at least 20-
30 minutes. 
4.4.2.6 Place the box on the housekeeping cart. 
 
5. Job Steps 
5.1 Operation Room Cleaning 
5.1.1 
Cleaning the Operation Room Between Cases 
This cleaning procedure is carried out between 2 surgeries (after completion of one surgery and 
before commencement of the other). This type of cleaning is carried out only after recommendation 
from Surgical team. 
 
The following cleaning steps are to be followed: 
5.1.1.1 Identify the work area risk access and place the safety signages (caution boards) outside the 
operation room. Ensure a signage is near both doors of the operation room.

## Page 97

METHOD STATEMENT 
MS-CLE-HMC-24 
Operation Room Cleaning 
DATE 
5/15/2024 
REVISION 
V2.0 
 
Template version: 2.0 dated 01-Feb-2018 
 
Page 6 of 10 
 
5.1.1.2 Wheel the housekeeping trolley and waste collection trolley just 
outside the operation room, do not take the trolley(s) inside. 
** Remember – both these trolleys are dedicated only for the 
surgical department and MUST NOT be used in any other areas of 
the hospital. 
5.1.1.3 Ensure all dirty/soiled linen have been removed by the nursing team prior to commencing 
cleaning. 
5.1.1.4 The operating room doors should remain closed while the cleaning is performed. 
5.1.1.5 Commence the cleaning process by first removing any large debris on the floor using a dustpan 
and brush. 
5.1.1.6 If any blood or body fluid spillage is observed on the floor, immediately clean it using the 
absorbent pads from the spill kit. Follow the HMC SPIL procedure. 
5.1.1.7 Remove all waste from the room as per policy (refer to Cl 7249 IC in Management of Infectious 
Waste). Always start with domestic waste first followed by 
removal of infectious waste. 
5.1.1.8 Place the waste bags in the waste collection trolley. Use the BLUE 
compartment for GENERAL waste and RED compartment for 
INFECTIOUS waste. 
5.1.1.9 After removal of waste from the operation room, commence the 
cleaning procedure by charging a YELLOW coloured MFC cloth in Oasis Pro 14 solution from 
the blue bucket. 
5.1.1.10 Clean all high touch areas/elements starting from the ceiling mounted pendants arms, light 
handles etc. Start from the farthest point from the light and wipe down all surfaces with 
Oasis Pro 14 solution. 
5.1.1.11 Pivot the light fixture and continue wiping the top surface of the light fixture. 
5.1.1.12 Use sporicidal wipes provided by the surgical team to wipe the light reflector surface. Allow a 
contact time of 3-5 minutes to dry. 
5.1.1.13 Wipe the operation table using a charged MFC in Oasis Pro 14 
solution. Start from the top and move downwards towards the 
frame and base. 
5.1.1.14 To effectively clean the operating table, the attachments need to 
be disassembled and moved. 
5.1.1.15 Thoroughly inspect and disinfect the table pad including the sides and bottom of the pad. 
**Ensure not to stack a clean pad on a surface that has not yet been cleaned. 
5.1.1.16 Wipe down and clean the top and bottom of each removable component. 
5.1.1.17 Always wipe the area of beneath the removable platforms before the components are put 
back in place. 
5.1.1.18 After cleaning the top surface of the operating table, move towards the base and thoroughly 
wipe the underneath side of the table. Continue wiping down till the legs and castor wheels. 
5.1.1.19 Change the MFC cloth when its visibly soiled. 
5.1.1.20 Using a charged MFC in Oasis Pro 14 solution, wipe all doors, door 
frame, door handles, glass in doors, kick plates, cabinets, all 
furniture/fixtures and any other high touch elements including 
waste bins (general and infectious waste).

## Page 98

MS-CLE-HMC-24

Page 7 of 10 
 
5.1.1.21 After completion of all furniture and fixtures cleaning, move onto floor cleaning. 
5.1.1.22 Mop the floor using pre-charged yellow coloured microfibre mops 
from the top-down system. 
5.1.1.23 In a systematic way, mop the floor in a figure 8 shape, using 
continuous strokes overlapping passes to make sure that all the 
areas are effectively and efficiently cleaned. Pay particular attention 
to the area under the operating table and work your way back towards the door ensuring 
that all the floor surfaces are covered. 
5.1.1.24 Remove the dirty mop and place it in the red coloured hamper of the service trolley. 
 
5.1.2 
Terminal Cleaning of Operation Room 
Terminal cleaning of the operation room is usually carried out at the end of the day or between 
cases, subject to instruction from surgical team. 
The following cleaning steps are to be followed: 
5.1.2.1 Identify the work area risk access and place the safety signages (caution boards) outside the 
operation room. Ensure a signage is near both doors of the operation room. 
5.1.2.2 Wheel the housekeeping trolley and waste collection trolley just outside the operation room, 
do not take the trolley(s) inside. 
5.1.2.3 ** Remember – both these trolleys are dedicated only for the surgical department and MUST 
NOT be used in any other areas of the hospital. 
5.1.2.4 Ensure all dirty/soiled linen have been removed by the nursing team prior to commencing 
cleaning. 
5.1.2.5 The operating room doors should remain closed while the cleaning is performed. 
5.1.2.6 Remove any large debris on the floor using a dustpan and brush. 
5.1.2.7 If any blood or body fluid spillage is observed on the floor, immediately clean it using the 
absorbent pads from the spill kit. Follow the HMC SPILL procedure. 
5.1.2.8 In case of any small amount of blood or body fluid found on the floor, wall or any other fixtures, 
spot clean using a disposable cloth charged in Oasis Pro 14 solution. 
5.1.2.9 Remove all waste from the room as per policy (refer to Cl 7249 IC in Management of Infectious 
Waste). Always start with domestic waste first followed by removal of infectious waste. 
5.1.2.10 Place the waste bags in the waste collection trolley. Use the BLUE compartment for GENERAL 
waste and RED compartment for INFECTIOUS waste. 
5.1.2.11 Commence the cleaning process by starting from the ceiling. 
5.1.2.12 Using a zebry pad, Wipe the ceiling/ceiling tiles in systematic strokes 
with overlapping passes as to not miss any area. 
5.1.2.13 After completion of wiping the ceiling, remove the dirty MFC and 
place it in the blue coloured hamper of the service trolley. 
5.1.2.14 Commence the process of cleaning the walls using the zebry pad with Oasis Pro 14 solution. 
5.1.2.15 Wipe the walls moving the MFC from top to bottom. 
5.1.2.16 Once the wall cleaning is completed, remove the zebry pad and place it in the blue coloured 
hamper of the service trolley. 
5.1.2.17 Clean all high touch areas/elements starting from the ceiling mounted pendants arms, light 
handles etc. Start from the farthest point from the light and wipe down all surfaces with Oasis 
Pro 14 solution.

5.1.2.18 Pivot the light fixture and continue wiping the top surface of the light fixture. 
5.1.2.19 Wipe the operation table using a charged MFC in Oasis Pro 14 solution. Start from the top and 
move downwards towards the frame and base. 
5.1.2.20 To effectively clean the operating table, the attachments need to be disassembled and moved. 
5.1.2.21 Thoroughly inspect and disinfect the table pad including the sides and bottom of the pad. 
**Ensure not to stack a clean pad on a surface that has not yet been cleaned. 
5.1.2.22 Wipe down and clean the top and bottom of each removable component. 
5.1.2.23 Always wipe the area of beneath the removable platforms before the components are put back 
in place. 
5.1.2.24 After cleaning the top surface of the operating table, move towards the base and thoroughly 
wipe the underneath side of the table. Continue wiping down till the legs and castor wheels. 
5.1.2.25 Change the MFC cloth as when it is visibly dirty. 
5.1.2.26 Using a charged MFC, wipe all doors, door frame, door handles, glass in doors, kick plates, 
cabinets, all furniture/fixtures and any other high touch elements including waste bins 
(general and infectious waste). 
5.1.2.27 Continue using charged MFC cloth to clean worktables and chairs. 
5.1.2.28 Repeat the process 5.1.2.20 to 5.1.2.31 using Actichlor Chlorine solution to disinfect all 
surfaces. 
5.1.2.29 After completion of all furniture and fixtures cleaning, move onto floor cleaning. 
5.1.2.30 Mop the floor using pre-charged yellow coloured microfibre mops from the top-down system 
with BLUE Handle. 
5.1.2.31 Prepare for Floor Scrubbing procedure. 
5.1.2.32 Move all movable furniture to one half of the operation room. 
5.1.2.33 Bring the Floor Scrubbing machine (single disc) and Wet & Dry vacuum inside the operation 
room. 
5.1.2.34 Place the red coloured pad to the pad holder in the bottom of the scrubbing machine. 
5.1.2.35 Fill 2 litres of anti-bacterial solution to the tank of the scrubbing machine. 
5.1.2.36 Connect the electrical power cable to the electrical socket. 
5.1.2.37 Commence the scrubbing process. Refer to the method statement for Floor Scrubbing (MS-
CLE-HMC-004) for further information. 
5.1.2.38 Start from the farthest corner of the room and run the machine in an overlapping pattern. 
5.1.2.39 Ensure that all areas of the floor on half side of the room is covered. 
5.1.2.40 Remove the chemical solution from the floor using a wet and dry vacuum. 
5.1.2.41 Rinse the floor with the water and remove the water from the floor using a wet and dry 
vacuum. 
5.1.2.42 While removing the water from the floor, also mop the floor and allow the area to dry. 
5.1.2.43 After the floor is completely dry, move all furniture from the other half of the room to the 
scrubbed side of the room. 
5.1.2.44 Commence the scrubbing process and follow steps 5.1.2.44 to 5.1.2.47. 
5.1.2.45 After completion of the scrubbing and drying process, disinfect the floor by mopping the floor 
using pre-charged yellow coloured microfibre mops charged in Actichlor solution. 
5.1.2.46 In a systematic way, mop the floor in a figure 8 shape, using continuous strokes overlapping 
passes to make sure that all the areas are effectively and efficiently cleaned. Pay particular

attention to the area under the operating table and work your way back towards the door 
ensuring that all the floor surfaces are covered. 
5.1.2.47 Remove the dirty mop and place it in the red coloured hamper of the service trolley. 
5.1.2.48 Reinstate all furniture to their original position. 
 
5.2 Scrub Area Cleaning 
5.2.1 
Apply Pro 14 solution on SS Sink, surgical scrub dispenser, splash guard, 
under the sink and tissue dispenser. 
5.2.2 
Allow a contact time of 5-10 minutes. 
5.2.3 
Scrub the SS Sink with yellow coloured sponge and rinse it with water. 
5.2.4 
Wipe the scrub sink using systematic strokes from top to bottom with 
overlapping passes as not to miss any area. 
5.2.5 
Disinfect the scrub sink area by wiping in systematic strokes from top to bottom with overlapping 
passes as not to miss any area. 
5.2.6 
Clean all areas thoroughly including wash basin, faucet, surgical scrub dispenser, splash guard, 
under the sink and tissue dispenser. 
5.2.7 
After completion of cleaning and disinfection, remove and discard gloves and perform hand hygiene 
as per HMC Policies CL 7241 Hand Hygiene. 
5.2.8 
 Replenish consumables such as Tissue, Surgical Scrub etc. 
 
 
6. Health and safety – Points to remember 
6.1 
Always place the precautionary/warning signage near doors on both sides of the operation room. 
6.2 
Wear the right non-slip safety footwear for the job. 
6.3 
Stay in a comfortable working position, do not over-stretch to reach the task area rather than using long 
handled tools. 
6.4 
Use appropriate PPEs (refer to HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
6.5 
To complete the job safely, follow the associated risk assessment. 
6.6 
Follow the HMC guidelines with regards to COVID 19 Precautionary measures. 
6.7 
Perform a complete workplace 360 degrees check for any unusual conditions or new hazards and report 
them to your team leader right away. 
6.8 
Only use appropriate tools and equipment for the task. 
6.9 
While handling waste, always treat every bag of waste as if it could have a sharp object in it. 
6.10 Never compress the waste materials down into the waste bin with your hands. 
6.11 Always carry the waste bags from the top and away from your body. 
6.12 Always clean from top to bottom surfaces and clean to dirty areas. 
6.13 When cleaning a surface, clean from back to front or farthest away to closest. 
6.14 Comply with all HMC regulations for safety, hygiene, and infection control. 
6.15 Never leave any tool or material in the area unattended. 
6.16 As much as possible, keep the pathways clear of obstructions. 
 
 
7. Quality Verification & Post Job Check 
7.1 
After completion of the task, the appearance of area shall be clean and satisfactory. 
7.2 
Ensure all elements as per the Terminal Cleaning Checklist are covered.

7.3 
Supervisor to acknowledge submit a signed Terminal Cleaning Checklist to surgical department. 
7.4 
In case any furniture/equipment etc. are moved from the cleaning area, it shall be restored back to its

7.5 
Ensure that the tools, signs, and barricading has been removed from the task location and safely take

Remove your PPE (HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices).

Wash your hands thoroughly after the task is completed (HMC Policies CL 7241 Hand Hygiene.)

Statement? (if yes, inform your team leader) 
7.11 Are there any faults with your tools and PPE that you must report?

1.8 To use correct PPE for the job as per the HMC policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices.

4. Pre-Job Check 
 
4.1 Infection Control & HSEQ 
4.1.1 
Wash hands as per the approved method prior to commencing task. Check hands for any cuts and 
abrasions, if required, ensure that it is covered with a waterproof dressing (Refer to HMC policies 
CL 7241 Hand Hygiene Policy). 
4.1.2 
Take the appropriate PPE, check before wearing as per the guidelines (Refer to HMC policies CL 7242 
Infection Control Practices). 
4.1.3 
Refer to HMC policies SA 1054_ HAZMAT & Waste management Program. 
4.1.4 
Ensure that you have received chemical handling and RA, MS, SDS, and COSHH training. 
4.1.5 
Identify the work area assess the risk access and place the warning sign. For corridor or an open 
space work area such as patient waiting area etc., ensure to place caution sign in both sides to notify 
the public and the staff. 
4.1.6 
Ventilate the area where appropriate. 
 
4.2 Tools, Materials & Equipment 
4.2.1 
Check and prepare the required spillage kit. 
4.2.2 
Check & prepare all the equipment required for the task. 
4.2.3 
Choose appropriate colour coded tools & equipment and ensure to assemble safely. 
4.2.4 
Condition of tools – check if these are in clean, good working condition & safe to use. 
4.2.5 
Check & prepare all the equipment and materials required on service trolley to save wasted 
journeys. 
 
4.3 Chemical Dilution & Usage 
4.3.1 
It is vital to always verify that the cleaning chemicals being utilized are specifically intended for the 
given activity. (Oasis Pro 14, Pro 41, Actichlor solution etc.) 
4.3.2 
Ensure that the chemicals spray bottles are all properly labelled with details such as dilution/expiry 
date & time. 
4.3.3 
Chemicals spray bottles nozzle must be colour coded according to the risk area. 
4.3.4 
Diluted chemical solution is dispensed from the Automatic Chemical Dispensing machine located in 
the housekeeping rooms. The chemical dispensing machine is pre-calibrated to dispense the 
chemical solution as per HMC Infection Control guidelines.

4.3.5 
The dilution ratio for Oasis Pro 14 and Pro 41 is 8ml chemical for 1 ltr water and 1:1000 ppm for 
Actichlor (1 Actichlor tablet added to 1 ltr water). 
4.3.6 
Always check the preparation date of the diluted cleaning chemicals. The usage of the chemical 
solution must be within 24 hours of the dilution process. 
4.3.7 
Ensure you have received training on chemical handling, RA, MS, SDS, and COSHH. 
4.3.8 
Follow the Proper dilution of the cleaning agents and should be referred to manufacturer’s 
recommendation and HMC-IPC guidelines. 
 
4.4 Spill Response – Guidelines in Assessing the Risk Involved 
4.1.1 
In the event of the spill, ensure to maintain your safety and safety of all personnel. 
4.1.2 
Evaluate the potential hazards as certain chemical spills may not be as dangerous as others. 
4.1.3 
Hydraulic oil will not create the same breathing hazards as concentrated acid, but acid will be less of 
a fire hazard than gasoline. 
4.1.4 
Those familiar with the chemicals in their workplace will have less difficulty identifying risks. 
4.1.5 
Risk includes not only the obvious direct risks to spill responders but also the risks to other 
equipment and materials in the area. 
4.1.6 
Acid spills are more dangerous in the presence of oxidisable materials such as oils and fuels. 
4.1.7 
When in doubt, leave the spill response to someone who knows what to do.

5.1 TYPE 1 SPILLAGE CLEAN UP – Body Fluids e.g., Blood, Vomit, Urine, etc. 
5.1.1 
Prepare all equipment required for the task. 
5.1.2 
Place cleaning in progress sign/barriers within ½ metre of spillage. 
5.1.3 
Collect a Type 1 – Biohazard Body Fluid Spillage Kit from the Janitorial Trolley or from the cleaning 
room. 
5.1.4 
Put on all Personal Protective Equipment (PPE) provided in the Type 1 Spillage Kit. 
5.1.5 
Check for any needles and sharps, carefully dispose, and secure those in a sharp’s container. If 
found, immediately inform the nurse in charge to record and the take necessary action. 
5.1.6 
Whenever possible, ensure that there is a proper ventilation in the work area. 
5.1.7 
Using the absorbent granules, sprinkle over the entire affected area, taking care not to touch the 
spillage with bare skin. 
5.1.8 
Allow the spill to turn gelatinous, then using the scoop and scraper, place detritus into the 
biohazard bag. 
5.1.9 
Continue the process until all the detritus are collected and cleared. 
5.1.10 Spray the area with disinfectant and leave for 2-3 minutes. Clean the area with dry wipes, then 
place bottle and wipes into the biohazard bag. 
5.1.11 Remove the apron and gloves and place them into the biohazard bag. 
5.1.12 Clean your hands thoroughly with the cleansing wipe, then put the used wipe into the biohazard 
bag, seal, and dispose of appropriately in the yellow bin at specified locations. 
5.1.13 Once completed, remove, clean, and disinfect all the cleaning equipment and caution signs, placing 
items back to the trolley or in storeroom. 
5.1.14 Hard floor areas to be cleaned thoroughly right after the spillage clean up; for guidance, refer to 
the method statements MS-CLE-HMC- 06 Damp Mopping, MS-CLE-HMC- 04 Floor Scrubbing with 
Machine and MS-CLE-HMC- 02 External Areas Floor Washing & Scrubbing as appropriate. 
5.1.15 For soft floor or carpet, affected area to be cleaned thoroughly right after the spillage clean up, 
refer to the method statement of MS-CLE-HMC- 21 Carpet Shampooing. 
5.1.16 Inspect the spill kit and notify the supervisor if any supplies need to be replenished. 
5.1.17 Wash your hands thoroughly as per (HMC Policies CL 7241 Hand Hygiene. 
 
5.2 TYPE 2 SPILLAGE CLEAN UP – Chemicals & Vehicle Oils 
5.2.1 
Collect a Type 2 “Spill Control Kit” from the cleaning room and caution signs. 
5.2.2 
Proceed to the spillage, place cleaning in progress sign within two metres of the spillage.

## Page 109

METHOD STATEMENT 
MS-CLE-HMC-42 
SPILLAGE CLEAN UP

REVISION

Page 8 of 10 
 
5.2.3 
Risk assesses the area & when in doubt, leave the spill response to someone who knows what to 
do and immediately inform the Supervisor. 
5.2.4 
Wear appropriate Personal Protection Equipment (PPE) before contact with any hazardous 
substances (refer to SDS). 
5.2.5 
If it is safe to do so, begin to contain the spread of the spill. Stop the source of the spill to prevent 
its growth. Tip up drums or shut off valves. If drains are at risk, seal them off with a Neoprene 
Drain Mat. Prevent the spill from spreading by surrounding it with socks and booms. 
5.2.6 
If the spill is of a highly reactive chemical, such as an acid or caustic, neutralise the chemical 
activity of the liquid to enhance worker safety and reduce the costs and dangers associated with 
handling the disposing of these substances. 
5.2.7 
Whether applying neutralisers or absorbing the spill; always work from the outside to the inside. 
Sorbent socks and booms have considerable capacity by themselves. Push sorbents along the 
ground towards the centre of the spill. If the spill is larger than the sorbent capacity of the socks 
and booms that surround it, use sorbent pillows or sheet to absorb the remaining liquid. 
5.2.8 
Ensure no residue remains and if needed, wash the area of the spill. 
5.2.9 
Dispose the spill according to its nature. Collect all waste residues and contaminated absorbents 
into a suitable container and label, identifying liquid and characteristics of the liquid absorbed. 
5.2.10 Exercise care when overseeing contaminated absorbents. 
5.2.11 For hard floors, thorough cleaning to be done on the affected area right after the completion of 
the spillage clean up, refer to the method statement of MS-CLE-HMC- 06 Damp Mopping, MS-CLE-
HMC- 04 Floor Scrubbing with Machine and MS-CLE-HMC- 02 External Areas Floor Washing & 
Scrubbing as appropriate. 
5.2.12 For soft floors or carpets, thorough cleaning to be done on the affected area right after the 
completion of the spillage clean up, refer to the method statement of MS-CLE-HMC- 21 Carpet 
Shampooing as appropriate. 
5.2.13 Inspect the spill kit and notify the supervisor if any supplies need to be replenished. 
5.2.14 Once completed, remove, clean, and disinfect all the cleaning equipment and caution signs, placing 
items back to the trolley or in storeroom. 
5.2.15 Wash your hands thoroughly as per (HMC Policies CL 7241 Hand Hygiene.) 
 
6. Health and safety – Points to remember 
6.11 
Always place the precautionary/warning signage. 
6.12 
Stay in a comfortable working position, do not over-stretch to reach the task area, use long 
handled tools instead.

7.19 
Are there any faults with your tools and PPE that you must report? 
7.110 Staff to Report to his / her team leader for any unusual situation or any new hazard that need to

7.111 Report all damages, incidents & accidents / health events to your team leader. 
7.112 OTL / Supervisor to make a note or report of what happened to cause the spill, what actions were

4. Pre-Job Check 
 
4.1 Infection Control & HSEQ 
4.1.1 
Wash hands according to the approved method before starting the task. Check for any cuts 
or abrasions on hands; if present, cover them with waterproof dressing (Refer to HMC 
policies CL 7241 Hand Hygiene Policy). 
4.1.2 
Wear appropriate PPE as per guidelines (Refer to HMC policies CL 7242 Infection Control 
Practices). 
4.1.3 
Identify the work area and display warning signs as necessary. 
4.1.4 
Place warning signs on both entrance and exit doors. 
4.1.5 
Ensure the work area is clear of hindrances or obstacles that could affect the cleaning or 
housekeeping operation. 
4.2 Tools & Equipment 
4.2.1 
Check and prepare all equipment required for the task. 
4.2.2 
Ensure tools are made of approved anti-magnetic material, verified by an MRI competent 
team member. 
4.2.3 
Select appropriate color-coded tools and equipment and assemble them safely. 
4.2.4 
Check the condition of tools for cleanliness, proper functionality, and safety. 
4.2.5 
Place dust mop handles with their respective mops on the housekeeping trolley. 
4.2.6 
Ensure the trolley is stocked with cleaning agents (chemicals), MFCs, MFM's, garbage bags 
(black and yellow), etc. 
4.2.7 
Always check the preparation date of diluted cleaning chemicals (Oasis Pro 14, Pro 41 Glass 
Cleaner, etc.). The chemical solution must be used within 24 hours of dilution. 
 
4.3 Top-Down System 
 
4.4.1 
Preparation of Anti-Bacterial Solution (Oasis Pro 14) 
4.4.1.1 In a clean bucket, fill 2 litres of diluted anti-bacterial cleaning solution (This solution is 
diluted and dispensed from the Ecolab dispenser located in the housekeeping room). 
4.4.1.2 Place 20-25 blue-coloured microfiber flat mops inside the plastic box with a BLUE handle. 
4.4.1.3 Place the box on a clean pallet in the housekeeping room and pour the diluted chemical 
solution into the plastic box over the chemical tray. 
4.4.1.4 Close the box with the lid and seal it properly. 
4.4.1.5 Turn the box upside down for pre-soaking (charging or impregnating) the mops for at 
least 20-30 minutes. 
4.4.1.6 Place the box on the housekeeping cart.

4.4.2 
Pre-charging of MFC Cloths in Anti-Bacterial Solution (Oasis Pro 14) 
4.4.2.1 Place folded blue MFC inside the bucket with a blue-coloured handle. 
4.4.2.2 Pour one liter of diluted anti-bacterial solution dispensed from the chemical dispenser. 
4.4.2.3 Pour evenly over the MFC and allow soaking time of 20 minutes to pre-charge 
(impregnating) the cloths. 
 
5. Job Steps 
 
5.1 Seek approval from the nursing in charge to clean the MRI room. 
5.2 Janitors or supervisors with pacemakers, neurostimulators, or metallic implants must not enter the MRI 
room. 
5.3 No loose metal objects are allowed inside the MRI room. 
5.4 Identify the work area, assess risks, and place caution signage (caution boards) outside the MRI room. 
5.5 Wheel the housekeeping trolley and waste collection trolley just outside the MRI reception; do not take 
the trolleys inside. 
5.6 Log in the janitor’s checklist. 
5.7 Start the cleaning process by removing any large debris on the floor using a dustpan and brush. 
5.8 Remove all waste from the room as per policy (SA-1054-Hazardous materials and waste management 
program & CL 7249 IC in Management of Infectious Waste). Always start with domestic waste first, 
followed by removal of infectious waste. (Infectious waste should be labelled with details.) 
5.9 Place the waste bags in the waste collection trolley. Use the BLUE compartment for GENERAL waste and 
the RED compartment for INFECTIOUS waste. 
5.10 Begin the cleaning process by starting from the ceiling. 
5.11 Charge the blue zebry pad in the Oasis Pro 14 solution using a spray bottle. 
5.12 Wipe the ceiling/ceiling tiles using the charged blue zebry pad in systematic strokes with overlapping 
passes to ensure no area is missed. 
5.13 After wiping the ceiling, remove the dirty zebry pad and place it in the blue-colored hamper of the 
service trolley. 
5.14 Clean the walls using the charged blue zebry pad in Oasis Pro 14 solution. 
5.15 Wipe the walls from top to bottom using the zebry pad. 
5.16 After cleaning the walls, remove the dirty zebry pad and place it in the blue-colored hamper of the 
service trolley. 
5.17 Clean all surfaces, furniture, and fixtures with a pre-charged blue MFC soaked in Oasis Pro 14 solution 
or by spraying Oasis Pro 14 Solution from the spray bottle onto the blue MFC. Start at the top and work 
downwards systematically. This includes all doors and frames, cupboard tops, domestic garbage bins, 
wall-mounted A/C grills, etc. 
5.18 Use another blue MFC charged with Oasis Pro 41 solution to clean the window glass and glass panels. 
5.19 Clean all high-touch areas/elements from top to bottom and in a clockwise or anti-clockwise manner. 
5.20 Change the MFC regularly as needed and dispose of them in the laundry hamper. 
5.21 Use a blue MFC soaked in Oasis Pro 14 solution to wipe all doors, door frames, door handles, glass in 
doors, kick plates, cabinets, all furniture/fixtures, and any other high-touch elements. 
5.22 After cleaning all furniture and fixtures, proceed to dust mopping. 
5.23 Dust mop the floor using a blue-coloured microfiber dust mop.

## Page 116

5.24 Start dust mopping adjacent to the skirting board, sweeping parallel to the skirting boards. 
5.25 Sweep systematically, starting from the far end corner of the room and coming back in an "S" type 
action without lifting the mop during sweeping. 
5.26 After dust mopping, proceed to floor cleaning. 
5.27 Mop the floor using pre-charged, blue-coloured microfiber mops from the top-down system 
bucket with a BLUE Handle. 
5.28 Mop the floor in a systematic manner, using continuous strokes overlapping passes in a figure 8 shape, 
ensuring all areas are effectively cleaned and work your way back towards the door to cover all floor 
surfaces. 
5.29 Remove the dirty mop and place it in the red-coloured hamper of the service trolley. 
5.30 Log out of the janitor’s checklist. 
5.31 Remove the caution signs only when the floor is completely dried. 
5.32 Notify the nursing team that the MRI room is ready for the next patient. 
5.33 Terminal Cleaning After Isolation Patient Visit – Contact/Droplet/Airborne Isolation - Follow MS-CLE-
HMC-18 - Isolation Room Terminal Cleaning. 
 
6. Health and safety – Points to remember 
6.1 Always place the precautionary/warning signage near doors on both sides of the MRI room. 
6.2 Wear the right non-slip safety footwear for the job. 
6.3 Stay in a comfortable working position, do not over-stretch to reach the task area rather than using 
long handled tools. 
6.4 Use appropriate PPEs (Refer to HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
6.5 To complete the job safely, follow the associated risk assessment. 
6.6 Perform a complete workplace 360 degrees check for any unusual conditions or new hazards and 
report them to your team leader right away. 
6.7 Only use appropriate tools and equipment for the task. 
6.8 While handling waste, always treat every bag of waste as if it could have a sharp object in it. 
6.9 Never compress the waste materials down into the waste bin with your hands. 
6.10 Always carry the waste bags from the top and away from your body. 
6.11 Always clean from top to bottom surfaces and clean to dirty areas. 
6.12 When cleaning a surface, clean from back to front or farthest away to closest. 
6.13 Comply with all HMC regulations for safety, hygiene, and infection control. 
6.14 Never leave any tool or material in the area unattended. 
6.15 As much as possible, keep the pathways clear of obstructions.

7. Quality Verification & Post Job Check 
 
7.1 After completing the task, ensure that the area's appearance is clean and satisfactory. 
7.2 Confirm that all elements outlined in the MRI room checklist have been addressed. 
7.3 If any furniture/equipment was moved during cleaning, ensure it is restored to its original state 
(reinstate the area). 
7.4 Remove tools, signs, and barricading from the task location and safely return them to the HK 
room/store, keeping the area clear for users. 
7.5 Double-check that no tools or materials are left in the area. 
7.6 Notify your team leader that the work is complete, and the area is free of materials. 
7.7 Remove your PPE according to HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices. 
7.8 Thoroughly wash your hands after completing the task as per HMC Policies CL 7241 Hand Hygiene. 
7.9 Did you notice any additional hazards during the work task that were not mentioned in the Method 
Statement? If yes, inform your team leader. 
7.10 Report any faults with your tools and PPE that you must report. 
7.11 Report all damages, incidents, accidents, or health events to your team leader. 
7.12 Ensure waste is properly disposed of according to SA-1054-Hazardous materials and waste 
management program.

4. Pre-Job Check 
 
4.1 Infection Control & HSEQ 
4.1.1 
Wash hands as per the approved method prior to commencing task. Check hands for any cuts and 
abrasions, if yes cover with waterproof dressing (Refer to HMC policies CL 7241 Hand Hygiene 
Policy). 
4.1.2 
Wear appropriate PPE as per guidelines (Refer to HMC policies CL 7242 Infection Control in 
Housekeeping Department) 
4.1.3 
Ensure you have been trained in RA, MS, SDS, COSHH and chemical handling. 
4.1.4 
To Identify the work area and place the warning signs as needed. 
4.1.5 
Ensure that the work area is free from any hindrance or obstacles that could affect the cleaning / 
Housekeeping operation. 
 
4.2 Tools & Equipment 
4.2.1 
Check & prepare all the equipment that may be required for the task. 
4.2.2 
Choose appropriate colour coded tools & equipment and ensure they are assembled safely. 
4.2.3 
Condition of tools – check if these are in clean, good working condition & safe to use. 
4.2.4 
Ensure the chemicals spray bottles are properly labelled with details such as dilution /expiry date 
& time. 
4.2.5 
Always check the preparation date of the diluted cleaning chemicals (Oasis Pro 14) usage of the 
chemical solution must be within 24 hours of the dilution process. 
4.2.6 
Any damage to tools, equipment or a reportable incident should be reported to the team leader. 
 
 
5. Job Steps 
 
5.1 Cleaning of Tools 
5.1.1 
Collect all used cleaning materials in the Housekeeping Trolley. 
 
5.1.2 
At the end of the shift, take the Housekeeping Trolley to the housekeeping room. 
 
 
5.1.3 
Clean all the equipment, mops, handles, buckets, containers etc. using MFC 
sprayed with Oasis Pro 14 Plus. 
 
 
5.1.4 
All cleaning materials from the trolley must be cleaned, disinfected, and left 
to dry.

## Page 121

METHOD STATEMENT 
MS-CLE-HMC-15 
Cleaning and Disinfection of Work Materials 
DATE 
5/13/2024 
REVISION 
V2.0 
 
Template version: 2.0 dated 01-Feb-2018 
 
Page 4 of 6 
 
5.1.5 
Thoroughly clean the housekeeping trolley including the base, wheels etc. 
and leave to dry. 
 
 
5.1.6 
After drying, all cleaned tools and materials to be placed on the trolley. 
 
 
5.1.7 
All consumables such as paper, plastic, hand soap etc. to be replenished in 
the housekeeping trolley. 
 
5.1.8 
The housekeeping trolley must be set up and kept ready for the next shift. 
5.1.9 
The janitor handing over the shift must fill in the trolley cleaning checklist 
and ensure that all elements of the checklist are completed. 
5.1.10 The janitor taking over the shift must verify the checklist and counter sign 
the document after checking that the Housekeeping Trolley is ready for use. 
5.1.11 For isolation rooms, clean all equipment, tools and housekeeping trolley 
inside the Ante room with Oasis Pro 14 and disinfect with Actichlor solution 
at 1000 ppm (1 tablet in 1 litre of water) 
5.1.12 In the absence of an Ante room, the equipment shall be cleaned outside the 
patient room, and placed in the dedicated corner of the housekeeping room 
and labelled as “Isolation Room”. 
5.1.13 Toilet bowl brush should be cleaned and disinfected inside the toilet cubicle. 
5.1.14 Rinse, drain and leave material to dry for next use. 
5.1.15 The buckets should be kept flipped for complete drying. 
 
5.2 Cleaning Micro Fibre Cloth (MFC)/Micro Fibre Mop (MFM) 
5.2.1 
After usage, all used MFC’s and MFM’s to be collected in the trolley and 
transported to the disposal hold within each department. 
5.2.2 
If MFC’s and MFM’s visibly soiled with body fluid, then it shall be placed in a 
water-soluble bag for laundering. 
5.2.3 
At the end of each shift, all MFC’s and MFM’s must be transported to the 
Housekeeping Laundry room for cleaning. 
5.2.4 
Place the MFC’s and MFM’s in the washing machine separately according to 
their colour coding. 
5.2.5 
Ensure to never use bleach or use fabric softener for MFC/MFM. 
5.2.6 
Only trained operators handle washing/drier machine operations. 
5.2.7 
The temperature must be set to 60-90 degrees Celsius for the washing and 
drying cycles. 
5.2.8 
Add adequate quantity of washing liquid into the washing machine detergent 
slot based on the load of MFC’s and MFM’s to be washed. 
5.2.9 
Close the door of washing machine and press start for the washing process. 
5.2.10 After washing cycle, transfer MFC’s / MFM’s to the drier machine with the 
help of a clean plastic container.

5.2.11 Select appropriate mode and press start button. 
5.2.12 Once drying cycle is completed, transfer the dried MFC/MFM to the assigned storage area for next 
use with the help of a dry plastic container. 
5.2.13 All material should be folded, covered in a dust bag and stored in a clean and dry area to avoid 
growth of micro-organisms. 
 
 
6. Health and Safety – Points to Remember 
 
6.1 
Always place the precautionary/warning signage. 
6.2 
Stay in a comfortable working position, do not over-stretch to reach the task area, use long 
handled tools instead. 
6.3 
Handle all chemicals safely according to COSHH / SDS. 
6.4 
Use appropriate PPEs as per (HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control in Housekeeping 
Department). 
6.5 
Avoid physical contact with chemical (specially with eyes). 
6.6 
To complete the job safely, follow the associated risk assessment. 
6.7 
Follow the HMC guidelines with regards to COVID 19 Precautionary measures. 
6.8 
Perform a complete workplace 360 degrees check for any unusual conditions or new hazards and 
report them to your team leader right away. 
6.9 
Use the appropriate tools for the task. 
6.10 
Staff to follow TBT for slip, trip & fall. 
6.11 
Always follow your superior's guidelines when performing a task. 
6.12 
Comply with all HMC regulations for safety, good hygiene, and infection control. 
6.13 
Never leave any tool or material in the area unattended. 
6.14 
As much as possible, keep the pathways clear of obstructions. 
 
 
7. Quality Verification & Post Job Check 
 
7.1 
Visually check the tools, equipment and materials cleaned & disinfected effectively. 
7.2 
Discarded or dis coloured MFC/MFM must be disposed as appropriate. 
7.3 
Check the issuance date of MFC/MFM on the fabric to track and whenever it hits the 200 days 
mark, the MFC/MFM must be discarded. 
7.4 
Damaged tools must be disposed as per the quality standards. 
7.5 
Ensure that the tools, signs, and barricading has been removed from the task location and safely 
take them back to the HK room/store and keep the area clear for user. 
7.6 
Double Check if there are no tools, materials left in the area. 
7.7 
Notify your team leader that the work is complete, and area is free of materials. 
7.8 
Remove your PPE (HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control in Housekeeping Department). 
7.9 
Wash your hands thoroughly after the task is completed (HMC Policies CL 7241 Hand Hygiene.)

5. Job Steps 
 
5.1 Place the warning sign “Cleaning in progress” at the appropriate area and visible for public. 
5.2 Using a clean MFC, wipe the area of the steel surface to remove dust and finger marks that adhered 
to the steel surface. 
5.3 Clean and disinfect the steel surfaces by spraying Oasis Pro 14 chemical solution into an MFC and apply 
it on the surface. 
5.4 Remove the excess chemical from the surface by using a squeegee. Ensure that no chemical is left over 
on the surface. 
 
 
5.5 Apply steel polish chemical into a sponge and gently rub it into the steel 
surface in a manageable size. 
 
 
 
5.6 Application process should be carried out in a systematic manner 
(vertical or horizontal overlapping passes). 
 
 
 
 
5.7 Remove the chemical residue from the surface by using a squeegee. 
Ensure that no chemical is left over on the surface and allow the 
surface to dry.

4. Pre-Job Check 
 
4.1 Issuance & Control 
4.1.1 The waste management team will collect the needle resistant gloves from the Housekeeping Store 
in their respective project. 
4.1.2 A receipt note should be signed by the staff while receiving the needle resistant gloves from the 
store. 
4.1.3 Needle resistant gloves assigned only for a dedicated waste handler allocated in each location. 
4.1.4 Supervisor to conduct a fit test of the gloves for all waste management staff and staff to check the 
condition of the gloves prior to receipt. 
4.1.5 The assigned employee is responsible to wash and disinfect his needle resistant gloves at end of each 
shift. 
4.1.6 By regularly washing your gloves, longevity of use is highly increased – keeping you and your team 
safer, longer.

## Page 131

METHOD STATEMENT 
MS-CLE-HMC-23 
Usage and Cleaning of Needle Resistant Gloves 
DATE 
5/16/2024 
REVISION 
V2.0 
 
Template version: 2.0 dated 01-Feb-2018 
 
Page 3 of 7 
 
4.2 Infection Control & HSEQ 
4.2.1 Wash hands as per the approved method prior to commencing task. Check hands for any cuts and 
abrasions, if yes cover with waterproof dressing (Refer to HMC policies CL 7241 Hand Hygiene 
Policy). 
4.2.2 Wear appropriate PPE as per guidelines (Refer to HMC policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices) 
4.2.3 Ensure you have been trained in RA, MS, SDS, COSHH and chemical handling. 
4.2.4 Ensure that the work area is free from any hindrance or obstacles that could affect the Housekeeping 
/ Waste Management operations. 
 
5. Job Steps 
 
 
5.1 Donning of Needle Resistant Gloves 
5.1.1 
It is applicable to only authorized FMM Waste Management Team to 
have control measures in place. 
 
 
5.1.2 
Wear latex disposable gloves first, then needle resistant gloves above 
that, this will help to avoid getting the hand sweat on the needle 
resistant gloves from inside, as well as any unpleasant odour 
(optional). 
 
 
5.1.3 
Tighten the wrist strap after all fingers and palm are comfortably 
placed inside the glove. 
 
 
 
5.2 Usage of Needle Resistant Gloves 
5.2.1 
Ensure that you are wearing Needle Resistant Gloves before entering the waste disposal area to 
transfer the waste (general and medical waste) into appropriate skip deployed in the disposal 
area. (Domestic waste skips and 660L medical waste bin) 
5.2.2 
Always hold the waste bag from the top and away from the body. 
5.2.3 
Do not touch the bag or compress it from the sides, bottom or top. 
5.2.4 
When transferring medical waste into the 660L bins in the infectious waste holding area 
(basement) and general waste into the compactor, always be very careful to keep the bag away 
from the body and it is safely transferred directly into the bin/compactor. 
5.2.5 
NRG not required in department waste holding area/dirty utility as the waste transferring 
happening only in waste disposal area at basement. Once the 240L domestic/Medical bin filled 
with 75% (3/4) waste, the same bin transported to waste disposal area located at basement. 
5.2.6 
NRG required to use only when transferring the waste from waste collection trolley to disposal 
area waste skips/bins. 
5.2.7 
NRG should not be used while transporting the waste from waste holding area till disposal area. 
 
5.3 Doffing of Needle Resistant Gloves 
5.3.1 
Doffing of needle resistant gloves after transferring the waste from the 
waste collection trolley to the waste disposal area skips/Bins. 
5.3.2 
Loosen the wrist strap and remove Needle Resistant Gloves.

## Page 132

METHOD STATEMENT 
MS-CLE-HMC-23 
Usage and Cleaning of Needle Resistant Gloves 
DATE 
5/16/2024 
REVISION 
V2.0 
 
Template version: 2.0 dated 01-Feb-2018 
 
Page 4 of 7 
 
5.3.3 
Remove and dispose the disposable gloves and follow HMC Policies CL 7241- Hand Hygiene. 
5.3.4 
At the end of shift, staff should take the gloves to the FMM designated laundry on site to launder 
the gloves. 
 
5.4 Disinfection of Needle Resistant Gloves 
5.4.1 
After completion of waste disposal, go to the nearest housekeeping room assigned for NRG 
disinfection and storage purpose. (Nearest to the waste disposal area - domestic and Medical 
waste). 
5.4.2 
Remove the Needlestick Resistant Gloves (NRG) and Continue to wear vinyl or nitrile gloves when 
disinfecting the NRG. 
 
5.4.3 
Disinfect NRG with 14 plus antibacterial all-purpose cleaner and maintain 
contact time of 5 mins. 
 
 
 
5.4.4 
Wipe the whole portion of the used gloves after 5 minutes and let it dry. 
 
 
5.4.5 
Disinfected NRG shall be placed in a new resealable bag for next use. 
 
 
5.4.6 
Resealable bags should be labelled with staff name to identify their own 
used NRG throughout the shift. 
 
 
5.4.7 
Dispose inner vinyl or nitrile gloves and perform hand hygiene. 
 
 
5.4.8 
DO NOT share NRG with other user unless gloves are washed and dried. 
5.4.9 
Do not spray any form of bleach on NRG, it can damage the gloves easily. e.g., Actichlor, chlorine- 
based disinfectant. 
 
 
5.5 Washing & Storage of Needle Resistant Gloves 
5.5.1 
Machine wash the Needle Resistant Gloves during end of the shift. While 
cleaning with washing machine, it is recommended cool wash with a mild 
laundry detergent and low tumble dry. (Do NOT use bleach and iron). 
 
 
 
5.5.2 
Hang indoors for a complete dry after low tumble dry (If required). 
Do not iron.

## Page 133

METHOD STATEMENT 
MS-CLE-HMC-23 
Usage and Cleaning of Needle Resistant Gloves 
DATE 
5/16/2024 
REVISION 
V2.0 
 
Template version: 2.0 dated 01-Feb-2018 
 
Page 5 of 7 
 
5.5.3 
Needle Resistant Gloves should be stored in clean, dry conditions, away from 
direct sunlight and extreme temperatures. 
 
 
5.5.4 
Clean laundered gloves shall then be placed on new resealable bags in the 
assigned housekeeping room at basement (inside NRG storage cupboard) 
for staff use. 
 
 
5.6 Routine Checks of Needle Resistant Gloves 
5.6.1 
Glove life varies depending on the application, environment, and amount of use. It is vital that you 
perform routine glove checks before beginning work every day. 
5.6.2 
Take note of areas that have begun to wear down, such as loose Velcro or a worn-down name tag. 
If you see holes in the synthetic leather or TP-X® material on the palm of your glove, this is an 
indication that its protective qualities may be compromised, putting you at risk of injury. Lingering 
moisture or a strong odour are also signing that your gloves may need to be replaced. 
5.6.3 
Keeping an eye out for these issues (and others) takes you one step further from a worksite hand 
injury, which is the goal of hand protection. 
5.6.4 
All above is to prevent needle sticks and punctures from sharps, as well as offering optimal 
resistance to cuts during the waste collection to disposal process. 
 
 
5.7 Proper Glove Care – Recommendation from Manufacturer 
 
5.7.1 
By regularly washing your Needle Resistant Gloves, they can last up to 300% longer, and keep you 
safer. When working on a job, you can easily wash your gloves on site. 
 
**The below steps are not applicable for current NRG usage** 
 
5.8 Waste Collection Process Single Room and Shared Rooms/Common Patient Bay Areas such as ED, Day 
care unit: - 
5.8.1 
This process is applicable in places such as single rooms, shared rooms, common patient bay 
area’s i.e., Emergency Department, Day Care Unit, etc. 
5.8.2 
If an activity includes more than waste collection (e.g., discharge or terminal cleaning) inside the 
single patient room or shared rooms/common patient bay areas, NRG must be used only to collect 
waste. This should be done prior to any other cleaning task. Once completed, then remove NRG 
using the doffing process described below (refer to 5.3.) followed by other cleaning activities 
before moving to another room or location. 
5.8.3 
If the purpose of the activity is only for waste collection and will involve several rooms or shared 
rooms/common patient bay areas, then NRG can be worn when moving from one room/bed to 
another by using the “non-touch method” when opening doors/door handles and curtains (e.g., 
using elbow). 
5.8.4 
Alternatively, someone should be there to assist in opening doors/curtains. Once the activity is 
done the staff should follow the doffing process described below (Refer to: 5.3) 
5.8.5 
The gloves shall be used to transfer waste from waste bin to the trash trolley and disposing to the 
660 litres bin. 
 
 
6. Health and safety – Points to Remember

These gloves are cut and puncture resistant, not cut and puncture proof. 
6.2

6.3 
Stay in a comfortable working position, do not over-stretch to reach the task area. 
6.4 
Handle all chemicals safely according to COSHH / SDS. 
6.5 
Use appropriate PPEs as per (HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
6.6 
To complete the job safely, follow the associated risk assessment. 
6.7

4. Pre-Job Check 
4.1 Infection Control & HSEQ 
4.1.1 
Wash hands as per the approved method prior to commencing task. Check hands for any cuts 
and abrasions, if yes cover with waterproof dressing (Refer to HMC policies CL 7241 Hand 
Hygiene Policy). 
4.1.2 
Wear appropriate PPE as per guidelines (Refer to HMC policies CL 7242 Infection Control 
Practices). 
4.1.3 
Ensure you have been trained in RA, MS, SDS, COSHH and chemical handling. 
4.1.4 
Place the Caution board. 
4.1.5 
Ensure that the work area is free from any hindrance or obstacles that could affect the Cleaning / 
Housekeeping operation. 
4.1.6 
Before commencing the task, any damage to surfaces or any incident should be reported to the 
team leader.

Follow FMM heat stress policy while collecting external waste collection. 
 
4.2 TOOLS & EQUIPMENT 
4.2.1 
Check & prepare all equipment that may be required for the task. 
4.2.2 
Choose appropriate colour coded tools & equipment and ensure they are assembled safely. 
4.2.3 
Condition of tools – check if these are in clean, good working condition & safe to use. 
4.2.4 
Floors are checked for any adhesive soiling, use a non-abrasive pad as required only. 
4.2.5 
Ensure the chemicals spray bottles are properly labelled with details such as dilution /expiry date 
& time. 
4.2.6 
Always check the preparation date of the diluted cleaning chemicals (14 Plus Antibacterial all-
purpose cleaner). The usage of the chemical solution must be within 24 hours of the dilution 
process. 
4.2.7 
To identify the work area and place the barricades and warning signs as needed. 
4.2.8 
Ensure that the work area is free from any hindrance or obstacles that could affect the Cleaning / 
Housekeeping operation. 
4.2.9 
A Housekeeping Team shall be assigned for transport of infectious waste. 
4.2.10 Be aware of the possible presence of biohazard/infectious wastes when handling all wastes. 
4.2.11 Make sure the domestic garbage bags labelled with appropriate waste traceability code shared 
by HMC OHS team - RLGH – HMC 29, RLGH S&T – HMC 29.1, MHS – HMC 32, AAH - 35 
4.2.12 Make sure the clinical waste bags labelled with clinical waste sticker along with facility name, 
date etc. 
 
 
5. Job Steps 
 
5.1 Domestic Waste Collection 
 
5.1.1 
Identify the garbage level in the bin and if it is exceeding the limit 
(75% /three-fourth (¾) full /as needed) 
5.1.2 
Always use needle resistant gloves while transferring any waste 
between waste bins or skips. 
5.1.3 
Remove the garbage along with the bag, tie it carefully. 
5.1.4 
Non-hazardous waste from wards, clinics, and offices etc., such as 
paper, wood, cardboard cartons, fabrics, food refuses, plastic, and 
rubber scraps should be put in black plastic bag which is placed 
inside the domestic waste bins. 
5.1.5 
Report and record if any sharps found in the domestic garbage. 
5.1.6 
Keep bags away from your body and do not push it with your 
hands. 
5.1.7 
Move the tied garbage bag into a waste collection trolley and 
transport to the waste disposal area in the facility.

5.1.10 Do not drag the garbage bag on the floor. 
5.1.11 Clear the large pieces of garbage left over at the bottom of the waste bin. (If applicable) 
 
 
5.2 Infectious or Clinical Waste Collection 
 
5.2.1 
Infectious or Clinical waste shall be bagged into a Yellow Plastic Bag. 
5.2.2 
Always use needle resistant gloves while handling sharp waste. 
5.2.3 
When this bag is three-fourth (¾) full /as needed, it should be 
labeled, sealed, tied, and transport into clinical waste holding area at 
the facility. 
5.2.4 
Sharp boxes - when (¾) full /as needed close the lid carefully, shall be 
bagged into a yellow plastic bag, it should be labelled (provided by 
HMC with auto clave specification), sealed, tied - all these tasks should 
be carried out by nursing team. 
5.2.5 
Transport the sharp boxes into clinical waste holding area at the 
facility. 
5.2.6 
Sharp waste must be handled with extra care and placed in the 
designated leak and puncture resistant containers. 
5.2.7 
Keep bags away from your body and do not push it with your hands. 
5.2.8 
Do not transfer garbage from one bag to another or compress it to fill 
with more garbage. 
5.2.9 
Do not drag the garbage bag on the floor. 
 
5.3 Waste Bin Washing 
 
5.3.1 
Move the waste bins to the designated bin washing area. 
 
 
5.3.2 
Wash the waste bin with water and apply antibacterial cleaning 
solution (Ecolab 14 Plus Antibacterial all-purpose cleaner) on a 
sponge into the bin surfaces.
