6. Health & Safety – Points to Remember 
6.1 Always place the precautionary/warning signage. 
6.2 Stay in a comfortable working position, do not over-stretch to reach the task area, use long handled 
tools instead. 
6.3 Handle all chemicals safely according to COSHH / SDS. 
6.4 Use appropriate PPEs (refer to HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
6.5 Avoid physical contact with chemical (specially with eyes). 
6.6 To complete the job safely, follow the associated risk assessment. 
6.7 Follow the HMC guidelines with regards to COVID 19 Precautionary measures. 
6.8 Perform a complete workplace 360 degrees check for any unusual conditions or new hazards and report 
them to your team leader right away. 
6.9 Only use appropriate tools and equipment for the task. 
6.10 Comply with all HMC regulations for safety, hygiene, and infection control. 
6.11 Never leave any tool or material in the area unattended. 
6.12 As much as possible, keep the pathways clear of obstructions. 
 
7. Quality Verification & Post Job Check 
7.1 Always make sure that waste bins should not be filled more than ¾ or 75% of the waste bin. 
7.2 Visually check the waste bins cleaned and disinfected after every waste disposal. 
7.3 Always use non leak garbage bags. 
7.4 Use correct labelling for infectious and non-infectious waste before disposing to waste holding area. 
7.5 Ensure that the tools, signs, and barricading has been removed from the task location and safely take 
them back to the HK room/store and keep the area clear for user. 
7.6 Double Check if there are no tools, materials left in the area. 
7.7 Notify your team leader that the work is complete, and area is free of materials. 
7.8 Remove your PPE (HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
7.9 Wash your hands thoroughly after the task is completed (HMC Policies CL 7241 Hand Hygiene.) 
7.10 Did you notice any additional hazards during the work task that wasn’t mentioned in the Method 
Statement? (if yes, inform your team leader)

Dispose each recycle waste streams into the recycle waste 
cage deployed on the designated location at the facility.

waste cage compartment.

4. Pre-Job Check 
 
4.1 Infection Control & HSEQ 
4.1.1 
Wash hands as per the approved method prior to commencing task. Check hands for any cuts 
and abrasions, if yes cover with waterproof dressing (Refer to HMC policies CL 7241 Hand 
Hygiene Policy). 
4.1.2 
Wear appropriate PPE as per guidelines (Refer to HMC policies CL 7242 Infection Control 
Practices) 
4.1.3 
Staff to have COSHH Briefing prior to use the chemical. 
4.1.4 
To Identify the work area and place the barricades and warning signs as needed. 
4.1.5 
Warning sign to be placed on both the entrance/exit doors. 
4.1.6 
Ensure that the work area is free from any hindrance or obstacles that could affect the Cleaning 
/ Housekeeping operation. 
 
4.2 Tools & Equipment 
4.2.1 
Check & prepare all the equipment that may be required for the task. 
4.2.2 
Choose appropriate colour coded tools & equipment and ensure they are assembled safely. 
4.2.3 
Condition of tools – check if these are in clean, good working condition & safe to use. 
4.2.4 
Always check the preparation date of the diluted cleaning chemical (Oasis Pro14 all-purpose 
cleaner, Ecolab High Performance Floor Cleaner). (dilution ratio – 8 ml into 1 Ltr of water) 
4.2.5 
 The usage of the chemical solution must be within 24 hours of the dilution process 
 
5. Job Steps 
5.1 Staircase Cleaning 
5.1.1 
Remove any trashes, visible dirt dry leaf etc. from the floor with a dustpan 
and brush. 
5.1.2 
Identify stains on the floor and do necessary spotting with floor 
scrapper/nonabrasive pads/hard brush. 
5.1.3 
Check for any damage and pest infestation and report as per the 
procedure. 
5.1.4 
Clean the ceiling, Wall, fixtures, fittings etc. with MFC – Refer to MS-CLE-
HMC-08 Dusting & Damp wiping. 
 
5.1.5 
Sweep the stairs, landing area from top to bottom paying particular 
attention to edges and corners. Refer to MS-CLE-HMC-07 Dust moping. 
5.1.6 
Pick up soil, place into refuse sack and remove to the disposal point. 
5.1.7 
Clean the floor with a damp Kentucky mop, clean adjacent skirting by 
cutting in a parallel to skirting. Refer to MS-CLE-HMC-06 Damp moping. 
5.1.8 
Ensure the mop bucket behind line of work on the landing. 
5.1.9 
Pay particular attention to the edges, corners, and risers. 
5.1.10 Rinse the mop frequently and change the cleaning solution if required.

5.2 Balusters and Handrails Cleaning. 
5.2.1 
Clean the balusters and handrails using a MFC paying particular attention 
to balustrade rail, string capping, outer string, and the handrail. 
 
5.2.2 
Refer to MS-CLE-HMC-08 Dusting & Damp wiping. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
6. Health and safety – Points to remember 
6.1 
Always place the precautionary / warning signage near doors. 
6.2 
The team leader or supervisor should continue to look for the lone workers. 
6.3 
Watchout for the low head rooms / Bumps 
6.4 
Wear the right non-slip safety footwear for the job. 
6.5 
Stay in a comfortable working position, do not over-stretch to reach the task area rather than using 
long handled tools. 
6.6 
Use appropriate PPEs (refer to HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
6.7 
To complete the job safely, follow the associated risk assessment. 
6.8 
Perform a complete workplace 360 degrees check for any unusual conditions or new hazards and 
report them to your team leader right away. 
6.9 
Only use appropriate tools and equipment for the task. 
6.10 Comply with all HMC regulations for safety, hygiene, and infection control. 
6.11 Never leave any tool or material in the area unattended. 
6.12 As much as possible, keep the pathways clear of obstructions. 
 
7. Quality Verification & Post Job Check 
 
7.1 
After completion of the task, the appearance of area shall be clean and satisfactory. 
7.2 
Floor, wall, balusters, handrails etc. must be free from dirt or dust. 
7.3 
Ensure that the tools, signs, and barricading has been removed from the task location and safely 
take them back to the HK room/store and keep the area clear for user. 
7.4 
Double Check if there are no tools, materials left in the area. 
7.5 
Notify your team leader that the work is complete, and area is free of materials. 
7.6 
Remove your PPE (HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
7.7 
Wash your hands thoroughly after the task is completed (HMC Policies CL 7241 Hand Hygiene.) 
7.8 
Did you notice any additional hazards during the work task that wasn’t mentioned in the Method 
Statement? (if yes, inform your team leader) 
7.9 
Are there any faults with your tools and PPE that you must report? 
7.10 Report all Damages, Incidents & accidents / Health events to your Team Leader. 
7.11 Waste must be properly disposed as per SA-1054-Hazardous materials and waste management 
program.

4. Pre-Job Check 
4.1 Machine Pre-check 
4.1.1 
Check that the unit switches on and off correctly. 
4.1.2 
Carry out a safety check of each part of the electrical equipment as appropriate. 
4.1.3 
Cables should be checked for exposed wires, cuts or scrapes - by running a clean colour coded 
cloth from the machine to the plug, if there are any faults then the machine should not be used 
but should be marked as “out of order” in line with the reporting procedure. 
4.1.4 
The cable should be neatly placed and should at no time be present as a trip hazard. 
4.1.5 
Filters should be clean and correctly fitted, if the filter is clogged it should be cleaned or 
changed. 
4.1.6 
The casing of the machine should be complete and in good working condition; the wheels free 
from debris and freely moving. 
4.1.7 
Ensure correct accessories are selected, pad drives and pads or brushes depending on the floor 
type. 
4.1.8 
Avoid standing on the cable is primarily for the operative’s safety, it is possible that something 
sharp on the sole of a shoe could cut though the wire, ensure that they are aware it is with their 
wellbeing in mind that this is stressed as a safety point. 
4.1.9 
Cut out switches should be checked- if present and identify potential hazard whilst working. 
4.1.10 Prior to commencing the damp mopping process, ensure that a thorough dust mopping of the 
area has been completed (except isolation rooms). For dust mopping procedure, please see MS - 
CLE-HMC-007-Dust moping.

## Page 156

METHOD STATEMENT 
MS-CLE-HMC-04 
 Floor Scrubbing with Machine 
DATE 
5/15/2024 
REVISION 
V3.0 
 
Template version: 2.0 dated 01-Feb-2018 
 
Page 4 of 7 
 
4.1.11 To remove any stubborn stains/marks before mopping, use an abrasive pad and in case of residual 
glue from floor stickering or chewing gum stuck on the floor, use a floor scrapper. 
 
4.2 Infection Control & HSEQ 
4.2.1 
Wash hands as per the approved method prior to commencing task. Check hands for any cuts and 
abrasions, if yes cover with waterproof dressing (Refer to HMC policies CL 7241 Hand Hygiene 
Policy). 
4.2.2 
Wear appropriate PPE as per guidelines (Refer to HMC policies CL 7242 Infection Control 
Practices) 
4.2.3 
Ensure you have been trained in RA, MS, SDS, COSHH and chemical handling. 
4.2.4 
Identify the work area Risk access and place the warning sign. If the area is like a corridor or an 
open space such as patient waiting area etc. the warning sign needs to be placed on both sides. 
4.2.5 
Ventilate the area where appropriate. 
 
4.3 Tools & Equipment 
4.3.1 
Check & prepare all the equipment that may be required for the task. 
4.3.2 
Choose appropriate colour coded tools & equipment and ensure they are assembled safely. 
4.3.3 
Condition of tools – check if these are in clean, good working condition & safe to use. 
4.3.4 
Floors are checked for any adhesive soiling, use a non-abrasive pad as required only. 
 
4.4 Top-Down System 
4.4.1 
In a clean bucket, prepare 2 litres of diluted cleaning solution (16 ml of Ecolab Oasis Pro 14 
chemical and 2 litres of water). This solution is diluted and dispensed from the Ecolab dispenser 
located in the housekeeping room. 
4.4.2 
Place 20-25 microfibre flat mops inside the plastic box with lid. 
4.4.3 
Place the box on a clean pallet in the housekeeping room and pour the diluted chemical solution 
into the plastic box over the chemical tray. 
4.4.4 
Close the box with the lid and seal it properly. 
4.4.5 
Turn the box upside down for pre-soaking (charging or impregnating) the mops for at least 20-30 
minutes. 
4.4.6 
Place the box on the housekeeping cart. 
 
4.5 Double Mop Bucket w/Wringer 
4.5.1 
For ISOLATION Rooms, a double mop bucket with wringer will be used along with disposable 
mops. 
4.5.2 
Ensure that each Isolation room is assigned with a dedicated double mop bucket with wringer 
and the exact room number is written on this bucket set and it is stored in the housekeeping 
room. 
4.5.3 
In the blue coloured bucket, prepare a cleaning solution with 8 ml Oasis Pro 14 chemical to 1 litre 
of water ratio.

## Page 157

METHOD STATEMENT

5. Job Steps 
5.1 Check the status of the Pad/Brush 
5.1.1 
Ensure that the machine is not yet plug to the outlet. 
5.1.2 
Lay down the machine slowly. 
5.1.3 
Remove the pad from the drive plate. 
 
5.1.4 
Check the pad/brush condition: Flip the pad if it is dirty or damaged. 
5.1.5 
Replace it if its thickness has been reduced by 50% of the original size. 
5.1.6 
Place the pad / brush on the drive plate. 
 
5.1.7 
If it is a new pad, it is recommended to immerse the pad in water before using it (especially if 
you don’t have a lot of experience with this machine). 
 
5.2 Filling the solution tank 
5.2.1 
Add water and the chemical in a bucket (it will depend on the type of floor to be cleaned) and fill the 
solution tank with the chemical diluted. Follow the Chemical dilution ratio Ecolab High Performance 
Floor Cleaner – 4/8 ml chemical in 1 Litre of water. 
 
5.3 Brush/Pad Selection Guidelines 
5.3.1 
The machine is having the proper pad/brush for the activity to be done? 
• 
White pad - for polishing and shine. 
• 
Red pad – for scrubbing general areas. 
• 
Green pad – for scrubbing general areas. 
• 
Black pad – stone/Hard surfaces. 
• 
Brush – stone, pavement. 
 
Please note: - It is very important that you check with your team leader what is the pad to use if you are not 
familiar with the area / task to do. 
 
5.4 Initiate the machine operation by following the below steps. 
5.4.1 
Place the barricade at the entrance of the area to be clean. 
5.4.2 
Place “work in progress” signage. 
5.4.3 
Plug the electric cable into the power supply. 
5.4.4 
Adjust the height of the handle (approximately at the height of the 
waist) using the lever available. 
5.4.5 
Press both switch locks, press the brush motor switch at the same time. 
5.4.6 
Now the machine is started, and the switch lock can be released. 
5.4.7 
Lift the bar connected to the solution tank to start pouring the diluted 
chemical on the floor.

## Page 158

METHOD STATEMENT

Page 6 of 7 
 
5.4.8 
Cleaning should be commenced from the farthest point from the exit using systematic 
overlapping passes, leaving a clean, even, and reflective surface ensuring the cable is always 
behind you, where applicable. 
5.4.9 
Depend on the floor size and soilage it may be necessary to change the 
pad. If electrical the machine should be safely unplugged whilst the pad is 
checked and changed as required. 
5.4.10 Run the machine following the directions shown in the picture with 
overlaps. Ensure that all the areas have been covered. 
5.4.11 If there is not enough water or if you are staying too much time on the same spot, “polo” marks 
will be left on the floor. 
5.4.12 Leave the floor for 5 minutes contact time. 
5.4.13 You need to be very careful not to damage the tiles or walls. 
5.4.14 While working next to glass walls, it is necessary to place rubber 
protectors to prevent the machine from reaching the glass. 
5.4.15 Remove the chemical from the floor by using a and wet dry vacuum or 
a squeegee. 
5.4.16 Rinse the floor with fresh water and remove the water by using wet & 
dry vacuum machine. Make sure no more chemical accumulation of l 
remains on the floor surface. 
5.4.17 Use a mop to dry the floor and keep the barricade on the area until is 
100% dry. 
5.4.18 Fold properly the cables without leaving any knot. 
5.4.19 Take the machine to the designated area for its storage. 
 
6. Health and safety – Points to remember 
 
6.1.1 
Wet Floor Caution signboard shall be displayed when cleaning to prevent accidents and floor 
should be allowed to dry thoroughly. Use large caution sign (e.g., concertina) for long corridors, 
high traffic areas, hallways, lobbies, etc. to protect patients, staff, and visitors from stepping on 
to the floor. 
6.1.2 
Stay in a comfortable working position, do not over-stretch to reach the task area rather than 
using long handled tools. 
6.1.3 
Handle all chemicals safely according to COSHH / SDS. 
6.1.4 
Use appropriate PPEs as per (HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
6.1.5 
Avoid physical contact with chemical (specially with eyes). 
6.1.6 
To complete the job safely, follow the associated risk assessment. 
6.1.7 
Follow the HMC guidelines with regards to COVID 19 Precautionary measures. 
6.1.8 
Perform a complete workplace 360 degrees check for any unusual conditions or new hazards and 
report them to your team leader right away. 
6.1.9 
Use the appropriate tools and equipment for the task. 
6.1.10 Staff to follow TBT for slip, trip & fall. 
6.1.11 Always follow your superior's guidelines when performing a task. 
6.1.12 Comply with all HMC regulations for safety, good hygiene, and infection control.

4.3 Floor Scrubbing Machines Pre-Check 
 
4.3.1 
Check that the unit switches on and off correctly. 
4.3.2 
Carry out a safety check of each part of the electrical equipment as appropriate. 
4.3.3 
Cables should be checked for exposed wires, cuts or scrapes - by running a clean colour coded 
cloth from the machine to the plug, if there are any faults then the machine should not be 
used but should be marked as “out of order” in line with the reporting procedure. 
4.3.4 
The cable should be neatly placed and should at no time be present as a trip hazard. 
4.3.5 
Filters should be clean and correctly fitted, if the filter is clogged it should be cleaned or 
changed. 
4.3.6

free from debris and freely moving. 
4.3.7

4.4 Top-Down System 
 
4.4.1 
Preparation of Anti-Bacterial Solution (Oasis Pro 14) 
4.4.1.1 In a clean bucket, fill 2 litres of diluted anti-bacterial cleaning solution 
(This solution is diluted and dispensed from the Ecolab dispenser located in the 
housekeeping room). 
4.4.1.2 Place 20-25 blue coloured microfibre flat mops inside the plastic box with lid and BLUE 
handle. 
4.4.1.3 Place the box on a clean pallet in the housekeeping room and pour the diluted chemical 
solution into the plastic box over the chemical tray. 
4.4.1.4 Close the box with the lid and seal it properly. 
4.4.1.5 Turn the box upside down for pre-soaking (charging or impregnating) the mops for at 
least 20-30 minutes. 
4.4.1.6 Place the box on the housekeeping cart.

5. Job Steps 
5.1 Floor Cleaning with Walk Behind Scrubber 
 
5.1.1 
Plan work route, when necessary, remove furniture and equipment 
 if required. 
5.1.2 
Commence the dust mopping task before using walk behind 
scrubber machine, Refer to MS -CLE-HMC-007 – Dust Mopping. 
5.1.3 
Switch off charger by unplug charger cable from red battery plug. 
Reconnect the red battery plug to the machine. 
5.1.4 
Disconnect the battery charger cable from the mains electricity supply. 
5.1.5 
Fill the tank with a clean water. The clean water temperature must not exceed 40 °C. 
5.1.6 
Prepare floor cleaning chemical (Ecolab High Performance Floor Cleaner) from the chemical 
dispenser into a clean bucket and pour into the machine solution tank. 
5.1.7 
If the squeegee is not connected to the machine: install and 
fasten it with the handwheels, then connect to the suction hose. 
5.1.8 
If the squeegee is in upper position, lower it with the lever. 
5.1.9 
Place the brush or the pad-holder under the deck. Use the 
appropriate brush to clean the floor. 
5.1.10 Lower the brush deck with the pedal. 
5.1.11 Turn on the key, then slightly press the handle switch to engage the brush. 
5.1.12 Adjust (if needed) the solution flow level according to the cleaning performance and 
application. 
5.1.13 Clean the floor, having regard to the brush position and 
pressure in relation to the soil age, using a systematic overlapping 
pattern and changing or turning the brushes or pads as necessary. 
5.1.14 Remove the splashes from walls, skirtings, or other items as they occur. 
5.1.15 Clean the water left over on the floor with pre-charged damp mop if any. 
5.1.16 Once you have finished cleaning empty the recovery tank with the 
hose. Then empty the solution tank with the transparent hose. 
This must be carried in a designated area. 
5.1.17 Wash with clean water the recovery tank and the vacuum grid. 
5.1.18 Press the click-off button and wait for the brush/pad holder to fall on the floor. Remove to 
clean or change brush. 
5.1.19 Take the squeegee off and remove debris. 
5.1.20 Check and clean the squeegee blades. 
5.1.21 Clean the filter by removing the transparent cover and the gasket. 
5.1.22 Check the machine body and frame then clean and disinfect the major touch points, body etc. 
5.1.23 Charge the batteries: Connect the battery charger cable to the mains electricity supply 
5.1.24 Park the machine at designated area with the recovery tank cover open. 
5.1.25 If the machine has been used until the Red light illuminates on the battery level indicator on 
the machine, a full recharge of the batteries (10-12 hours) will be required. Failure to carry 
out this this procedure may cause damage to the batteries. Charging the batteries after every 
use is recommended.

5.2 FLOOR CLEANING WITH RIDE ON SCRUBBER 
 
5.2.1 
Plan work route, when necessary, remove furniture and equipment 
if required. 
5.2.2 
Commence the dust mopping task before using walk behind 
scrubber machine, Refer to MS -CLE-HMC-007 – Dust Mopping. 
5.2.3 
Disconnect battery charger and store the cable. 
5.2.4 
Disconnect the battery charger cable from the mains electricity supply. 
5.2.5 
Fill the solution tank with fresh water. The freshwater temperature must not exceed 40 °C. 
5.2.6 
Prepare floor cleaning chemical (Ecolab High Performance Floor Cleaner) from the chemical 
dispenser into a clean bucket and pour into the machine solution tank. 
5.2.7 
Solution level indicator is on the side of the tank shows current level. 
5.2.8 
If the squeegee is not connected to the machine: install and 
fasten it by using the two knobs, then connect the suction hose. 
5.2.9 
Adjust squeegee angle and blade pressure for optimal water 
 pick-up by turning the adjustment knob on the support. 
5.2.10 Place the brush or the pad-holder under the deck and install it manually. Use the appropriate 
brush to clean the floor. 
5.2.11 Insert the operator key and start the machine. Lower the brush by pressing the brush button. 
5.2.12 Push the drive pedal to start cleaning. Adjust the maximum forward speed by the speed 
potentiometer. For the reverse mode press the R on the control panel. 
5.2.13 Adjust (if needed) the solution flow level according to the cleaning performance and 
application. 
5.2.14 Clean the floor, having regard to the brush position and pressure 
 in relation to the soil age, using a systematic overlapping pattern 
 and changing or turning the brushes or pads as necessary. 
5.2.15 Remove the splashes from walls, skirtings, or other items as they occur. 
5.2.16 Once you have finished cleaning empty the recovery tank with the 
hose. Then empty the solution tank with the transparent hose. 
5.2.17 Wash with fresh water the recovery tank and the vacuum grid. 
5.2.18 Press the click-off button and wait for the brush/pad holder to fall on the floor. 
5.2.19 Remove to clean or change brush. 
5.2.20 Take the squeegee off and remove debris. 
 Check and clean the squeegee blades. 
5.2.21 Check the machine body and frame then clean and disinfect with 
Oasis PRO 14 chemical the major touch points, body etc. 
5.2.22 Charge the batteries: Connect the battery charger cable to the 
 electrical socket. 
5.2.23 Park the machine at designated area with the recovery tank cover open.

5.3 FLOOR CLEANING WITH ROBOT 
5.3.1 
Before commencing the task, make sure the device is fully charged. 
5.3.2 
Fill the water in the tank according to the water level mentioned 
on the machine. 
5.3.3 
Locate the machine for the assigned task. 
5.3.4 
Turn on the machine key. 
5.3.5 
Unlock the monitor and log in with required user credentials. 
5.3.6 
Choose the appropriate run mode according to the task, 
wet run for scrubbing purpose and dry run for sweeping purpose. 
5.3.7 
Select the mapped location where the task to be commenced. 
5.3.8 
Attach the required accessories such as squeegee blade 
for wet run and dust mop cloth for dry run. 
5.3.9 
Observe the machine operation always. 
5.3.10 After the selected area’s task completion, change the machine in manual mode. 
5.3.11 Continue cleaning with remaining mapped locations as 
the entire ground floor corridor mapped into 4 sections. 
5.3.12 Continue the same steps till completion of remaining mapped areas. 
5.3.13 Recharge the battery if required in between the task. 
5.3.14 After completion, wheel the robot into the nearest housekeeping room. 
5.3.15 Clean and disinfect the with Oasis PRO 14 chemical touch 
points and body. 
5.3.16 Detach the accessories and dust mop for laundry. 
5.3.17 Do not change the water for 3-4 days as the machine inbuilt with water purifying system, the 
machine purifies dirty water into clean water and transferring to the clean water tank by 
default. 
5.3.18 Return the machine into designated storage area. 
5.3.19 Remove the key and store in the key rack. 
5.3.20 Put on the machine for charge for next use. 
 
6. Health and safety – Points to remember 
6.1 
Always place the precautionary / warning signage near doors. 
6.2 
Wear the right non-slip safety footwear for the job. 
6.3 
Stay in a comfortable working position, do not over-stretch to reach the task area rather than using 
long handled tools. 
6.4 
Use appropriate PPEs (refer to HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
6.5 
To complete the job safely, follow the associated risk assessment. 
6.6 
Perform a complete workplace 360 degrees check for any unusual conditions or new hazards and 
report them to your team leader right away. 
6.7 
Only use appropriate tools and equipment for the task. 
6.8 
Comply with all HMC regulations for safety, hygiene, and infection control. 
6.9 
Never leave any tool or material in the area unattended. 
6.10 As much as possible, keep the pathways clear of obstructions.

1. Training & induction Requirements

correct PPE for the job; Use correct chemical and explain the dilution if required; Describe donning & doffing of 
PPE’s and importance of infection control in an isolation area.

External

4. Pre-Job Check 
4.1 Infection Control & HSEQ 
4.1.1 
Wash hands as per the approved method prior to commencing task. Check hands for any cuts 
and abrasions, if yes cover with waterproof dressing (Refer to HMC policies CL 7241 Hand 
Hygiene Policy).

4.1.2 
Wear appropriate PPE as per guidelines (Refer to HMC policies CL 7242 Infection Control 
Practices) 
4.1.3 
Ensure you have been trained in RA, MS, SDS, COSHH and chemical handling. 
4.1.4 
Place the Work signage caution board. 
4.1.5 
Ensure that the work area is free from any hindrance or obstacles that could affect the 
Cleaning / Housekeeping operation. 
4.1.6 
Before commencing the task, any damage to surfaces or any incident should be reported to 
the team leader. 
4.1.7 
Follow FMM heat stress policy. 
 
4.2 Tools & Equipment 
4.2.1 
Check & prepare all the equipment that may be required for the task. 
4.2.2 
Choose appropriate colour coded tools & equipment and ensure they are assembled safely. 
4.2.3 
Condition of tools – check if these are in clean, good working condition & safe to use. 
4.2.4 
Floors are checked for any adhesive soiling, use a non-abrasive pad as required only. 
4.2.5 
Always check the preparation date of the diluted cleaning chemicals (Oasis Pro 14, Ecolab 
Oasis orange force 137). Dilution ratio – 4/8 ml chemical into 1 Litre of water (If in case 
chemical not available from the Ecolab chemical dispenser machine). 
4.2.6 
The usage of the chemical solution must be within 24 hours of the dilution process. 
4.2.7 
Ensure for electrical equipment PAT test is done. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
5. Job Steps 
5.1 Job Steps 
 
5.1.1 
Remove the trashes and waste from the floor with a dustpan and 
brush. 
5.1.2 
Identify stains on the floor and do necessary spotting with the help of 
hard brush and available chemical related to the surface. Use Ecolab 
Oasis Orange Force 137. 
5.1.3 
Make sure you follow chemical contact time (30 to 60 seconds) - 
depends on the floor surface and stain to achieve a good result. 
5.1.4 
Identify available water points nearby and connect hose pipe / collect 
water into a container.

## Page 171

METHOD STATEMENT 
MS-CLE-HMC-02 
External Areas Floor Washing & Scrubbing 
DATE 
5/14/2024 
REVISION 
V2.0 
 
Template version: 2.0 dated 01-Feb-2018 
 
Page 4 of 6 
 
5.1.5 
Spread water into a manageable area especially where the chemical is 
applied. 
5.1.6 
Remove the soiled water from the surface with the help of a floor 
squeegee. 
5.1.7 
Rinse the floor again with water to avoid any chemical residues on the 
floor surface. 
5.1.8 
 Remove the excess water from the floor again with the help of a floor squeegee. 
5.1.9 
Make sure the floor is free from water. 
 
5.2 For Operating Pressure wash machine. 
5.2.1 
Read your operator's manual and follow the manufacturer's instructions for safe use, 
maintenance, and storage. Assemble equipment and check for safety, paying particular 
attention to any plugs, cables, hoses and connections. 
5.2.2 
Steps for using power wash machine. 
5.2.3 
Install the nozzle and the spray lance. 
5.2.4 
Connect high pressure hose: to the hand spray gun using safety pin / 
lock /to the Pressure machine. 
5.2.5 
Tighten the screw connection between the hose and the machine. 
5.2.6 
Connect the supply water hose (minimum length 7.5 m, minimum 
diameter 3/4“) to the facilities water valve for continuous supply of water (for e.g., a tap- Cold 
Water). Open the water supply. 
5.2.7 
Switch on the Machine with the key, push down accelerator & boost 
the engine. 
5.2.8 
Position the spray tip at least 2 feet (0.6 m) from the surface to start. 
5.2.9 
Hold the nozzle at a 45-degree angle to the surface. This will help 
direct loosened debris away from you. Pull the trigger to begin 
spraying the water. 
5.2.10 Gradually move the tip closer. You risk damaging the surface if you hold the tip closer than 12 
inches (0.3 m) 
5.2.11 Move the spray in a side-to-side motion, without spraying too long in any one spot. 
5.2.12 Turn the spray pipe towards the left or right to select the desired spray type. 
5.2.13 High pressure circular spray (0°) for especially stubborn dirt. 
5.2.14 High pressure flat spray (25°) for large dirt areas 
5.2.15 Flat low-pressure spray (CHEM) for operating using detergents or cleaning at low pressure. 
5.2.16 To keep control of the water stream, hang on to the spray wand with both hands. 
5.2.17 Avoid contact with the high-pressure stream of water. Keep your hands, feet and body out of 
the way. Maintain good footing. 
5.2.18 Do not reach so far that you lose your balance. Never point the sprayer at another person, 
whether the pressure washer is operating or not. It could discharge unexpectedly. 
5.2.19 Do not use a pressure washer in an area where the water could contact electrical equipment. 
5.2.20 On Job completion Switch off the engine to "STOP" till the engine comes to a halt. 
5.2.21 Shut off water supply. 
5.2.22 Remove the plug and recoil / rewind all cables and hoses.

5.2.23 Clean the equipment and check for safety of Equipment. 
5.2.24 Remove excess water from floor surfaces and leave as dry as is possible using a long-handled 
squeegee, directing water to the nearest drains. 
5.2.25 Return the equipment and materials to the Designated storage area. 
6. Health and safety – Points to Remember 
6.1 Always place the precautionary/warning signage. 
6.2 Stay in a comfortable working position, do not over-stretch to reach the task area, use long handle tools 
instead. 
6.3 Handle all chemicals safely according to COSHH / SDS. 
6.4 Use appropriate PPEs (refer to HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
6.5 Avoid physical contact with chemical (specially with eyes). 
6.6 To complete the job safely, follow the associated risk assessment. 
6.7 Follow the HMC guidelines with regards to COVID 19 Precautionary measures. 
6.8 Perform a complete workplace 360 degrees check for any unusual conditions or new hazards and report 
them to your team leader right away. 
6.9 Only use appropriate tools and equipment for the task. 
6.10 Comply with all HMC regulations for safety, hygiene, and infection control. 
6.11 Never leave any tool or material in the area unattended. 
6.12 As much as possible, keep the pathways clear of obstructions. 
6.13 Wait until the floor is entirely dry before removing the barricades, and then double-check that the 
floor is dry and clean. 
 
7. Quality Verification & Post Job Check 
 
7.1 Visually check the floor is free from any stains, mud, dirt accumulation, chemical residues, excess water 
etc. 
7.2 Wait until the floor is entirely dry before removing the barricades, and then double-check that the floor 
is dry and clean. 
7.3 Ensure that the tools, signs, and barricading has been removed from the task location and safely take 
them back to the HK room/store and keep the area clear for user. 
7.4 Double Check if there are no tools, materials left in the area. 
7.5 Notify your team leader that the work is complete, and area is free of materials. 
7.6 Remove your PPE (HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
7.7 Wash your hands thoroughly after the task is completed (HMC Policies CL 7241 Hand Hygiene.) 
7.8 Did you notice any additional hazards during the work task that wasn’t mentioned in the Method 
Statement? (if yes, inform your team leader) 
7.9 Are there any faults with your tools and PPE that you must report? 
7.10 Report all Damages, Incidents & accidents / Health events to your team leader. 
7.11 Waste must be properly disposed as per SA-1054-Hazardous materials and waste management 
program.

4. Pre-Job Check 
 
4.1 Infection Control & HSEQ 
4.1.1 
Wash hands as per the approved method prior to commencing task. Check hands for any cuts and 
abrasions, if yes cover with waterproof dressing (Refer to HMC policies CL 7241 Hand Hygiene 
Policy). 
4.1.2 
Wear appropriate PPE as per guidelines (Refer to HMC policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices) 
4.1.3 
Ensure that the work area is free from any hindrance or obstacles that could affect the Cleaning / 
Housekeeping operation. 
4.1.4 
Follow FMM heat stress policy. 
4.2 Tools & Equipment 
4.2.1 
Check & prepare all the equipment that may be required for the task. 
4.2.2 
Check that operator is trained and competent to operate the machine. 
4.2.3 
Check if the operator has equipment familiarization 
4.2.4 
Check if the O & M is available for the machine and operator is aware of it. 
4.2.5 
Choose appropriate colour coded tools & equipment and ensure they are assembled safely. 
4.2.6 
Log in to the daily machine usage checklist. 
4.2.7 
Ensure the city ranger machine is filled with adequate fuel, fuel filling must be from a Jerry can into 
the fuel tank. 
4.2.8 
Ensure the required pressure level of each tyre. (Refer to section 5.2) 
4.2.9 
Make sure the city ranger sweeping brushes installed properly and functioning. 
 
5. Job Steps 
5.1 Machine Safety 
 
5.1.1 
Avoid roll-over 
5.1.2 
Do not drive the machine in a place where can slide, tip or roll. 
5.1.3 
Do not drive on slopes with an incline of more than 10°. 
 
 
 
 
5.2 Tire Pressure 
 
5.2.1 
The tire pressure must be checked and adjusted to 1.5 bar (22 psi) when the Suction Sweeper is 
attached. 
5.2.2 
Lower tire pressure increases the risk of roll-overs. 
 
5.3 Fuel Check and Filling 
 
5.3.1 
Make sure the machine has filled with sufficient fuel to cover the areas as per the schedule. 
5.3.2 
If fuel need to be filled then, must contact team leader for assistance. 
5.3.3 
Fuel filling must be executed in front of team leader by the operator. 
5.3.4 
Fuel must be filled from the Jerry can to the fuel tank. 
5.3.5 
Open the fuel tank neck cap, connect the Jerry can funnel into the fuel tank neck, lift the jerry can 
carefully to flow the fuel into the fuel tank through the fuel tank neck.

## Page 177

5.3.6 
Make sure there is no fuel overflow from the fuel tank during the filling and after. 
5.3.7 
Close the fuel cap properly and lock it. 
5.3.8 
Always keep adequate fuel in the fuel tank. 
5.3.9 
If any spillage happened during the filling process, observe if the fuel evaporates from the 
floor/road. 
5.3.10 Jerry can have to be cleaned and returned to the appropriate storage space. 
 
5.4 Emptying the Hopper 
 
5.4.1 
Before emptying the hopper, make sure that the machine is firmly placed on. 
5.4.2 
 A level surface and is not “angled that there is sufficient space for the open back cover. 
5.4.3 
Make sure the hopper is secured. 
5.4.4 
Check the hopper is firmly attached to the Machine. 
5.4.5 
Prevent people from standing close to the Suction Sweeper. 
5.4.6 
Make sure there is no one close to the machine when it is in 
use. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
5.5 Assembling the Hopper Frame 
 
5.5.1 
The hopper frame is folded up and hung on the hopper delivery. 
5.5.2 
Assembling the hopper frame: 
 
5.5.2.1 Take the hopper frame down and unfold it. 
5.5.2.2 Put the frame together using the two bolts supplied. 
 
5.6 Moving the Hopper Frame: 
 
5.6.1 
The hopper frame can be transported on the Suction Sweeper. Fold 
it down in reverse order and hang it on the hopper. 
5.6.2 
The hopper must never be emptied when the hopper frame is hanging on the Suction Sweeper. 
 
 
 
5.7 Checks Before Start-up 
 
5.7.1 
Topping-up the water It is important the water tank 
is filled up before starting. 
 
 
 
Topping the water

The water level can be read on the right side of the

A water sprayer is positioned in the Hopper.

Four water-sprayers have enough water for 1¼ hours

If the only water-sprayer in the hopper issued, then 
it will have enough water for four hours of use. 
 
 
5.8 Adjusting Brushes 
 
5.8.1 
Adjust the brushes using the nose wheel. Adjust the 
brushes so that they precisely brush the ground to 
give a maximum clearing width, with half of the 
brush bristles touching the ground at any given time. 
5.8.2 
Correctly adjusted brushes last longer. 
5.8.3 
Make sure the brushes do not press too hard against 
the surface being cleaned. 
 
5.8.4 
Adjusting the angle of the external side-brush 
Tank Gauge 
Water sprayer 
Water Sprayer front to brush

5.8.4.1 Loosen the counter nut. 
5.8.4.2 Unscrew the parallel rod. 
5.8.4.3 The forward edge of the brush flowers. 
5.8.4.4 Screw the parallel rod back. 
5.8.4.5 The forward edge of the brush lifts. 
5.8.4.6 The optimal brushing position is with the forward 
1/3 of the brushes in contact with the ground. 
 
 
 
 
5.9 Adjusting the Vacuum Nozzle 
 
5.9.1 
Adjust the distance between the surface and vacuum 
nozzle using the height-adjustment fixture. 
 
 
5.9.2 
With the nose wheel screwed all the way down, the 
distance between the hydraulic motor and the ground 
is 95 mm. 
 
 
5.9.3 
This adjustment also affects the coupling between the 
front brushes and the machine. Check that the vacuum 
hose is tightly connected to the vacuum nozzle. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
5.10 
Checking the Rotary Filter and Turbine 
 
5.10.1 Checking the rotary filter and turbine. Remove any 
dirt before starting.

5.11 
Checking the Rotary Filter Water-Sprayer. 
 
5.11.1 Engage the handbrake. 
5.11.2 Turn the ignition key to position WITHOUT starting 
the machine. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
5.11.3 Switch the rear PTO ON. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
5.11.4 Check water comes out of the water-sprayer below 
the filter in the hopper tank. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
5.11.5 The Suction Sweeper must not be used if the water 
sprayer in the hopper is not working. Stop the turbine immediately. If there is imbalance/vibration 
in the filter or in the turbine. Low-level switch has been fitted which automatically stops the water 
pump if the water tank is empty. 
 
5.12 
Suction Sweeper Start-up 
 
5.12.1 Start the machine Switch ON the rear PTO. Water for 
the rotary filter starts to flow when the rear PTO is 
switched on. 
5.12.2 Switch ON the front PTO (brushes rotate).

5.12.3 Lower the front brushes by moving the joystick down. 
5.12.4 Engage the weight distribution using the red button on 
the joystick.

5.12.6 Drive the machine wherever the cleaning required. 
 
5.12.7 Give special attention to the road edges and corners.

5.13.1 Engage the handbrake.

5.13.3 Push the throttle to maximum. 
 
5.13.4 Turn the change-over handle so the arrow points 
down. 
 
 
 
 
 
5.13.5 Lift the locking clip and pull the vacuum hose out. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
5.13.6 Pull out the vacuum nozzle.

5.14 
Emptying the Hopper 
 
5.14.1 The hopper can empty directly onto the ground or 
tip into container. Before emptying the hopper, 
make sure that: The machine is on a level surface, 
and that there is sufficient space for the opened 
back cover. STOP the front and rear PTO. 
 
 
 
 
5.14.2 Drive to the nearest disposal area. Begin emptying by 
using the lower joystick to tip the hopper. 
 
5.14.3 Lower the joystick once the hopper is empty (The 
hopper will move back to its normal position). 
 
5.14.4 Release the handle when the hopper is back in position. 
The hopper is fitted with a hose-break valve, which will 
close if the hopper lowers too quickly. 
 
5.14.5 If this happens, raise the hopper a little again, and then 
lower it slowly. 
 
5.14.6 The rear hatch must not be opened until suction has 
ceased. Otherwise, there is a risk of personal injury. The 
suction continues to run for approx. 15 s after the 
turbine has been switched off (PTO rear). There is a risk 
of crushing while the hopper is being emptied. 
 
5.14.7 Make sure the hopper is completely lowered after emptying! 
 
5.15 
Emptying the Hopper for Water. 
 
5.15.1 The water in the hopper can be emptied by unscrewing 
the drain plug.

5.16 
Cleaning/Replacing Filter System and Turbine. 
 
5.16.1 Daily cleaning and maintenance will extend the lifetime of 
both the machine and the attachment. Clean the Suction 
Sweeper with water after use. Avoid high pressure cleaning 
of the snap couplings. Clean the snap couplings with an MFC. 
 
 
 
5.16.2 Cleaning/replacing filter system and turbine Attention! In 
case of imbalance in the filter or turbine, the machine must 
be stopped immediately to prevent vibration damage. 
 
 
 
5.16.3 Checking the filter and turbine daily. Dirt can cause 
imbalance in the rotary filter and turbine. It must be 
removed immediately! Give special attention to the filter and 
turbine while the machine is operating in difficult conditions. 
 
 
5.16.4 Cleaning the rotary filter and turbine. Remove the rotary 
filter and rotary filter grill and clean with water (max. temp 
50°C). Check for dirt on the turbine and clean. 
 
 
 
 
 
5.16.5 Flush the turbine until it is clean. A high-pressure cleaner is 
recommended for this purpose. Attach the rotary filter grill 
and rotary filter. Bolt should be tightened to 45 Nm of 
torque. If the rotary filter cannot be cleaned or is worn, it 
should be replaced. 
 
6. Health and safety – Points to remember 
6.1 As the machine is articulated, the rear end of the machine swings out when turning. 
6.2 Make sure that no one is near the machine while it is in use as there is a danger of crushing. 
6.3 Do not let go of the lever when the hopper tank is lowered as that is dangerous. Keep a good grip of 
the lever until the hopper is on the machine. 
6.4 Make sure no one gets their fingers trapped when the hopper is lowered after tipping. 
6.5 The hopper must never be emptied when the hopper frame is hanging on the Suction Sweeper 
6.6 Wear the right non-slip safety footwear for the job. 
6.7 Stay in a comfortable working position, do not over-stretch to reach the task area rather than using 
long handled tools. 
6.8 Use appropriate PPEs (refer to HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices).

4. Pre-Job Check 
4.1 Infection Control & HSEQ 
4.1.1 
Wash hands as per the approved method prior to commencing task. 
4.1.2 
Check hands for any cuts and abrasions, if yes cover with waterproof dressing (Refer to HMC 
policies CL 7241 Hand Hygiene Policy). 
4.1.3 
Wear appropriate PPE as per guidelines (Refer to HMC policies CL 7242 Infection Control 
Practices) 
4.1.4 
Ensure you have been trained in RA, MS, SDS, COSHH and chemical handling. 
4.1.5 
Barricade the area as appropriate. 
4.1.6 
Place the Work signage caution board. 
4.1.7 
Ensure that the work area is free from any hindrance or obstacles that could affect the Cleaning 
/ Housekeeping operation. 
4.1.8 
Before commencing the task, any damage to surfaces or any incident should be reported to the 
team leader. 
4.1.9 
Follow FMM heat stress policy. 
4.1.10 For External areas, stop the task if the wind speed is 12.5 m/s or more. Follow GL-HSEQ-02-
Working Under Severe Weather Conditions 
4.1.11 Staff to know about HMC Emergency protocols and Location of the FMM First aid kit.

4.2 Tools & Equipment 
4.2.1 
Check & prepare all the equipment that may be required for the task. 
4.2.2 
Choose appropriate colour coded tools & equipment and ensure they are assembled safely. 
4.2.3 
Condition of tools – check if these are in clean, good working condition & safe to use. 
4.2.4 
Ensure the chemicals spray bottles are properly labelled with details such as dilution /expiry date 
& time. 
4.2.5 
Always check the preparation date of the diluted cleaning chemicals (Oasis Pro 14, Ecolab Oasis 
orange force 137/Ecolab Pro 41). Dilution ratio – 8 ml chemical into 1 Litre of water (If in case 
chemical not available from the Ecolab chemical dispenser machine). 
4.2.6 
The usage of the chemical solution must be within 24 hours of the dilution process. 
4.2.7 
Ensure for electrical equipment PAT test is done. 
 
4.3 Permit to work 
4.3.1 
Obtain permit to work from the concerned departments followed by HMC permit to work form. 
4.3.2 
Follow FMM permit to work form with required signatures, maintain according to the required 
frequencies recommended by HSEQ department. 
4.3.3 
Approved permit to work form must be available in the file with façade cleaning team for any 
time inspection. 
4.3.4 
A briefing must be conducted to the façade team by the team leader with that particular day’s 
permit to work and target to be achieved etc. 
4.3.5 
HSE officers to be inspected the area before starting the task to make sure everything safe and 
readiness to proceed with the task. 
 
5. Job Steps 
5.1 Cleaning of Wall and cladding. 
5.1.1 
Select manageable size of the wall to be cleaned in one stretch, always follow the cleaning 
direction from top to bottom. 
5.1.2 
Place the podium ladder on the selected area – refer the below 5.5 Podium Ladders Inspections 
and Use. 
5.1.3 
Make sure appropriated PPEs are worn before commencing the task. 
5.1.4 
Identify visible stains on the wall, apply diluted Ecolab pro 14 chemical on the stain and scrub 
with an abrasive pad. 
5.1.5 
After removing the visible stains, start using the pressure washer machine to clean the wall 
surface effectively, use the pressure washer gun by following systematic overlapping passes 
(vertical/horizontal). Refer to the below 5.4 Operating Pressure Washer Machine. 
5.1.6 
After completion of wall washing with pressure washer machine on the selected wall surface, 
check on the metal claddings, perform damp wiping with a damp MFC – refer to MS-CLE-HMC-
008 for Damp wiping the cladding surface. 
 
5.2 Cleaning of Windows and Frames. 
5.2.1 Refer to MS-CLE-HMC-005 – Glass Cleaning.

## Page 189

METHOD STATEMENT

Page 5 of 7 
 
 
5.3 Cleaning of External Floor - Refer to MS-CLE-HMC-002 - External Area Floor Washing and Scrubbing 
5.3.1. Identify stains on the floor and do necessary spotting with the help of hard brush and available 
chemical related to the surface. Use diluted Ecolab Oasis Orange Force 137. 
5.3.2. identify available water points nearby and connect hose pipe / collect water into a container. 
5.3.3. Use pressure washer machine to clean the floor effectively, refer to 5.4 Operating Pressure Washer 
Machine. 
5.3.4. Remove the soiled water from the surface with the help of a floor squeegee, push the water into 
the nearest drainage system. 
5.3.5. Remove the excess water from the floor with the help of a floor squeegee. 
5.3.6. Wash the drain plate to remove accumulated dust or excess mud with pressure washer machine or 
with hose pipe. 
5.3.7. Make sure the floor is free from water before leaving the location. 
 
5.4 For Operating Pressure wash machine. 
5.4.1 
Read your operator's manual and follow the manufacturer's instructions for safe use, 
maintenance, and storage. 
5.4.2 
Assemble equipment and check for safety, paying particular attention to any plugs, cables, hoses 
and connections. 
5.4.3 
Install the nozzle and the spray lance. 
5.4.4 
Connect high pressure hose: to the hand spray gun using safety pin / lock /to the Pressure 
machine. 
5.4.5 
Tighten the screw connection between the hose and the machine. 
5.4.6 
Connect the supply water hose (minimum length 7.5 m, minimum diameter 3/4“) to the facilities 
water valve for continuous supply of water (for e.g., a tap- Cold Water). Open the water supply. 
5.4.7 
Switch on the Machine with the key, push down accelerator & Boost the engine. 
5.4.8 
Position the spray tip at least 2 feet (0.6 m) from the surface to start. 
5.4.9 
Hold the nozzle at a 45-degree angle to the surface. This will help direct loosened debris away 
from the user. Pull the trigger to begin spraying the water. 
5.4.10 Gradually move the tip closer. You risk damaging the surface if you hold the tip closer than 12 
inches (0.3 m) 
5.4.11 Move the spray in a side-to-side motion, without spraying too long in any one spot. 
5.4.12 Turn the spray pipe towards the left or right to select the desired spray type. 
5.4.13 High pressure circular spray (0°) for especially stubborn dirt. 
5.4.14 High pressure flat spray (25°) for large dirt areas 
5.4.15 Flat low-pressure spray (CHEM) for operating using detergents or cleaning at low pressure. 
5.4.16 To keep control of the water stream, hang on to the spray wand with both hands. 
5.4.17 Avoid contact with the high-pressure stream of water. Keep your hands, feet and body out of the 
way. Maintain good footing. 
5.4.18 Do not reach so far that you lose your balance. Never point the sprayer at another person, 
whether the pressure washer is operating or not. It could discharge unexpectedly. 
5.4.19 Do not use a pressure washer in an area where the water could contact electrical equipment. 
5.4.20 On Job completion Switch off the engine to "STOP" till the engine comes to a halt. 
5.4.21 Shut off water supply.

5.4.22 Remove the plug and recoil / rewind all cables and hoses. 
5.4.23 Clean the equipment and check for safety of Equipment. 
5.4.24 Remove excess water from floor surfaces and leave as dry as is possible using a long-handled 
squeegee, directing water to the nearest drains. 
5.4.25 Return the equipment and materials to the Designated storage area. 
 
5.5 Podium Ladder Inspections and Use. 
5.5.1 
Check all 4 castors are secure and the fixing bolts are tightened correctly. 
5.5.2 
Check all 2-wheel brakes operate and lock the wheel and castor into position. 
5.5.3 
Check the tubes and aluminium components of the podium step. There should not be any cracks 
or excessive dents or distortion. 
5.5.4 
Check there is free movement of all hinge points of the podium step. Check the plywood platform. 
5.5.5 
There should not be excessive contamination, cracking, holes or damage, including any missing 
or damaged rivet fixings. 
5.5.6 
Check the following podium step locking mechanisms are operating and locking correctly. Ladder 
claws and locks, gate claw and lock, rear folding frame elbow locking joints and the platform 
locking tabs. 
5.5.7 
Make sure the total load (1 person plus tools & materials) should not be more than 150kg. 
5.5.8 
Make sure the 4 castors are pointing outwards and the wheel brakes are locked. 
5.5.9 
Check the access ladder is located correctly. 
5.5.10 Make sure the podium step is on flat, level, dry and firm ground. 
5.5.11 Make sure the podium step is correctly positioned to avoid overreaching. 
5.5.12 Make sure the platform is at the correct height. 
5.5.13 Make sure the safety gate is shut and locked once you are on the platform. 
 
5.5.1 
Key Notes: 
5.5.1.1 Never stand on the guardrails, boxes, stepladders, or other objects on the platform, to gain 
additional working height. 
5.5.1.2 Never suspend the podium step from another structure or level podium step. 
5.5.1.3 The maximum number of occupants must not exceed one. 
5.5.1.4 Never use the podium step if any part is damaged or shows signs of excessive wear. 
5.5.1.5 Never move the podium step when a person is standing on it. 
5.5.1.6 Never over-reach or lean too far over the side of the podium step. 
5.5.1.7 Never use the podium step as a method of gaining access to another structure or level. 
5.5.1.8 In addition, Use Pre use Ladder checklist. 
 
6. Health and Safety – Points to Remember 
6.1 Always place the precautionary/warning signage. 
6.2 Stay in a comfortable working position, do not over-stretch to reach the task area, use long handle tools 
instead. 
6.3 Handle all chemicals safely according to COSHH / SDS. 
6.4 Use appropriate PPEs (refer to HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
6.5 Avoid physical contact with chemical (specially with eyes). 
6.6 To complete the job safely, follow the associated risk assessment. 
6.7 Follow the HMC guidelines with regards to COVID 19 Precautionary measures. 
6.8 Perform a complete workplace 360 degrees check for any unusual conditions or new hazards and report 
them to your team leader right away. 
6.9 Only use appropriate tools and equipment for the task. 
6.10 Comply with all HMC regulations for safety, hygiene, and infection control.

4.2.1 
Check & prepare all the equipment that may be required for the task.

Choose appropriate colour coded tools & equipment and ensure they are assembled safely.

Condition of tools – check if these are in clean, good working condition & safe to use.

To identify the work area and place the barricades and warning signs as needed.

4.2.9 
Cable wires are checked for any knuckles, cuts, or damages and reported immediately if unsafe 
to use towards the Direct Supervisor or else, recoil the cable wire correctly back to the machine. 
4.2.10 Before checking the power of the Suction Cleaning Machine, make sure to check the socket if it is 
turned off before plugging the machine to the outlet, the power of the machine then is tested if 
it works properly. 
4.2.11 Ensure equipment PAT test is done. 
 
5. Job Steps 
5.1 Place warning sign “Cleaning in progress” at the entrance or exit that should 
be visible to all whoever passing nearby. 
5.2 Adjust the furniture position suitable to perform the task. 
5.3 Large items of rubbish are cleared by using dustpan and brush before 
suction cleaning is commenced. 
5.4 Check for damage or heavy stains to be reported to team leader 
immediately. 
5.5 If in case of any removable stains or chewing gum stains – refer to MS-CLE-
HMC-021 Carpet Shampooing.

5.6 To start work, position the cable behind the line of work. 
5.7 Always pay attention to the edges and corners using the crevice nozzle or 
with suitable head. 
5.8 Adjust the brush/beater bar to suit the surface. 
5.9 Traffic lanes must be given more attention when doing the suction cleaning. 
5.10 Task carried out systematically in parallel overlapping passes so that all areas are effectively and 
efficiently cleaned. 
5.11 Re-check the surface again to identify if any area missed and repeat the task where required. 
Re arrange the furniture to exact position. 
 
6. Health and safety – Points to remember 
6.1 Always place the precautionary/warning signage 
6.2 Stay in a comfortable working position, do not over-stretch to reach the task area rather than using 
long handled tools. 
6.3 Handle all chemicals safely according to COSHH / SDS. 
6.4 Use appropriate PPEs as per (HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
6.5 Avoid physical contact with chemical (specially with eyes). 
6.6 To complete the job safely, follow the associated risk assessment. 
6.7 Follow the HMC guidelines with regards to COVID 19 Precautionary measures. 
6.8 Perform a complete workplace 360 degrees check for any unusual conditions or new hazards and 
report them to your team leader right away. 
6.9 Use the appropriate tools and equipment for the task. 
6.10 Comply with all HMC regulations for safety, hygiene, and infection control. 
6.11 Never leave any tool or material in the area unattended. 
6.12 As much as possible, keep the pathways clear of obstructions. 
6.13 Always pay full attention to the operations of the cleaning equipment as well as the task you are 
doing. 
 
7. Quality Verification & Post Job Check 
7.1 Visually check the floor/carpet cleaned well, should be free from dust, debris, stains etc. 
7.2 Make sure the furniture returned to the right place if those removed during cleaning. 
7.3 Carpet should be dried if carpet spotting process carried out. 
7.4 Warning signs, materials, tools, equipment etc. checked, cleaned, disinfected, and removed as 
necessary. 
7.5 Remove your PPE as per (HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
7.6 Wash your hands thoroughly as per HMC Policies CL 7241 Hand Hygiene. 
7.7 Did you notice any additional hazards during the work task that wasn’t mentioned in the Method 
Statement? (if yes, inform your team leader) 
7.8 MFC’s should be laundered as appropriate.

5. Job Steps 
5.1 Place warning sign “Cleaning in Progress” at the entrance or exit that should 
be visible to public. 
5.2 Vacuum the carpet with the help of a dry vacuum machine – Refer to MS-CLE-
HMC-012 Suction cleaning. 
5.3 Pay attention to clean carpet corners/edges while vacuuming. 
5.4 Identify minor/major stains on the carpet. 
5.5 Apply Ecolab Revitalize Miracle Spotter (Ready to use) into a sponge. 
5.6 (soft side). Alternative use of MFC/soft hand brush for carpet spotting. 
5.7 Gently execute spotting on the minor/major stains on the carpet. 
5.8 Wipe over the spotted area with MFC to clear the stain. 
5.9 Remove gum stains using with scrapper or gum removing tool. 
5.10 Make sure there are no damages on the carpet fibre. 
5.11 Pour diluted Ecolab Revitalize Carpet Upholstery Extraction cleaner. 
5.12 into the machine solution tank with the help of a chemical container. 
5.13 Run the carpet shampooing machine over the selected carpet portion. 
5.14 Shampooing should be commenced from the farthest point from the exit. 
5.15 Control the chemical flow according to the chemical requirement. 
5.16 on the carpet and the dirt accumulation. 
5.17 Should follow systematic overlapping passes while shampooing for better 
result or to miss any carpet portion. 
5.18 Machine operation should be in the following the directions shown in the 
picture with overlaps. Ensure that all the areas have been covered. 
5.19 If carpet shampooing commencing with a single disc machine, 
5.20 Then use carpet extractor machine/ wet and dry vacuum machine to 
remove. 
5.21 The applied chemical solution from the carpet. 
5.22 Redo with carpet extractor machine if the carpet remains wet. 
5.23 Leave the carpet for drying in room temperature or with the help of blower 
machine (optional depends on the urgency). 
5.24 Replace the furniture if those removed while carrying carpet shampooing task. 
 
6. Health and safety – Points to remember 
6.1 Always place the precautionary/warning signage. 
6.2 Stay in a comfortable working position, do not over-stretch to reach the task area rather than using long 
handled tools. Handle all chemicals safely according to COSHH / SDS. 
6.3 Use appropriate PPEs as per (HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
6.4 Avoid physical contact with chemical (specially with eyes). 
6.5 To complete the job safely, follow the associated risk assessment. 
6.6 Follow the HMC guidelines with regards to COVID 19 Precautionary measures. 
6.7 Perform a complete workplace 360 degrees check for any unusual conditions or new hazards and 
report them to your team leader right away. 
6.8 Use the appropriate tools and equipment for the task. 
6.9 Staff to follow TBT for slip, trip & fall. 
6.10 Always follow your superior's guidelines when performing a task.

5. Job Steps 
5.1 Place warning sign “Cleaning in progress” at the entrance or exit that 
should be visible to public. 
5.2 Move the fabric sofa to a convenient place where the task can perform 
smoothly. 
5.3 Vacuum the fabric sofa with the help of a dry vacuum machine – Refer 
to MS-CLE-HMC-012 Suction cleaning. 
5.4 Identify the minor or major stains over the fabric. 
5.5 Apply Ecolab Revitalize Miracle Spotter (Ready to use) into a sponge 
(soft side). 
5.6 Gently execute spotting on minor or major stains on the fabric. 
5.7 Wipe over the spotted area with MFC to clear the stains. 
5.8 Take diluted Ecolab Revitalize Carpet Upholstery Extraction Cleaner in a 
chemical container. 
5.9 Beat the chemical with a hand brush in a circular motion to create 
chemical foam for fabric shampooing. 
5.10 Take the chemical foam on a sponge and rub gently on the fabric in one 
direction in systematic parallel over lapping passes. 
5.11 Make sure the entire fabric surfaces covered with shampoo foam and carefully rubbed. 
5.12 Wait for contact time to react the chemical into the fabric (60 to 120 seconds) 
5.13 Remove the chemical foam with the help of an extractor machine or wet 
vacuum machine. 
5.14 Do not miss any fabric surface where the shampoo foam applied. 
5.15 Redo the application of extraction machine if the fabric looks still wet. 
5.16 Leave the sofa for drying in room temperature or with the help of blower machine (optional depends 
on the urgency).

4. Pre-Job Check 
4.1 Infection Control & HSEQ 
4.1.1 
Wash hands as per the approved method prior to commencing task. Check hands for any cuts 
and abrasions, if yes cover with waterproof dressing (Refer to HMC policies CL 7241 Hand 
Hygiene Policy). 
4.1.2 
Wear appropriate PPE as per guidelines (Refer to HMC policies CL 7242 Infection Control 
Practices) 
4.1.3 
To Identify the work area and place the barricades and warning signs as needed. 
4.1.4 
Warning sign to be placed on both the entrance/exit doors. 
4.1.5 
Ensure that the work area is free from any hindrance or obstacles that could affect the Cleaning 
/ Housekeeping operation. 
4.1.6 
Follow FMM heat stress policy. 
 
4.2 Tools & Equipment 
4.2.1 
Check & prepare all the equipment that may be required for the task. 
4.2.2 
Choose appropriate colour coded tools & equipment and ensure they are assembled safely. 
4.2.3 
Condition of tools – check if these are in clean, good working condition & safe to use. 
4.2.4 
Hose reels must be in functioning condition. 
4.2.5 
Always check the preparation date of the diluted cleaning chemical (Ecolab Oasis orange force 
137). Dilution ratio – 4/8 ml chemical into 1 Litre of water. 
4.2.6 
 The usage of the chemical solution must be within 24 hours of the dilution process 
4.2.7 
Ensure for electrical equipment PAT test is done. 
 
5. Job Steps 
5.1 Manual Floor Washing 
5.1.1 
Remove any trashes, dry leaf etc. from the floor with a dustpan and 
brush. 
5.1.2 
Identify stains on the floor and do necessary spotting with the help of 
hard brush. 
5.1.3 
Floors are checked for any adhesive soiling, use abrasive pad and Ecolab 
Oasis Orange Force 137. as required only. 
5.1.4 
Identify available water points nearby and connect hose pipe / collect 
water in a wringer bucket trolley from the nearest housekeeping room. 
5.1.5 
Due to unavailability of water outlets in Administration area and 
Restaurant area courtyards, hose pipes shall be connected from the roof top water outlets and 
the hose pipe shall be dragged down from the roof top till courtyard floor. 
5.1.6 
Other hose reel shall be used inside the courtyard only when required. 
5.1.7 
One janitor shall be stand on the roof top to adjust the hose pipe direction further down to 
courtyard. 
5.1.8 
Spread water into a manageable area and use hard brush to remove the sand and dust.

## Page 212

METHOD STATEMENT 
MS-CLE-HMC-33 
Courtyard Floor Washing 
DATE 
5/14/2024 
REVISION 
V2.0 
 
Template version: 2.0 dated 01-Feb-2018 
 
Page 4 of 6 
 
5.1.9 
Chemical shall be used only when heavy/oil stain found on the floor (spotting with additional 
precautions) applicable for the courtyards designed with land scape; this is to protect the plants 
from any type of chemical reaction. 
5.1.10 Push the soiled water into the nearest drainage from the floor surface 
with a floor squeegee. 
5.1.11 Rinse the floor again with water to remove complete dirt from the 
floor. 
5.1.12 Remove the excess water from the floor again with a floor squeegee. 
5.1.13 Clean the drain plate with water and wipe with a clean green coloured 
MFC. 
5.1.14 Make sure the floor is free from water before removing the caution sign and barricade. 
5.1.15 Hose pipe must be pulled back slowly and carefully to avoid any scratches or damages on the 
courtyard side glasses. 
 
5.2 For Operating Pressure wash machine. 
5.2.1 
Read your operator's manual and follow the manufacturer's instructions for safe use, 
maintenance, and storage. Assemble equipment and check for safety, paying particular 
attention to any plugs, cables, hoses and connections. 
5.2.2 
Steps for using power wash machine. 
5.2.3 
Install the nozzle and the spray lance. 
5.2.4 
Connect high pressure hose: to the hand spray gun using safety pin / lock /to the Pressure 
machine. 
5.2.5 
Tighten the screw connection between the hose and the machine. 
5.2.6 
Connect the supply water hose (minimum length 7.5 m, minimum diameter 3/4“) to the 
facilities water valve for continuous supply of water (for e.g., a tap- Cold Water). Open the 
water supply. 
5.2.7 
Switch on the Machine with the key, push down accelerator & Boost the engine. 
5.2.8 
Position the spray tip at least 2 feet (0.6 m) from the surface to start. 
5.2.9 
Hold the nozzle at a 45-degree angle to the surface. This will help direct 
loosened debris away from you. Pull the trigger to begin spraying the 
water. 
5.2.10 Gradually move the tip closer. You risk damaging the surface if you 
hold the tip closer than 12 inches (0.3 m) 
5.2.11 Move the spray in a side-to-side motion, without spraying too long in 
any one spot. 
5.2.12 Turn the spray pipe towards the left or right to select the desired spray 
type. 
5.2.13 High pressure circular spray (0°) for especially stubborn dirt. 
5.2.14 High pressure flat spray (25°) for large dirt areas 
5.2.15 Flat low-pressure spray (CHEM) for operating using detergents or cleaning at low pressure. 
5.2.16 To keep control of the water stream, hang on to the spray wand with both hands. 
5.2.17 Avoid contact with the high-pressure stream of water. Keep your hands, feet and body out of 
the way. Maintain good footing.

## Page 213

METHOD STATEMENT 
MS-CLE-HMC-33 
Courtyard Floor Washing 
DATE 
5/14/2024 
REVISION 
V2.0 
 
Template version: 2.0 dated 01-Feb-2018 
 
Page 5 of 6 
 
5.2.18 Do not reach so far that you lose your balance. Never point the sprayer at another person, 
whether the pressure washer is operating or not. It could discharge unexpectedly. 
5.2.19 Do not use a pressure washer in an area where the water could contact electrical equipment. 
5.2.20 On Job completion Switch off the engine to "STOP" till the engine comes to a halt. 
5.2.21 Shut off water supply. 
5.2.22 Remove the plug and recoil / rewind all cables and hoses. 
5.2.23 Clean the equipment and check for safety of Equipment. 
5.2.24 Remove excess water from floor surfaces and leave as dry as is possible using a long-handled 
squeegee, directing water to the nearest drains. 
5.2.25 Return the equipment and materials to the Designated storage area. 
 
6. Health and safety – Points to remember 
6.1 
Always place the precautionary / warning signage near doors. 
6.2 
Wear the right non-slip safety footwear for the job. 
6.3 
Stay in a comfortable working position, do not over-stretch to reach the task area rather than using 
long handled tools. 
6.4 
Use appropriate PPEs (refer to HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
6.5 
To complete the job safely, follow the associated risk assessment. 
6.6 
Perform a complete workplace 360 degrees check for any unusual conditions or new hazards and 
report them to your team leader right away. 
6.7 
Only use appropriate tools and equipment for the task. 
6.8 
Comply with all HMC regulations for safety, hygiene, and infection control. 
6.9 
Never leave any tool or material in the area unattended. 
6.10 As much as possible, keep the pathways clear of obstructions. 
 
7. Quality Verification & Post Job Check 
7.1 
After completion of the task, the appearance of area shall be clean and satisfactory. 
7.2 
In case any furniture/equipment etc. are moved from the cleaning area, it shall be restored back 
to its original state (reinstate the area). 
7.3 
Ensure that the tools, signs, and barricading has been removed from the task location and safely 
take them back to the HK room/store and keep the area clear for user. 
7.4 
Double Check if there are no tools, materials left in the area. 
7.5 
Notify your team leader that the work is complete, and area is free of materials. 
7.6 
Remove your PPE (HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
7.7 
Wash your hands thoroughly after the task is completed (HMC Policies CL 7241 Hand Hygiene.) 
7.8 
Did you notice any additional hazards during the work task that wasn’t mentioned in the Method 
Statement? (if yes, inform your team leader) 
7.9 
Are there any faults with your tools and PPE that you must report? 
7.10 Report all Damages, Incidents & accidents / Health events to your Team Leader. 
7.11 Waste must be properly disposed as per SA-1054-Hazardous materials and waste management 
program.

4. Pre-Job Check 
4.1 INFECTION CONTROL & HSEQ 
4.1.1 
Wash hands as per the approved method prior to commencing task. Check hands for any cuts 
and abrasions, if yes cover with waterproof dressing (Refer to HMC policies CL 7241 Hand 
Hygiene Policy). 
4.1.2 
Wear appropriate PPE as per guidelines (Refer to HMC policies CL 7242 Infection Control 
Practices) 
4.1.3 
Ensure you have been trained in RA, MS, SDS, COSHH and chemical handling. 
4.1.4 
Place the Work signage caution board. 
4.1.5 
Ensure that the work area is free from any hindrance or obstacles that could affect the Cleaning 
/ Housekeeping operation. 
4.1.6 
Before commencing the task, any damage to surfaces or any incident should be reported to the 
team leader. 
4.1.7 
Follow FMM heat stress policy. 
 
4.2 TOOLS & EQUIPMENT 
4.2.2 
Check & prepare all the equipment that may be required for the task. 
4.2.3 
Choose appropriate colour coded tools & equipment and ensure they are assembled safely. 
4.2.4 
Condition of tools – check if these are in clean, good working condition & safe to use. 
4.2.5 
Floors are checked for any adhesive soiling, use a non-abrasive pad as required only. 
4.2.6 
Ensure the chemicals spray bottles are properly labelled with details such as dilution /expiry 
date & time. 
4.2.7 
Always check the preparation date of the diluted cleaning chemicals (Oasis Pro 14, Oasis Pro 
41). Dilution ratio – 4/8 ml chemical into 1 Litre of water (If in case chemical not available from 
the Ecolab chemical dispenser machine). 
4.2.8 
 The usage of the chemical solution must be within 24 hours of the dilution process. 
4.2.9 
Ensure for electrical equipment PAT test is done.

5. Job Steps 
5.1 EXTERNAL AREA 
5.1.1 
Notify security team with security cabins deep cleaning schedule. 
Inspect security cabins to find out any damages or defects before 
commencing the task if any, report it to the maintenance team and 
record it for work completion report. (Cracks, breakage, permanent 
stains, colour fade etc. 
5.1.2 
Barricade the area according to the traffic movements and place 
caution boards “Cleaning in progress”. 
5.1.3 
Identify hard stains on the external wall. 
5.1.4 
Use abrasive pad and Pro 14 chemical to remove stains if any. 
(Dilution ratio 1:4/8 depends up on the stain) 
5.1.5 
Start washing external walls with pressure wash machine. (Top to 
bottom) Refer the below steps for using jet pressure washer machine. 
5.1.6 
Push the dirty/excess water from the floor to the nearest drain hole. 
5.1.7 
Clean exterior window glasses and frames (refer MS-CLE-HMC-005 - 
Glass Cleaning). 
5.2 INTERNAL AREA 
5.2.1 
Empty the trash bin if it is filled with ¾ of trashes, replace with new 
bin liner. 
5.2.2 
Clean ceiling panels and high walls with a long stick (refer MS-CLE-HMC-008- Dusting & Damp 
Wiping) 
5.2.3 
Remove wall marks (internal) with abrasive pad and Pro 14 chemical to remove stains if any. 
(Dilution ratio 1:4/8 depends on the stain) 
5.2.4 
Clean all the furniture and other assets with MFC and Oasis Pro 14 
chemical (refer MS-CLE-HMC-008- Dusting & Damp Wiping). 
5.2.5 
Clean interior window glasses, mirror, and frames (refer MS-CLE-
HMC-005 - Glass Cleaning). 
5.2.6 
Scrub the floor with machine (refer MS-CLE-HMC-004 - Floor 
scrubbing). 
 
5.3 FOR WASHROOM CLEANING PROCEDURE (If available in the security cabin) 
5.3.1 
Empty the trash bin if it is filled with ¾ of trashes, replace with new bin liner. 
5.3.2 
Clean ceiling panels and high walls with a long stick (refer MS-CLE-HMC-008- Dusting & Damp 
Wiping) 
5.3.3 
Remove wall marks (internal) with abrasive pad and Pro 14 chemical to remove stains if any. 
(Dilution ratio 1:4/8 depends on the stain). 
5.3.4 
Clean interior window glasses, mirror, and frames (refer MS-CLE-HMC-005 - Glass Cleaning). 
5.3.5 
Clean the WC (refer MS-CLE-HMC-010 - WC Cleaning). 
5.3.6 
Clean the Wash basin (refer MS-CLE-HMC-009 - Wash Basin Cleaning). 
5.3.7 
Scrub the floor (refer MS-CLE-HMC-004 - Floor scrubbing).

Condition of tools – check if these are in clean, good working condition & safe to use. 
4.2.4 
Hose reels must be in functioning condition. 
4.2.5 
Always check the preparation date of the diluted cleaning chemical (Ecolab Oasis orange 
force 137). Dilution ratio – 4/8 ml chemical into 1 Litre of water. 
4.2.6 
 The usage of the chemical solution must be within 24 hours of the dilution process. 
4.2.7 
Ensure for electrical equipment PAT test is done.

MS-CLE-HMC-40 
Pergola Cleaning 
DATE 
5/12/2024

5. Job Steps 
5.1 Pergolas Cleaning 
5.1.1 
Choose the area of pergola to be cleaned. 
5.1.2 
Barricade the area of the works to avoid any patient 
/visitor accessing the area. 
5.1.3 
Place the podium ladder on the selected area 
according to the height of pergola. 
5.1.4 
Reach to the desired area to be cleaned through the 
podium ladder. 
5.1.5 
Cleaning of the pergolas shall be start from top and 
end at bottom. 
5.1.6 
Remove any visible dirt/dust from the pergola 
frames with a MFC and Abrasive pad if required. 
(Refer to MS-CLE-HMC-08 Dusting & Damp 
Wiping) 
5.1.7 
The cleaning of pergola frames shall be performed 
from the highest level of the pergolas frame using 
the power washer machine. 
5.1.8 
Power washer gun nozzle to be adjusted to low mode to avoid wasting of water aligned with 
environmental safety. 
5.1.9 
Use a dry applicator attached with an extension pole can push the excess water from the 
pergola frame surface if any. 
5.1.10 Wipe the pergola frames and lights with a dry MFC. 
5.1.11 Change the MFC whenever required. 
5.1.12 Move to another area after the completion of above steps. 
 
5.2 Pergolas Floor Washing. 
5.2.1 
Refer to MS-CLE-HMC-02 External Area Floor Washing and Scrubbing. 
5.2.2 
If the pergola is situated close to plant, avoid using chemicals. 
 
5.3 For Operating Pressure Wash Machine. 
5.3.1 
Read your operator's manual and follow the manufacturer's instructions for safe use, 
maintenance, and storage. Assemble equipment and check for safety, paying particular 
attention to any plugs, cables, hoses, and connections. 
5.3.2 
Steps for using power wash machine. 
5.3.3 
Install the nozzle and the spray lance. 
5.3.4 
Connect high pressure hose: to the hand spray gun using safety pin / lock /to the Pressure 
machine. 
5.3.5 
Tighten the screw connection between the hose and the machine. 
5.3.6 
Connect the supply water hose (minimum length 7.5 m, minimum diameter 3/4“) to the 
facilities water valve for continuous supply of water (for e.g., a tap- Cold Water). Open the 
water supply. 
5.3.7 
Switch on the Machine by turn on mode switch.

## Page 225

METHOD STATEMENT 
MS-CLE-HMC-40 
Pergola Cleaning 
DATE 
5/12/2024 
REVISION 
V2.0 
 
Template version: 2.0 dated 01-Feb-2018 
 
Page 5 of 6 
 
5.3.8 
Position the spray tip at least 2 feet (0.6 m) from the surface to start. 
5.3.9 
Hold the nozzle at a 45-degree angle to the surface. This will help direct loosened debris 
away from you. Pull the trigger to begin spraying the water. 
5.3.10 Gradually move the tip closer. You risk damaging the surface if you hold the tip closer than 
12 inches (0.3 m) 
5.3.11 Move the spray in a side-to-side motion, without spraying too long in any one spot. 
5.3.12 Turn the spray pipe towards the left or right to select the desired spray type. 
5.3.13 High pressure circular spray (0°) for especially stubborn dirt. 
5.3.14 High pressure flat spray (25°) for large dirt areas 
5.3.15 Flat low-pressure spray (CHEM) for operating using detergents or cleaning at low pressure. 
5.3.16 To keep control of the water stream, hang on to the spray wand with both hands. 
5.3.17 Avoid contact with the high-pressure stream of water. Keep your hands, feet, and body out 
of the way. Maintain good footing. 
5.3.18 Do not reach so far that you lose your balance. Never point the sprayer at another person, 
whether the pressure washer is operating or not. It could discharge unexpectedly. 
5.3.19 Do not use a pressure washer in an area where the water could contact electrical 
equipment. 
5.3.20 On Job completion Switch off the engine to "STOP" till the engine comes to a halt. 
5.3.21 Shut off water supply. 
5.3.22 Remove the plug and recoil / rewind all cables and hoses. 
5.3.23 Clean the equipment and check for safety of Equipment. 
5.3.24 Remove excess water from floor surfaces and leave as dry as is possible using a long-
handled squeegee, directing water to the nearest drains. 
5.3.25 Return the equipment and materials to the Designated storage area. 
 
 
6. Health and safety – Points to remember 
6.1 Always place the precautionary / warning signage near doors. 
6.2 Stay in a comfortable working position, do not over-stretch to reach the task area rather than using 
long handled tools. 
6.3 Use appropriate PPEs (refer to HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
6.4 To complete the job safely, follow the associated risk assessment. 
6.5 Perform a complete workplace 360 degrees check for any unusual conditions or new hazards and 
report them to your team leader right away. 
6.6 Only use appropriate tools and equipment for the task. 
6.7 Comply with all HMC regulations for safety, hygiene, and infection control. 
6.8 Never leave any tool or material in the area unattended. 
6.9 As much as possible, keep the pathways clear of obstructions. 
 
7. Quality Verification & Post Job Check 
7.1 After completion of the task, the appearance of pergolas frames, lights, floor etc. shall be clean and 
satisfactory. 
7.2 In case any waste bins are moved from the cleaning area, it shall be restored back to its original state 
(reinstate the area). 
7.3 Excess water must be removed from the floor and the floor must be free of water.

5. Job Steps 
 
5.1 Identify the work area risk access and place the safety sign (caution board) 
nearby. 
 
5.2 When the smoking stand is not blocked, place a damp green duster 
over the ashtray and take it to the janitorial room. 
 
5.3 Spray water on the ashtray in the smoking area to ensure that the cigarette 
buds are already put off before throwing them in the garbage bag. 
 
5.4 Clear the cigarette buds and other litters from the ashtray 
using disposable gloves and dustpan. 
 
5.5 Clean and wash the ashtray using Pro 14 chemical using the green sponge. 
 
5.6 Clean the smoking stand using a green damp duster. 
 
5.7 The ashtray's sand needs to be replaced each month. 
 
6. Health and safety – Points to remember 
6.1 Always place the precautionary / warning signage near doors.
