4. Pre-Job Check 
4.1 Infection Control & HSEQ 
4.1.1 
Wash hands as per the approved method prior to commencing task. Check hands for any cuts 
and abrasions, if yes cover with waterproof dressing (Refer to HMC policies CL 7241 Hand 
Hygiene Policy). 
4.1.2 
Wear appropriate PPE as per guidelines (Refer to HMC policies CL 7242 Infection Control 
Practices) 
4.1.3 
Ensure you have been trained in RA, MS, SDS, COSHH and chemical handling. 
4.1.4 
Place the work signage caution board. 
4.1.5 
Ensure that the work area is free from any hindrance or obstacles that could affect the 
Cleaning / Housekeeping operation. 
4.1.6 
Before commencing the task, any damage to surfaces or any incident should be reported to 
the team leader. 
4.1.7 
Ensure adequate ventilation as appropriate. 
4.1.8 
Ensure electrical wall socket switches are in off position while plugging the vacuum machine 
or PC power cables. 
4.1.9 
Make sure vacuum/blower machine switch is in “off” mode before plugging on electrical wall 
socket. 
4.1.10 Check that the unit switches on and off correctly.

4.1.11 Carry out a safety check of each part of the vacuum machine as appropriate. 
 
4.2 TOOLS & EQUIPMENT 
4.2.1 
Check & prepare all the equipment that may be required for the task. 
4.2.2 
Choose appropriate colour coded tools & equipment and ensure they are assembled safely. 
4.2.3 
Condition of tools – check if these are in clean, good working condition & safe to use. 
4.2.4 
Ensure the chemicals spray bottles are properly labelled with details such as dilution /expiry 
date & time. 
4.2.5 
Always check the preparation date of the diluted cleaning chemicals (Oasis Pro 14, Ecolab 
PRO 41). The usage of the chemical solution must be within 24 hours of the dilution process. 
4.2.6 
Chemical dilution ratio 4/8 ml chemical into 1 Litre of water, if in case chemical not available 
in the chemical dispenser machine. 
 
5. Job Steps 
5.1 Points to remember: 
5.1.1 
Contact Hospital Security Department for required approvals before scheduling and 
commencing the task. 
5.1.2 
Proceed to control room, knock on the door, and enter only after permission is granted. 
5.1.3 
Log in/out to control room is mandatory. 
5.1.4 
 Note down if any issues are reported by control room team regarding any damages on the PC 
or its accessories before commencing the task. 
5.1.5 
Visually check for any other damages which is not reported by control room team, report if 
any. 
5.1.6 
Switching off the PC’s and unplugging before commencing the cleaning task is recommended 
in order to avoid any electrical hazards. 
5.1.7 
Ask for assistance from control room team to switch off/on PC to avoid any interruptions of 
control room system functions/operations. 
5.1.8 
Wait till the PC completely shuts down, please remember ONLY THE CONTROL ROOM 
OPERATOR SHOULD SHUT DOWN THE PC. 
5.1.9 
After the PC is shut down completely, switch of the power supply, first from the extension 
cord/power strip (if any in use) followed by electrical wall socket. 
5.1.10 Disconnect the power cables from the extension cord/power strip. 
5.1.11 Take a picture of the cable connections before unplugging them from the slots this will help 
you reconnect the cables in the same order after the work is completed. 
 
5.2 Steps:

## Page 240

5.2.1 
CPU 
5.2.1.1 Move the CPU to space where it is more convenient to perform the task outside the control 
room. 
5.2.1.2 Wear the Antistatic wrist band and connect to the base of the CPU while vacuuming and 
cleaning the inside of the CPU. 
5.2.1.3 Keep the CPU on a table in a vertical standing position. 
5.2.1.4 Carefully remove the CPU side cover. 
5.2.1.5 Clean the internal parts of the CPU with an antistatic vacuum/blower machine, use nozzle to 
reach between the parts and congested spaces inside the CPU. 
5.2.1.6 Use blower mode of the machine in lower pressure to blow the dust from the CPU internal 
parts. 
5.2.1.7 Make sure you don’t touch any internal / delegate part of the PC with the blower / vacuum 
nozzle. 
5.2.1.8 Close the CPU side cover carefully after removing the dust from internal parts of the CPU. 
5.2.1.9 Use acrylic hand duster to remove the dust from CPU external body including cables. 
5.2.1.10 Perform the dusting of exterior surfaces and cables with a PINK coloured dry MFC cloth. 
5.2.1.11 Damp wipe exterior surfaces with chemical charged (PRO 14 cleaner) GREEN coloured MFC 
cloth. Use Pro 14 chemical spray bottle to charge the MFC cloth. 
5.2.1.12 Wait till the chemical completely dried. 
5.2.1.13 Return the CPU back to its place and plug in all the cables back to the CPU as it was prior to 
disconnection (refer to the picture taken earlier to ensure the cables are connected to the 
right socket). 
5.2.1.14 Double check if all the cables are connected properly. 
5.2.1.15 Connect the power cord to the extension cable/power strip. 
 
5.2.2 
Monitor 
5.2.2.1 Use acrylic hand duster/3-inch hand brush to remove the dust from monitor external 
surfaces. Pay attention while cleaning cables, edges, corners, front and back side of monitor 
etc. 
5.2.2.2 Use antistatic vacuum cleaner to remove any accumulated dust remains in the external 
surfaces, if required. 
5.2.2.3 Perform the dusting of exterior surfaces and cables with a PINK coloured dry MFC cloth.

5.2.2.4 Damp wipe exterior surfaces with chemical charged (Oasis PRO 14) GREEN coloured MFC 
cloth except cables and monitor glass. 
5.2.2.5 Damp wipe monitor glass with another chemical charged GREEN MFC (Oasis PRO 41 – Glass 
Cleaner) (Use 2 different GREEN MFC cloths for different chemical application) 
5.2.2.6 Make sure not to wipe any electrical socket or cables with a damp/wet cloth. 
5.2.2.7 Wipe the exterior surfaces of monitor glass with a PINK coloured dry MFC cloth. 
5.2.2.8 Position the monitor and to the right place as it was. 
5.2.2.9 Connect the plug of the extension cord/power strip to the wall socket. 
5.2.2.10 Switch on the wall socket followed by switching on the extension cord/power strip. 
5.2.2.11 Switch on the PC by pressing power button on the CPU. 
5.2.2.12 Always ask assistance from control room team to ensure all wires are connected in the right 
place and confirm if the CPU is functional. 
5.2.2.13 Notify the control room Operator about the completion of the job and to verify the 
connections and PC restart. 
 
5.2.3 
VMS (Video management system), Keyboard, and other accessories 
5.2.3.1 Switch off VMS one at a time, ask assistance from control room team to switch off/unplug. 
5.2.3.2 Use acrylic hand duster/3-inch hand brush to remove the dust from external surfaces of 
VMS/keyboard/other accessories. 
5.2.3.3 Use antistatic vacuum cleaner to remove any accumulated dust remains in the external 
surfaces especially on keyboard (Optional for VMS and other accessories). 
5.2.3.4 Perform the dusting of exterior surfaces and cables with a PINK coloured dry MFC cloth. 
5.2.3.5 Damp wipe exterior surfaces and cables with chemical charged (Pro14) GREEN coloured MFC 
cloth, wait till chemical completely dried out. Use Pro 14 chemical spray bottle to charge the 
MFC cloth. 
5.2.3.6 Switch on the VMS and the other accessories, ask assistance from control room team. 
 
6. Health and Safety – Points to Remember 
6.1 Always place the precautionary/warning signage 
6.2 Stay in a comfortable working position, do not over-stretch to reach the task area rather than using 
long handled tools. 
6.3 Do not clean any internal part or any electrical cable with damp cloth or chemical. 
6.4 Handle all chemicals safely according to COSHH / SDS. 
6.5 Use appropriate PPEs (refer to HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
6.6 Avoid physical contact with chemical (specially with eyes).

5.1.4 
After obtaining all relevant approvals, review the CCTV camera cleaning schedule and identify 
camera locations. 
5.1.5 
Ensure a well-stocked cleaning toolbox is set up with adequate supplies required for the task. 
5.1.6 
Prior to commencement of the task, setup workstation to execute the task.

5.2 Steps for cleaning CCTV cameras: - 
5.2.1 
Clean exterior surface of cameras and brackets by using 3-Inch soft brush. 
5.2.2 
Use acrylic dusters to remove dust from the dome. 
5.2.3 
Perform the dusting of exterior surfaces with a PINK coloured dry MFC cloth. 
5.2.4 
Damp wipe exterior surfaces with chemical charged (Oasis Pro 41 Glass cleaner) GREEN 
coloured MFC cloth. 
5.2.5 
Wipe the exterior surfaces with a PINK coloured dry MFC cloth. 
5.2.6 
After completion of the cleaning process, visually check the camera for any dust or moisture 
inside the dome and notify control room immediately. 
5.2.7 
For outdoor cameras, ensure all poles for the cameras are cleaned as well. 
5.2.8 
Ensure Identification labels are not damaged during the cleaning process; labels to be covered 
with transparent tape if required. 
5.2.9 
After completion of scheduled camera cleaning routine for the day, submit the completion 
report to control room for approval and signature. 
5.3 Steps for cleaning camera pole: - 
5.3.1 
Place the reachable podium ladder in front of the pole. 
5.3.2 
Start the cleaning task from top to bottom cleaning process. 
5.3.3 
Perform dusting of exterior surfaces with a PINK coloured dry MFC cloth. 
5.3.4 
Check for visible stains and clean it with an abrasive pad and Pro 14 chemical. 
5.3.5 
Notify if any permeant stain/mark/rust on the pole. 
5.3.6 
Damp wipe exterior surfaces with chemical charged (Oasis Pro 14 All purpose) GREEN coloured 
MFC cloth. 
5.3.7 
Wipe the exterior surfaces with a PINK coloured dry MFC cloth. 
5.3.8 
Make sure all the screws, nuts and bolts on the pole are sprayed with WD-40 chemical to avoid 
rust.

4. Pre-Job Check 
4.1 Infection Control & HSEQ 
4.1.1 Wash hands as per the approved method prior to commencing task. Check hands for any cuts and 
abrasions, if yes cover with waterproof dressing (Refer to HMC policies CL 7241 Hand Hygiene 
Policy). 
4.1.2 Wear appropriate PPE as per guidelines (Refer to HMC policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices) 
4.1.3 Only trained personnel should perform the work with required competent supervision. 
4.1.4 Staff to have COSHH Briefing prior to use the chemical. 
4.1.5 Available required permit to work (PTW) /Notification of Project/Work / INOT (Approved documents 
must be ready 48 hours prior to work commencement) 
4.1.6 Facility focal point must be informed on work commencement. 
4.1.7 Tool box talk and onsite briefing must be conducted before work commencement. 
4.1.8 To Identify the work area and place the barricades and warning signs as needed. 
4.1.9 Warning sign to be placed on both the entrance/exit doors. 
4.1.10 Ensure that the work area is free from any hindrance or obstacles that could affect the Housekeeping 
operation. 
4.1.11 Follow FMM heat stress policy.

4.1.12 Follow FMM Work at height procedure. 
4.1.13 Follow working under severe weather condition procedure. 
4.1.14 Ensure staff have work at height training. 
4.1.15 Ensure to check the wind speed time to time using anemometer. 
 
4.2 Tools & Equipment 
4.2.1 Check & prepare all the equipment that may be required for the task. 
4.2.2 Ensure the Operators have equipment familiarization. 
4.2.3 Operator to perform pre use inspection using GL-HSEQ-11-T15 BMU Pre-Use Check List inspection 
4.2.4 Choose appropriate colour coded tools & equipment and ensure they are assembled safely. 
4.2.5 Condition of tools – check if these are in clean, good working condition & safe to use. 
4.2.6 Ensure the BMU unit having 3rd party certificate. 
4.2.7 Ensure the Operators have valid 3rd party operator’s certification. 
4.2.8 Ensure Operators must know about Operations manual of the BMU. 
4.2.9 Ensure that all control measures are available, and the operations will comply with Method 
Statement. 
4.2.10 Never use tools that contain strong solvents and abrasives. 
4.2.11 While cleaning with different material always consider cleaning methods for the most sensitive 
materials. 
4.2.12 Water deposition on the cradle body, inside the platform and in different locations must be 
prevented since will cause corrosion. 
4.2.13 Ensure BMU unit/Davit arm functioning properly and report if any damages noticed before start 
using. 
 
5. Job Steps 
5.1 BMU Davit Arm installation. 
5.1.1 Identify the task location where the davit arm to be installed. 
5.1.2 Move the davit arm by carrying with support of 3 to 4 employees. 
5.1.3 Place the davit arm over the arm legs which is fixed on the roof top. 
5.1.4 Locate hole of davit arm and the leg on same sequence then insert the lock into the connecting hole. 
5.1.5 Ensure the locks are inserted properly and functioning. 
5.1.6 Follow the above steps while changing the area to another. 
5.2 Work Sequence of BMU Unit 
5.2.1 Set-up working area, set-up tools and equipment required for the task. 
5.2.2 Place the cradle on the selected work area where the task to be performed. 
5.2.3 Set-up area and BMU system and place corresponding signage and applicable barricade. 
5.2.4 Plug the BMU electrical supply wire on the electrical panel. 
5.2.5 Put on the power key and switch on - Initiate the green light inside the cradle. 
5.2.6 Ensure that the BMU system is safe and ready to operate with required updated documents. 
5.2.7 Set-up required equipment and tools to be used in the actual working area to be review and tested 
and testing the emergency stop system prior start the work. 
5.2.8 Extend the cradle cable till the davit arm located on the roof top. 
5.2.9 Cable extension shall be done by attaching the cable on a rope from the cradle.

5.2.10 Another staff shall be pulling the other end of the rope attached with cradle cable from the roof top. 
5.2.11 Once the cable reached on the roof top davit arm location, engage the connections of davit arm and 
the cable then lock it carefully. 
5.2.12 Adjust the tightness cradle hoist left and right. 
5.2.13 After the tightness adjusted, set the control hoist on cradle booth. 
5.2.14 Lift the cradle by operating the control panel up to 7 feet height. 
5.2.15 Once confirmed that the wire rope installations are correct and safe to use, the cradle operator on 
the ground will energize the cradle. 
5.2.16 When the green light inside the cradle switched on, that indicate the BMU system is ready and safe 
to use. 
5.2.17 Using manual switches to turn left, right, up and down and set at the middle to operate bottle hoist. 
5.2.18 Once the desired area reached, the cleaning task shall be performed from top to bottom. 
5.2.19 Continuous closed supervision is required while performing the task. 
5.2.20 After task completion, put down the BMU to the safe levelled area by controlling down button and 
control the low bypass button before touching the floor to engage the low bypass safety feature. 
5.2.21 Clean the cradle and disinfect the major touch point by using Oasis Pro14 all-purpose cleaner and 
disinfectant. 
5.2.22 In case of any emergency, use emergency stop/release button to bring down the cradle. 
 
6. Health and safety – Points to remember 
6.1 Always place the precautionary / warning signage nearby doors. 
6.2 Pay full attention while access OR Egress to / From the BMU cradle, extra caution for the edges to be 
considered. 
6.3 During severe weather conditions OR sandstorm, no work is allowed on the Cradle. 
6.4 Do not handle the electrical power supply plug with wet hands! Pull the electrical plug by holding onto the 
plug and not by the cord. 
6.5 Ensure all switches are in the OFF position, before plugging the plug into the mains socket. 
6.6 Wear the right non-slip safety footwear for the job. 
6.7 Stay in a comfortable working position, do not over-stretch to reach the task area rather than using long 
handled tools. 
6.8 Use appropriate PPEs (refer to HMC Policies CL 7242 Infection Control Practices). 
6.9 To complete the job safely, follow the associated risk assessment. 
6.10 Perform a complete workplace 360 degrees check for any unusual conditions or new hazards and report 
them to your team leader right away. 
6.11 Only use appropriate tools and equipment for the task. 
6.12 Comply with all HMC regulations for safety, hygiene, and infection control. 
6.13 Never leave any tool or material in the area unattended. 
6.14 As much as possible, keep the pathways clear of obstructions. 
 
7. Quality Verification & Post Job Check 
7.1 After completion of the task, the appearance of area shall be clean and satisfactory. 
7.2 In case any furniture/waste bin etc. are moved from the cleaning area, it shall be restored back to its 
original state (reinstate the area). 
7.3 Ensure that the tools, signs, and barricading has been removed from the task location and safely take 
them back to the HK room/store and keep the area clear for user. 
7.4 Ensure the davit arms back to the position, detach and store in a safe place if required.
